## 3. Definitions for reading the audit independently

All spaces are finite-dimensional real Euclidean spaces; all norms and
inner products below are Euclidean. Log denotes the natural logarithm.
Fix lambda=1+sqrt(2). For each enclosing integer m>=6 put

    beta=1-1/m, c=lambda(1+1/m), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    b_j=1+a*c^j, n_m=2^m-1, S_j=sum H_j.

All H_j in this paragraph depend on this SAME m. The original benchmark
has C_0=B_0=1 and, for n>=1, takes the maximum over every integer split
0<=r<n of

    C_n=max Omega(C_r,C_(n-r-1)),
    B_n=max G(C_r,B_(n-r-1)),
    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2, U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

The accepted constant is Z_1=lim_m B_(2^m-1)/lambda^m. The sole accepted
predecessor gives its existence, positivity, finiteness, and lower bound
Z_1>=lambda^(-r)B_(2^r-1) for every integer r>=0.

For the geometric construction define

    K=14/5, E=1/100, kappa=K/m, epsilon=E/m,
    theta_sp=arctan(1/epsilon),
    chi=sqrt(1+epsilon^2) exp(kappa*theta_sp), d=1-kappa^2,
    T=(exp(d*theta_sp/kappa)-1)/d,
    H=chi[1+(1+kappa)T]-1, A=epsilon+(H+1)/epsilon,
    R_cut=max(A/epsilon,1+2/epsilon^2),
    J=max(1,ceil(log(R_cut/a)/log c)).

The unsubscripted H in these costs is not a word. If J<m, select the
first occurrences of levels J,...,m-1 and let k=m-J. The selected values
are b^(i)=b_(J+i), indexed0<=i<k. Their incoming ordinary sums are
s_0=S_J and s_i=S_(J+i-1) for 1<=i<k; the terminal ordinary sum is
s_k=S_(m-1). If J>=m, selection is empty and s_0=S_m.

The two finite scalar quantities audited are

    P_m=1/[2(1+s_k)] product_(i<k)
                  [b^(i)/(chi*s_i+2A+epsilon*b^(i))]^2,
    P5_m=1/(1+2s_k) product_(i<k)
                  [(b^(i)-1)/(chi*s_i+max(epsilon*b^(i),A))]^2.

The first is the exact payoff of the constructed objective about0. The
second is only a scalar comparison device in this audit. In the empty
case the actual first expression is 1/[2(1+S_m)]. The worst-case quantity
W_n(h) is the supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over
all such smooth convex functions, finite dimensions, minimizers and
initial points distinct from those minimizers, under
x_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L)grad f(x_t).

## 4. Physical word, cutoff, and finite cases

Put zeta=a/(c-2) and v_mass=c(c-2). The polynomial identity

    v_mass=1+(4+2sqrt(2))/m+lambda^2/m^2

shows v_mass>1>beta>0, so0<zeta=beta/v_mass<1. Starting at S_0=0,
the source's expression for S_j satisfies

    2[zeta*c^j+(1-zeta)*2^j-1]+1+a*c^j
       =zeta*c^(j+1)+(1-zeta)*2^(j+1)-1,

because 2zeta+a=zeta*c. This also checks that no child parameter was
silently replaced by the child's own depth.

The recursive concatenation gives the first occurrence of level j at
zero-based index2^j-1. Between the first occurrences of levels j and
j+1 lies H_j, not H_(j-1). After the first top-level checkpoint lies
the full H_(m-1). For J<m the ordinary lengths are2^J-1, followed by
2^j-1 for J<=j<=m-2, followed by2^(m-1)-1. Adding the m-J selected
steps gives 2^m-1. This verifies all physical indices and the exceptional
first denominator used later.

Since c>1, the exact ceiling yields a*c^J>=R_cut whenever selection is
nonempty. Every later selected level is larger. Thus

    b_j=1+a*c^j>=2+2/epsilon^2,
    epsilon*b_j>=epsilon+A>A.

The first inequality is precisely the doubled source-format threshold,
so local b_j/2 satisfies1+epsilon^(-2). The second makes the maximum
in P5_m equal epsilon*b_j at EVERY selected checkpoint. The large
exponential activation cost has not been discarded.

At k=1, J=m-1: the incoming and terminal ordinary words are both
H_(m-1). There are no bulk factors. At k=0 the candidate explicitly uses
one dimension and a half-Huber function. Both cases therefore have an
actual function, not just a formal empty-product convention. The proof
of the limiting theorem uses only the eventually nonempty case.

## 5. Backward arc: direct check of every projection case

For 0<=u<=theta_sp write

    e(u)=(cos u,-sin u), t(u)=(-sin u,-cos u),
    R(u)=chi exp(-kappa*u), v(u)=R(u)e(u)=(p(u),-q(u)),
    n(u)=e(u)+kappa*t(u).

Then v(0)=(chi,0), v(theta_sp)=(epsilon,-1). Since theta_sp<pi/2,
p>0, q>=0, and q/p=tan u<=1/epsilon. Differentiating p gives
p'=-R(kappa*cos u+sin u)<0, hence epsilon<=p<=chi. The vertical
component q can exceed1; this does not affect the proof.

For any two allowed angles u,w, Delta=w-u lies in(-pi/2,pi/2). The
ratio of <n(u),v(w)> to R(u) is

    exp(-kappa*Delta)(cos Delta+kappa*sin Delta).

Its derivative is-(1+kappa^2)exp(-kappa*Delta)sin Delta. It increases
up to Delta=0 and decreases after 0, where it equals1. Since the origin
has inner product0<R(u), n(u) supports the convex hull K_arc of 0 and
the ENTIRE arc. At u=0, the vector(0,1) supports this hull as well.
This tests remote arc pairs, rather than only neighboring directions.

For any finite list of nonnegative local steps alpha_1,...,alpha_N,
the terminal backward state is r_N=v_N=(epsilon,-1). Write each state
r_j=(U_j,-Y_j). Invariants are U_j>=epsilon, Y_j>=1 and
Y_j/U_j<=1/epsilon. For an uncapped state at angle u_j,

    r_j=R(u_j)[(1+a_j)e(u_j)+kappa*a_j*t(u_j)], a_j>=0.

For step alpha=alpha_j, the cap condition is
Y_j<=kappa[U_j+(alpha-1)chi]. With mu=U_j+(alpha-1)chi, this implies
mu>0 and

    r_(j-1)-v(0)=mu*n(0)+(0,kappa*mu-Y_j),
    r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha*v(0).

Both coefficients describe supporting normals, so v(0) is a
projection. The resulting inequality Y_(j-1)<=kappa(U_(j-1)-chi)
makes the cap condition true at every earlier nonnegative step. Its
vertical coordinate stays fixed.

If the cap condition fails, the state cannot already be capped. Put

    F_j(u)=<r_j,e(u)-t(u)/kappa>/R(u).

One has F_j(u_j)=1,
F_j(0)=(U_j-Y_j/kappa)/chi<1-alpha, and

    F_j'(u)=(kappa+kappa^(-1))(U_j*cos u+Y_j*sin u)/R(u)>0.

Thus F_j(u)=1-alpha has exactly one solution in(0,u_j], including
u=u_j if alpha=0. To check the normal coefficient, the representation
of r_j gives, for 0<=u<=u_j,

    <r_j,t(u)>=R(u_j)[(1+a_j)sin(u_j-u)
                              +kappa*a_j*cos(u_j-u)]>=0.

At the root, F_j(u)=1-alpha is equivalent to

    r_j+(alpha-1)v(u)=(<r_j,t(u)>/kappa)n(u).

This is a nonnegative supporting normal. Adding v(u) establishes the
required representation and projection at the previous state. Adding
alpha(p,-q) preserves all three invariants because p>=epsilon*q.
The elementary expansion of squared distances confirms that a
supporting-normal inequality implies Euclidean projection. No inverse
GD map was assumed globally invertible.

For two successive uncapped angles with Delta=u_new-u_old>=0, their
t(u_old) components give

    a_old=B_Delta+A_Delta*a_new,
    A_Delta=exp(-kappa*Delta)(cos Delta+sin Delta/kappa),
    B_Delta=exp(-kappa*Delta)sin Delta/kappa.

Both are nonnegative. With d=1-kappa^2>0, differentiating A_Delta
gives A_Delta'-(d/kappa)A_Delta<=0, so A_Delta<=exp(d*Delta/kappa).
Also B_Delta<=Delta/kappa and 1+dB_Delta<=exp(d*Delta/kappa). Hence

    1+d*a_old<=exp(d*Delta/kappa)(1+d*a_new).

Telescoping from a_N=0 over a total angle no greater than theta_sp
yields a_j<=T. Consequently the positive vertical coordinate is at
most chi[1+(1+kappa)T]=1+H. Caps do not increase it. If
P=sum alpha_j*p(u_(j-1)) and Q=sum alpha_j*q(u_(j-1)), then

    r_0=(epsilon+P,-1-Q),
    0<=P<=chi*sum alpha_j, 0<=Q<=H, P>=epsilon*Q.

These estimates are uniform in the number and size of the local steps.
This is the point that lets the construction include every ordinary
step in each long physical gap without a cumulative geometric error.

## 6. Finite local function, static global assembly, and exact payoff

For a physical block of sum s followed by checkpoint b, use the above
backward construction with its actual entries divided by2, so its local
sum is s/2. Put b_loc=b/2 and

    sigma=1/(chi*s/2+epsilon*b/2+A),
    z_circ=(1,0)-sigma(epsilon+P,-1-Q),
    U=1-sigma(P+epsilon*b_loc), Y=sigma*Q,
    V_fin={0,v(0),v_0,...,v_N},
    phi(z)=sigma*max_(v in V_fin)<v,z-z_circ>,
    f_loc(x)=min_z[phi(z)+||x-z||^2/2].

Here sigma>0, U>=0, Y>=0. The allowance gives

    sigma[P+epsilon+(1+Q)/epsilon]<=1.

The minimum in f_loc exists uniquely: phi is finite, convex and
nonnegative, while the quadratic makes the objective coercive and
strictly convex. If z is the minimizer and g=x-z, the subgradient
condition is g in partial phi(z). It is sufficient by the completed
square. For necessity, test the minimum at z+t(w-z), use convexity,
divide by t and let t decrease to 0. Thus no unstated envelope theorem
is required.

For two such pairs, adding subgradient inequalities gives
||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>. The trial point z at x+w gives the upper
value bound f_loc(x+w)<=f_loc(x)+<g,w>+||w||^2/2. Subgradient support
at z evaluated at the actual new minimizer z' gives the lower bound

    f_loc(x+w)>=f_loc(x)+<g,w>+||w-(z'-z)||^2/2.

These inequalities establish differentiability, global supporting
affines and convexity; the first displayed inequality gives the
1-Lipschitz gradient. They also prove f_loc>=0 and
{f_loc=0}={phi=0}.

For x_j=z_circ+sigma*r_j and z_j=x_j-sigma*v_j, the projection inequality
makes v_j maximize <v,z_j-z_circ> over V_fin. Therefore sigma*v_j is
an active subgradient of phi and grad f_loc(x_j)=sigma*v_j. Since
V_fin is finite, the objective is finite even though its construction
was described with a continuous arc. The half-step ordinary run starts
at(1,0), ends before its checkpoint at(U+sigma*epsilon*b_loc,Y),
and the local checkpoint ends at(U,Y+sigma*b_loc).

For each nonzero v=(p,-q) in V_fin,

    <v,-z_circ>=-p+sigma[p(epsilon+P)+q(1+Q)]<=0,
    <v,(U,Y)-z_circ>=sigma[(1-b_loc)epsilon*p+q]<=0.

The first uses q/p<=1/epsilon and the allowance; the second uses
b_loc>=1+epsilon^(-2). Decreasing the first coordinate or increasing
the second preserves the needed signs. This proves exactly the two
physical zero regions used by the assembly. It never asserts a
nonnegative horizontal proximal coordinate.

Apply this construction in advance to each incoming block i and use
subscripts i for its local quantities. Define

    D_0=1, ell_0=0,
    G_i=sigma_i*b^(i)/2=b^(i)/(chi*s_i+2A+epsilon*b^(i)),
    D_(i+1)=D_i*G_i, ell_(i+1)=D_i*Y_i,
    delta_tail=D_k/(1+s_k).

All D_i are positive and finite for any fixed m; all ell_i are
nonnegative. Let Hub_delta(z) equal0 for z<=0, z^2/2 for 0<=z<=delta,
and delta*z-delta^2/2 for z>=delta. Its derivative is the clipped
coordinate max(0,min(z,delta)). With coordinates numbered0,...,k,
the actual normalized objective is

    F_m(x)=1/2 sum_(i<k) D_i^2
              f_i((x_i-ell_i)/D_i,x_(i+1)/D_i)
           +1/2 Hub_(delta_tail)(x_k-ell_k).

This is one static function on R^(k+1), fixed by the word before GD.
At 0 each local first input is nonpositive and second input is 0, so
F_m(0)=0. Every term is nonnegative and convex. Scaling a local
1-smooth function by D_i^2 and its coordinates by1/D_i preserves its
smoothness on that coordinate pair. The complete quadratic upper
remainder is

    1/4[sum_(i<k)(w_i^2+w_(i+1)^2)+w_k^2]<=||w||^2/2.

Interior coordinates and the terminal coordinate are counted twice;
the first coordinate is counted once. This proves the precise global
constant1, not a constant growing with dimension. Applying the upper
bound to F_m(x)-<grad F_m(y),x>, whose minimum is attained at y,
with increment-(grad F_m(x)-grad F_m(y)), gives

    F_m(x)-F_m(y)-<grad F_m(y),x-y>
                 >=||grad F_m(x)-grad F_m(y)||^2/2.

Adding its reverse proves the1-Lipschitz gradient everywhere. A smaller
possible optimal Lipschitz constant is immaterial: the objective meets
the required bound1 without any subsequent step rescaling.

At the beginning of block i, the actual claimed coordinates are
x_j=ell_j+D_jU_j for j<i, x_i=ell_i+D_i, and x_j=0 for j>i. This
holds initially at e_0. Throughout the current ordinary local path,
x_i>=ell_i+D_iU_i>=ell_i and 0<=x_(i+1)<=D_iY_i=ell_(i+1).
For every earlier component, its first input is its frozen U_j and
its second is at least Y_j. For the immediately preceding component
this last assertion follows from x_i>=ell_i; for older components
it follows from their already frozen coordinates. For every future
component the first input is nonpositive and the second is 0. Thus all
these components vanish; their gradients vanish because they are
differentiable nonnegative functions at a minimum. The terminal Huber
term likewise vanishes until its activation.

Only the current local component contributes a gradient. Its gradient
on physical coordinates is D_i/2 times the local one. Therefore a
physical step h_t becomes precisely h_t/2 after dividing coordinates
by D_i. This establishes every ordinary update in its original order.
The physical checkpoint leaves

    x_i=ell_i+D_iU_i,
    x_(i+1)=D_i(Y_i+sigma_i*b^(i)/2)=ell_(i+1)+D_(i+1).

The induction advances without changing any old function. During the
next block x_(i+1) remains at least ell_(i+1), so the old function
stays zero. This explicitly rules out a hidden fresh-child reset.

In the terminal block let u be the sum of executed physical steps.
Its shifted coordinate equals

    D_k-delta_tail*u/2
       >=D_k-delta_tail*s_k/2=delta_tail(1+s_k/2)>=delta_tail.

Thus every terminal query and the terminal point stay in the affine
Huber region. Earlier components remain zero. Substitution gives

    F_m(x_(n_m))
       =1/2[delta_tail*D_k-delta_tail^2*s_k/2-delta_tail^2/2]
       =D_k^2/[4(1+s_k)].

Hence2F_m(x_(n_m))=P_m exactly. For f_m=L*F_m and steps h_t/L, all
iterates are identical and the L in the payoff cancels. At k=0 the
same proof is the single function Hub_(1/(1+S_m))/2, starting at 1.

## 7. Every directed row and both radius conventions

For actual query/terminal/minimum data let g_a=grad F_m(x_a) and
f_a=F_m(x_a). The global inequality just established, with x=x_b and
y=x_a, is equivalent to

    Q_ab=f_a-f_b+<g_a,x_b-x_a>+||g_a-g_b||^2/2<=0.

It applies to every ordered pair, including either orientation of
terminal and optimum rows. For L*F_m the last coefficient becomes
1/(2L). The GD equations were proved physically, and the Gram matrix
is PSD because these are actual vectors. No missing interpolation
cycle must be supplied by a separate finite interpolation theorem.

The specified minimizer is 0 and ||e_0||=1. To audit the different
closest-radius claim, write z_circ,i=(c_(i,1),c_(i,2)). Every slope
in its finite set has the decomposition

    (p,-q)=[(p-epsilon*q)/chi]*(chi,0)+q*(epsilon,-1),

with both coefficients nonnegative. Both endpoint slopes are present.
Consequently its local zero set is exactly

    z_1<=c_(i,1),
    epsilon(z_1-c_(i,1))-(z_2-c_(i,2))<=0.

Because the entire objective is a sum of nonnegative terms, its
minimizer set is the closed convex polyhedron

    C_min={x: x_i<=zeta_i,
              epsilon*x_i-x_(i+1)<=epsilon*zeta_i-D_i*c_(i,2)
                    for 0<=i<k; x_k<=ell_k},
    zeta_i=ell_i+D_i*c_(i,1).

The scale factors and the ell_i shifts in this formula are correct:
multiply each local inequality by D_i before substituting the inputs.
This set contains 0 by the established zero-region argument. At i=0,

    zeta_0=1-sigma_0(epsilon+P_0)<1.

Thus e_0 lies outside C_min. In fact the first halfspace already gives
dist(e_0,C_min)>=sigma_0(epsilon+P_0)>0. Nonemptiness and closedness
give attainment of the closest distance; convexity gives a unique
projection. Since0 is available, the exact closest distance rho_m
satisfies0<rho_m<=1. At k=0 the minimum set is the nonpositive half-line
and rho_m=1.

It follows that the payoff about0 is exactly P_m, while the payoff
about the closest minimizer is exactly P_m/rho_m^2>=P_m. This audit
does NOT infer that rho_m=1 or that the latter payoff has limit L_joint.
The asserted exact limit concerns the specified-minimizer value only.
For the lower bound on W_n either minimizer choice is permissible.

## 8. Exact activation phase and simultaneous product errors

The exact identity behind the activation estimate is

    A=epsilon+sqrt(1+epsilon^2)/[epsilon(1-kappa)]
                    [exp(theta_sp/kappa)-kappa*exp(kappa*theta_sp)].

It follows by simplifying1+(1+kappa)T and multiplying by chi. For the
fixed positive K,E, the identity theta_sp=pi/2-arctan(epsilon) and
the elementary integral remainder for arctan give
theta_sp=pi/2-E/m+O(m^-3). The bracket is exp(theta_sp/kappa) times
1 minus an exponentially small nonnegative quantity. The separate
additive epsilon is exponentially negligible relative to the positive
main term. The elementary logarithm integral bounds then give

    log A=pi*m/(2K)+log m-log E-E/K+O(1/m),
    log(A/epsilon)=pi*m/(2K)+2log m-2log E-E/K+O(1/m).

These estimates have constants independent of m. The other alternative
1+2/epsilon^2 in R_cut grows only polynomially, so eventually R_cut
equals A/epsilon exactly. Since log c=log lambda+1/m+O(m^-2) and
log a=-log lambda+O(1/m), division and the ceiling yield

    J=theta*m+(2/log lambda)log m+O(1),
    theta=pi/(2K log lambda)=5pi/(28log lambda).

The rounding contributes at most1 and is retained in O(1). The
explicit rational bound theta<220/343<1 is checked below. Thus the
selection is eventually nonempty and k/m tends1-theta. This correctly
pays both the linear number of discarded levels and their logarithmic
correction; it does not replace the original finite cutoff.

Define exact errors, with zeta=a/(c-2),

    delta_l=[(1-zeta)2^l-1]/(zeta*c^l),
    xi_j=1/(a*c^j),
    D_first=chi(1+delta_J)+epsilon(c-2)(1+xi_J),
    D_bulk,j=chi(1+delta_(j-1))+epsilon*v_mass(1+xi_j),
    d_first=chi+epsilon(c-2), d_bulk=chi+epsilon*v_mass.

The D_first and D_bulk,j here are scalar denominators; they are not
the physical scales D_i. Directly normalizing the exact product gives

    P5_m=(c-2)^2*v_mass^[2(k-1)]
           /[(1+2S_(m-1))*D_first^2
                         *product_(j=J+1)^(m-1)D_bulk,j^2].

The first factor differs because its incoming sum is S_J. There is
no extra c in its numerator. This is essential for the factor 1/lambda
in the final constant.

As m grows, c stays bounded away from 2, a and zeta stay bounded away
from 0, and 1-zeta=O(1/m). The following are bounds for the ENTIRE sets
of errors, not estimates at a fixed level:

    sum_(l>=J)|delta_l|
       <=[(1-zeta)/zeta]*(2/c)^J/(1-2/c)
                         +c^(-J)/[zeta(1-1/c)],
    sum_(j>=J)xi_j=c^(-J)/[a(1-1/c)].

Since J grows linearly with m, these bounds decay exponentially.
Dividing D_first and D_bulk,j by their positive leading terms gives
relative errors bounded respectively by

    |delta_J|+epsilon(c-2)xi_J,
    |delta_(j-1)|+epsilon*v_mass*xi_j.

Counting delta_J twice covers both the first factor and the first
bulk factor. The sum tends0, so each error eventually has magnitude
at most1/2. Integrating1/(1+t) gives |log(1+t)|<=2|t| on that interval.
This proves a simultaneous o(1) logarithmic error over the whole
growing product. The infinite sums are only scalar upper estimates;
the constructed function remains finite-dimensional.

To compare the actual P_m with this reference, put s^(J)=S_J and
s^(j)=S_(j-1) for j>J, and define

    V_j=2A/(chi*s^(j)+epsilon*b_j),
    tau_tail=(1+2S_(m-1))/[2(1+S_(m-1))].

The exact ratio is

    P_m/P5_m=tau_tail product_(j=J)^(m-1)
                                      [(1+xi_j)/(1+V_j)]^2.

For all large m, S_l>=zeta*c^l/2 for every l>=J. The first term has
incoming level J; every later term has incoming level j-1. Summing
their geometric upper estimates gives

    sum_j V_j
       <=4A*c^(-J)/(zeta*chi) [1+1/(1-1/c)]
       <=4epsilon(c-2)/chi [1+1/(1-1/c)].

The last inequality is precisely A*c^(-J)<=epsilon*a from the
unchanged cutoff. The separate1 pays the exceptional first record;
the geometric series pays the repeated H_J and every subsequent gap.
Although A grows exponentially, its total relative product penalty is
only O(epsilon)=O(1/m). An incorrect pointwise argument could have
lost a constant here; the displayed sum avoids that error.

Using0<=log(1+t)<=t for t>=0 and
-log tau_tail<=1/(1+2S_(m-1)), one obtains

    |log(P_m/P5_m)|
       <=1/(1+2S_(m-1))
         +2c^(-J)/[a(1-1/c)]
         +8epsilon(c-2)/chi [1+1/(1-1/c)] ->0.

Thus the additive2A, the replacement b-1 by b, and the exact terminal
factor have all been paid simultaneously. The finite products need
not satisfy P_m>=P5_m, and the source makes no such claim.

## 9. Exact limiting constant and optional interface losses

Let gamma=4+2sqrt(2), q_rc=2gamma=8+4sqrt(2). The elementary logarithm
expansions give, with uniform remainders depending only on m,

    log v_mass=gamma/m+O(m^-2),
    log chi=pi*K/(2m)+O(m^-2),
    log d_bulk=(pi*K/2+E)/m+O(m^-2).

There are at most m factors, so their combined second-order remainder
is O(1/m). The simultaneous denominator control gives

    P5_m=(c-2)^2/[(1+2S_(m-1))*d_first^2]
                       *[v_mass/d_bulk]^[2(k-1)]*exp(o(1)).

Here d_first tends1, c-2 tends1/lambda, and zeta tends1. Dividing the
exact S_(m-1) by zeta*c^(m-1) and using its already bounded error gives

    lambda^m/(1+2S_(m-1)) ->lambda*exp(-1)/2.

This uses (lambda/c)^m=(1+1/m)^(-m)->exp(-1), while the remaining
factor c/zeta tends lambda. Meanwhile

    2(k-1)log(v_mass/d_bulk)
           ->(q_rc-piK-2E)(1-theta).

The logarithmic delay in J contributes O(log m/m), not a new limiting
factor. The exact cancellation a/zeta=c-2 explains why beta is not
separately multiplied or approximated k times. Multiplying by the
accepted convergence B_(n_m)/lambda^m->Z_1 and using P_m/P5_m->1 gives

    lim_m B_(n_m)*P_m
       =Z_1/(2lambda)
           exp[-1+(8+4sqrt(2)-14pi/5-1/50)
                            (1-5pi/(28log lambda))]
       =L_joint.

Only existence of the accepted ray limit is needed; no new benchmark
rate is asserted. The geometric and scalar errors before benchmark
convergence are O(log m/m) plus exponentially decreasing terms.

The source's optional primary-interface bookkeeping also checks out.
For k>=1 let hatD_i=D_i for i<k and hatD_k=sqrt(tau_tail)*D_k. Then
2F_m(x_n)=hatD_k^2/(1+2S_(m-1)). Set eta_i=1+V_(J+i) for i<k-1 and
eta_(k-1)=(1+V_(m-1))/sqrt(tau_tail). The actual transfer equality
then implies the claimed lower inequality with numerator b^(i)-1,
denominator chi*s_i+max(epsilon*b^(i),A), and loss eta_i. All eta_i
are at least1, and

    0<=sum_i log eta_i
           <=sum_j V_j-(1/2)log tau_tail ->0.

At k=1 the one transfer is exactly the final exceptional eta case;
hatD_0 remains1. No physical norm, function, or trajectory was changed.
The radius multiplier for the actual specified minimizer is 1 and the
additional asymptotic transfer loss is 0. This optional interpretation
is not necessary for the direct proof of the limit, but does not hide
an extra cost.

## 10. Accepted benchmark data and every exact constant comparison

The full benchmark dependency is accepted fact 04219554e41d9750, whose
statement explicitly identifies C_n=1+S_circle(n), B_n=1+2S_bullet(n)
and U_n^ZJ=1/B_n. Its Section 7 proves the unconditional ray enclosure

    lambda^(-r)B_(2^r-1)<=Z_1
       <=lambda^(-r)(sqrt(B_(2^r))+3/100)^2.

This is the original ALL-split recurrence, not a balanced-only or
restricted-tree denominator. The later seed hypotheses in that fact
do not qualify the preceding ray theorem.

Section 8 fixes the complete finite table

`/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/audit_c78/input_interval_table.csv`

at SHA256
`2a7585bc0bade9f85461456c13d60bbe618f85c7cc816e1ddc5b4c1a1d611e68`.
That hash was independently matched by this audit. The actual header is

    n,P_lo_integer,P_hi_integer,B_lo_integer,B_hi_integer,
      argmax_lower_P,argmax_lower_B,possible_maximizers_P,possible_maximizers_B

The row for n=1023 is

    8131234882988797741402851581,
    8131234882988797741402857321,
    19205851554343006584447327614,
    19205851554343006584447339831,
    511,667,[511],[667].

Thus the candidate selected the correct B lower column and horizon,
not a primitive endpoint or upper endpoint. The accepted denominator
is D80=2^80=1208925819614629174706176, and the saved lower numerator
is L1023=19205851554343006584447327614.

The accepted proof describes and certifies all stored lower endpoints,
not only the two endpoints used for its later phase comparison. Its
lower check uses one actual admissible split with already enclosed
lower children; monotonicity and induction certify that the actual
all-split maximum is at least that lower value. Hence the row above is
within the certified table's precise scope. This audit read the proof
and row; it did NOT execute the defining program or independent checker.
Hash agreement identifies the fixed artifact; it is not being used as
a substitute for the accepted enclosure proof.

The remaining arithmetic was checked textually. A short explicit way
to verify the source's 15880 comparison from the actual integers is

    D80<120893*10^19,
    15880*120893=1919780840<1920000000,
    L1023>1920000000*10^19.

All inequalities follow directly from the displayed decimal integers
and the displayed small multiplication. Thus 15880*D80<L1023 and
B_1023>15880. No new benchmark value was computed.

The square-root comparisons are exact:

    (140/99)^2<2 because 19600<19602,
    2<(99/70)^2 because 9800<9801.

Repeated multiplication, or squaring lambda^5=41+29sqrt(2), gives
lambda^10=3363+2378sqrt(2). Therefore

    lambda^10<3363+2378*(99/70)=6726+6/35<6727.

The accepted lower ray endpoint at r=10 yields

Also sqrt(2)<10/7, by2<100/49, so lambda<17/7. Consequently

    Z_1/(2lambda)>(59/25)*(7/34)=413/850.

For pi, polynomial division gives the identity

    x^4(1-x)^4/(1+x^2)
       =x^6-4x^5+5x^4-4x^2+4-4/(1+x^2).

Integrating on[0,1] gives 22/7-pi. The original integrand is positive
on(0,1), so pi<22/7 strictly. No decimal approximation to pi is used.

Let t=(lambda-1)/(lambda+1)=sqrt(2)-1. It satisfies
1>t>41/99>0. Since log lambda=2*integral_0^t du/(1-u^2), keeping
the first two nonnegative terms gives

    log lambda>2(t+t^3/3)
       >2[(41/99)+(41/99)^3/3]
        =2548888/2910897>7/8.

The final comparison has the exact positive slack
8*2548888-7*2910897=14825. Thus

    1-theta>123/343.

The other exponent coefficient is positive, with the strict lower bound

       >8+560/99-44/5-1/50=23941/4950>0.

Because both factors are positive, multiplying these lower bounds
preserves the orientation. The exponent in L_joint is strictly larger

       =1246893/1697850>73/100.

The last comparison can be checked by the positive cross-multiplication

Its numerator is 6000000+4380000+1598700+389017. Combining with the

Indeed12367717*413=5107867121. On the common denominator 5100000000,

## 11. Surviving prescribed-family statement and eventual quantifier

The following is the exact scope that survives this audit. It remains
an UNVERIFIED analytical candidate until the verifier accepts it.

For the original words H_m defined in Section 3 of this report, there
exists a finite integer M such that, for every integer m>=M and every
L>0, the single function f_m=L*F_m defined above on R^(m-J+1) is convex,
differentiable and L-smooth, has f_m(0)=min f_m=0, and starts at e_0
with ||e_0||=1. Its GD trajectory uses every original physical step of
H_m, and every directed interpolation row, including optimum and
terminal rows, holds. The exact payoff about this specified minimizer
is

    2(f_m(x_(2^m-1))-f_m(0))/(L||e_0||^2)=P_m,
    lim_m B_(2^m-1)*P_m=L_joint
       =Z_1/(2lambda)
          exp[-1+(8+4sqrt(2)-14pi/5-1/50)
                           (1-5pi/(28log lambda))]
       >5107867121/5100000000>1001/1000.

Consequently, for EVERY integer m>=M,

    P_m>(1+1/1000)U_(2^m-1)^ZJ,
    W_(2^m-1)(H_m)>=P_m,
    liminf_m B_(2^m-1)*W_(2^m-1)(H_m)>=L_joint.

The exact closest-radius payoff for this same function is
P_m/rho_m^2>=P_m, where rho_m=dist(e_0,C_min) lies in(0,1]. No exact
limit for rho_m or for the closest-radius payoff is asserted.

For the eventual quantifier, set
eta_margin=(L_joint-1001/1000)/2>0. Convergence gives a finite depth
after which every m satisfies |B_(n_m)P_m-L_joint|<eta_margin. Hence
B_(n_m)P_m>(L_joint+1001/1000)/2>1001/1000 for every later m. Increase
that depth to ensure m>=6 and J<m. The resulting M is independent of L
because scaling by L leaves the trajectory and payoff unchanged.
There is no claimed numerical cutoff or dependence on a selected
subsequence of depths. Ordinary convergence, rather than monotonicity,
is sufficient.

This is an eventual hard-instance lower bound for a prescribed family.
It does not prove a bound for every nonnegative schedule. A high value
of W_n on one word family does not disprove a minimax claim about
inf_h W_n(h); it also does not prove the original uniform affirmative
exact-profile target. No checkpoint-pattern optimum is assumed.

## 12. Defects, repair recommendation, provenance, and closeout

**First mathematical defect:** none identified after the full joint
review. In particular, the potential accumulated-allowance failure,
exceptional-first-prefix factor, factor-of-two normalization, and
closest-radius mismatch are all explicitly resolved by arguments in
the frozen source itself.

**Suggested mathematical repair:** none required by this audit. The
separate file `repair_recommendation.md` records this result and the
scope restrictions; it is not an edited or replacement proof. No
component source or frozen joint source was changed.

Recall used TASK.md, latest master_guidance, relevant shared findings
and dead ends, and the accepted fact graph. In particular, recalled
dead end917a02ef7f3a294d only rejects the polynomial-allowance shortcut
for a specified earlier mechanism. It was not used as a premise; the
current source retains the full exponential A and explicitly pays it
in the cutoff and product bound. Earlier component audit 6a97b480bbea2211
supplied awareness, not truth.

Skills applied were query-memory, direct-proving, construct-counterexamples,
and search-math-results. The counterexample checks targeted actual
interfaces: uncapped zero steps, first cap, arbitrarily large local
steps, possible q>1 or negative horizontal normal, empty/one-record
selection, returning old coordinates, an unbounded minimum polyhedron,
and a growing number of denominator errors. No counterexample emerged;
the verdict relies on the detailed direct checks above, not on absence
of a counterexample.

Retrieval was conditioned on the mature joint-audit subproblem,
search_mode=repair, intent=background/construction. Matlas returned
small-step self-contraction (arXiv:2003.04201, Corollary 3.9),
pseudocontractive updates (arXiv:1405.7741, Proposition 3), and an
extragradient saddle result (arXiv:2102.07922, Lemma 6). Their step
restrictions or algorithms do not supply this arbitrary-large-step
construction. None was imported. The already known exact primary
paper by Yuhan Ye and Kaizhao Liu, *Silver Rate Is (Almost) Optimal
for Gradient Descent*, arXiv:2609.09152v2 (2026), was accessed at
https://arxiv.org/html/2609.09152v2; its normalization and local/global
assembly proof in Section 3.1 and AppendixA.2--A.3 were read from the
existing exact extracted text, copied into this audit's downloads
directory. The source's circular arc differs from the candidate's
logarithmic arc, so no geometric theorem was transferred as a black
box. The current candidate supplies the needed projection and allowance
proofs itself. The factor 1/2 and physical half-step convention match
the source proof, which also explains its deliberately weakened stated
payoff. No new external theorem is a proof premise in this audit.

This round used text/source reads, file writing/copying, shared-memory
tools, literature retrieval, and metadata/integrity hashing only. It
used ZERO mathematical programs, numerical evaluations, trajectory
replays, solvers, benchmark DP runs, formal gates, installations,
consults, or subagents. No accepted fact was produced or revoked.
An initially broad tool display was truncated; the complete candidate,
skills and precise accepted dependency were subsequently read directly
in bounded outputs. No correctness claim relies on the truncated
display. No tool failure was suppressed.

The assigned one-round audit ends with this report and shared evidence.
No formal acceptance, finalization, publication, shutdown, or resource
renewal is inferred. The original project target remains OPEN.

# A double-zero Newton chain with a tilted projection realization

Status: complete UNVERIFIED candidate for a precisely stated growing-span
class, plus an explicit open positive-height interface. No optimization,
installation or fact_submit. The only fact inputs are 1252b8823bb5a290
(all-pair interpolation and its explicit function construction) and
b98bb807d514e80d (the exact benchmark recursions). All new claims below are
proved within this joint draft. This is not the original all-schedule target.

## 1. Actual chronology and the surviving double-zero representation

Fix m>=128 and, throughout this horizon, keep

 lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,
 d_j=1+a c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j),
 n=2^m-1, q=n-1.

Let h_0,...,h_(q-1) be H_m with its last d_0 removed. Queries are0,...,q;
the final step b is any number in[0,2], including the original b=d_0. Write

 s_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, S=s_q, P_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t z),
 mu_j=1/d_j, p_ij=P_i(mu_j).

Also write S_j=sum H_j, so S_total=S_m and S=S_m-d_0. Useful elementary
bounds are12/5<c<5/2,3/8<a<1/2 and11/8<d_0<3/2. To check them, use
12/5<1+sqrt2<17/7 and m>=128: c<2193/896<5/2 and
a>(127/128)/(5/2)=127/320>3/8. The other bounds follow immediately.
The ratios d_j/d_(j+1) decrease with j because (1+u)/(1+cu) decreases in u;
their initial value is less than(3/2)/(2-1/128)=192/255<4/5.

Consider the real polynomial quotient

                 chi(z)=z^2 product_(j=0)^(m-1)(z-mu_j).       (1)

Its dimension is m+2, and it contains the double-zero direction which is NOT
annihilated by the schedule. Every real linear map from this quotient has
unique vector coefficients u,v,w_0,...,w_(m-1) such that

 T[p]=u p(0)+v p'(0)+sum_j d_j w_j p(mu_j).

Indeed the evaluation/derivative map into these m+2 coordinates is injective:
its kernel consists of polynomials divisible by chi; on degree<m+2 the
kernel is zero. Equality of dimensions makes it an isomorphism. The positive
factors d_j only rescale these coordinates.

Thus the actual common-quotient data are exactly

 R_i=sum_j p_ij w_j, Z_i=sum_j d_j p_ij w_j,
 x_i=u-s_i v+Z_i, g_i=v+R_i, ||x_0||=||u+Z_0||=1.     (2)

All vectors are real and their common Gram matrix may be ANY PSD matrix,
including arbitrary initial correlations and singular limits. No mode is
assumed individually legal and no metric is assumed diagonal.
The polynomial identity for P_(i+1) proves x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i.

The first occurrence of d_j has step index2^j-1. Hence

 p_ij>0 for0<=i<2^j, and p_ij=0 for i>=2^j.            (3)

Before that first occurrence all steps are strictly less than d_j, so all
factors are positive. At that occurrence a zero factor appears and remains.
In particular every R_i and Z_i vanishes for i>=2^(m-1). These queries form
the genuine constant-gradient suffix x_i=u-s_i v, g_i=v.

For arbitrary shared values f_i, the complete nonstar interpolation law is

 f_i-f_j+<v+R_i,(s_i-s_j)v+Z_j-Z_i>
                                  +||R_i-R_j||^2/2<=0.        (4)

The optimum constraints are

 ||v+R_i||^2/2<=f_i
       <=<v+R_i,u-s_i v+Z_i>-||v+R_i||^2/2.            (5)

Every ordered pair in (4) remains. These are exactly the accepted fact's
inequalities, so together with a real Gram realization they are sufficient
for ONE actual convex1-smooth interpolant, including every queried value and
the optimum. This is a restricted m+2-dimensional temporal system, not the
unrestricted n+1-node reparameterization of the original problem.

## 2. A definite function-realization proposal: tilted squared distance

For the chosen mechanism define

                 y_i=x_i-R_i=u-s_i v+sum_j(d_j-1)p_ij w_j.

Choose the shared values

                    f_i=||R_i||^2/2+<v,x_i>-||v||^2/2.       (6)

At the optimum set R_star=-v and y_star=v; formula(6) still gives f_star=0
at x_star=0. Substituting (6) into every original Q_ij gives the exact law

                   Q_ij=<R_i,y_j-y_i><=0              (7)

for ALL ordered pairs i,j in {star,0,...,q}. In particular the original
optimum constraints are retained, rather than imposed separately on modes.

When (7) holds, let C=conv{v,y_0,...,y_q}. For any query i and any c in C,
<R_i,c-y_i><=0. Since x_i=y_i+R_i,

 ||x_i-c||^2=||R_i||^2+||c-y_i||^2-2<R_i,c-y_i>
                                                >=||R_i||^2.

Thus the Euclidean projection of x_i onto C is y_i. The star condition
likewise says that the projection of0 onto C is v. Consequently the actual
function

             F_C(x)=dist(x,C)^2/2+<v,x>-||v||^2/2       (8)

interpolates ALL the prescribed jets and has minimum zero at zero.

For completeness, Euclidean projections onto a closed convex set obey
<P_Cx-P_Cz,x-z>>=||P_Cx-P_Cz||^2, obtained by adding the two projection
normal inequalities. Therefore I-P_C is1-Lipschitz. Comparing the minimum
squared-distance expressions at x and x+d gives differentiability of
dist(x,C)^2/2 with gradient x-P_Cx. This function is convex, since joint
convexity of ||x-c||^2 and convexity of C give the convexity inequality after
taking minima over c. Addition of the linear term preserves convexity and
smoothness. Its gradient at0 is-v+v=0, proving the minimum assertion.

The actual minimizer set is also explicit. The star inequalities give
<v,c>>=||v||^2 for every c in C. Completing the square gives

 F_C(x)=min_(c in C){||x-(c-v)||^2/2+<v,c>-||v||^2}.

Hence argmin F_C={c-v:c in C,<v,c>=||v||^2}. One may require this face to be
the singleton{v}, or require0 to be the closest point in this set to x_0,
when normalizing by the TRUE closest-minimizer distance. The upper result
below holds with any designated minimizer0 and ||x_0||=1, and hence also
with that stronger true-distance convention. The lower calibration has a
unique minimizer, so its denominator is exactly the true distance.

The same obstruction applies to ANY convex1-smooth interpolant whose queried
values obey (6), not only to the particular representative (8). Only the
necessary all-pair law (7) is used in its proof.

## 3. Full obstruction: every level direction j>=2 vanishes

For EVERY legal data set (2),(6),

                       w_j=0 for all2<=j<m.            (9)

No linear independence or sign assumption on the w_j is used. This is an
actual interpolation obstruction, not a negative direction of a formal dual.

Prove(9) in descending order, starting with j=m-1. Assume w_ell=0 for every
ell>j and set

                  i=2^(j-1), l=i+1, p=p_ij.

For j>=2 these are both actual queries before the first d_j step is executed.
Every lower mode has already vanished by (3), so

 R_i=p w_j, R_l=p' w_j,
 p>0, p'=p(1-d_0/d_j)>0, s_l=s_i+d_0.                (10)

The last equality uses h_i=d_0: index2^(j-1) is even, so the next ruler
entry is level zero. All higher mode vectors vanish by the induction.

The two ACTUAL ordered inequalities (7) for i,l give

 p<w_j,y_l-y_i><=0,   -p'<w_j,y_l-y_i><=0.

Since p,p'>0, their scalar common factor must be zero. Expanding the actual
positions y gives

 <w_j,v>=-(1-1/d_j)p||w_j||^2.                        (11)

At the last queried index q, all modes have vanished, so y_q=u-Sv. The
actual REMOTE inequality (7) from i to q is therefore

 0>=<w_j,y_q-y_i>
   =-(S-s_i)<w_j,v>-(d_j-1)p||w_j||^2
   =(d_j-1)p||w_j||^2[(S-s_i)/d_j-1],                (12)

where (11) was substituted and the positive p factor in R_i was divided out.
But S-s_i>d_j: the retained suffix after query i includes the later first
d_j step and also the positive step h_i=d_0. The first d_j occurs before q,
even for j=m-1. Thus every factor in the last expression except ||w_j||^2
is strictly positive. It follows that w_j=0, closing the induction.

The remote pair i,q is load-bearing. Keeping only the adjacent pair would
give (11), not the contradiction. The residual's nonzero negative correlation
with v is explicitly allowed until the remote queried inequality rules it out.

After (9), (3) implies

                         g_i=v for EVERY i>=2.        (13)

Although the proposed temporal basis grew with m, legality has collapsed its
active directions to the two lowest levels and the double-zero block.

## 4. Quantitative full-class objective and an actual nonquadratic calibration

Let

        T_mass=S-d_0-d_1=sum H_m-2d_0-d_1.

For every b in[0,2], every legal transcript in this class and EVERY convex
1-smooth interpolant of its queried jets satisfy

       2F(x_q-b g_q)<=1/[2T_mass+1+b(2-b)].            (14)

The first two actual steps are d_0 in(11/8,3/2) and d_1=2-1/m in(0,2).
Adding the two optimum interpolation inequalities gives <g_i,x_i>>=||g_i||^2.
Thus for h in[0,2],

 ||x_i-hg_i||^2<=||x_i||^2-h(2-h)||g_i||^2<=||x_i||^2.

It follows that ||x_2||<=1. By(13), x_q=x_2-T_mass v. The optimum inequality
at q and final-step smoothness then imply, with t=||v||,

 2F(x_q-bv)<=2<v,x_2>-[2T_mass+1+b(2-b)]||v||^2
            <=2t-[2T_mass+1+b(2-b)]t^2.

Completing the scalar square proves(14). This includes the original b=d_0
and does not constrain the gradient at the unqueried final point.

The class is nonempty, genuinely nonquadratic, and asymptotically attains the
Huber scale. Put S_total=sum H_m, p=1/(2S_total+1), x_0=1, u=1, v=p and
all w_j=0. Choose C=[p,1] in R. Formula(8) gives

                   F_C(x)=dist(x,[p,1])^2/2+px-p^2/2.

This single function is convex and1-smooth with UNIQUE minimizer0: the face
minimizing pc over C is the singleton{p}. Every actual iterate of H_m lies in
[p,1], since x_i=1-p sum_(t<i)h_t and x_n=(S_total+1)/(2S_total+1)>p.
All queried gradients are p, all values have the required form(6), the
initial distance is exactly1, and the normalized final objective is

                            2F_C(x_n)=1/(2S_total+1).  (15)

No quadratic with minimum at0 has the same nonzero gradient at two distinct
scalar queries. Thus the data are nonquadratic. This is a calibration of the
obstruction, not a new improved lower-bound mechanism. Combining(14),(15)
shows the exact supremum over this class is Huber-tight up to a factor
1+O(1/S_total) at the original final step.

## 5. Comparison with the EXACT B_n, with every new bound proved

Keep B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1 and U_n^ZJ=1/B_n exactly as defined in accepted
b98bb807d514e80d. For this class the exact ratio is bounded by

                     B_n/[2T_mass+1+b(2-b)].          (16)

Here is a self-contained sufficient upper envelope for this exact expression;
it is not a replacement of the target profile. Put rho=log_2 lambda>1 and
C_k=S_circle(k)+1. The accepted recursions are

 C_n=max_(k+l=n-1)Omega(C_k,C_l),
 Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
 B_n=max_(k+l=n-1)G(C_k,B_l),
 G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,
 C_0=B_0=1.

We first prove C_n<=(n+1)^rho. For t>0 define D(t)=(1+t)^rho-1 and
g(z)=log D(exp z). Direct differentiation, with t=exp z, gives

 g''(z)=rho t(1+t)^(rho-2)
           [(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/D(t)^2>0.

The bracket is positive because it is zero at t=0 and has positive derivative
rho[(1+t)^(rho-1)-1] for t>0. Hence g(z)+g(-z)>=2g(0), or

 D(t)D(1/t)>=(2^rho-1)^2=2.

For x,y>0 put u=x^(1/rho), w=y^(1/rho), Z=(u+w)^rho. This inequality gives
(Z-x)(Z-y)>=2xy, so Z^2-(x+y)Z-xy>=0. Also Z>=x+y, by the increasing
derivative of (u+w)^rho-u^rho in u. Thus Z lies above the positive quadratic
root Omega(x,y), proving Omega(u^rho,w^rho)<=(u+w)^rho. Zero arguments follow
by substitution. Induction in the C recurrence proves the claimed envelope.

Next sqrt(y^2+8xy)<=y+4x for x,y>=0 gives G(x,y)<=y+4x. Using
l^rho+(k+1)^rho<=(l+k+1)^rho for rho>=1, induction proves

                         B_n<=1+4n^rho.               (17)

All recursions and constants refer to the actual B_n; no unverified comparator
from another finding is being adopted as a lemma.

Counting the ruler levels gives

 1+S_j=A_mass c^j+(1-A_mass)2^j, 0<=j<=m,
 A_mass=a/(c-2) in(0,1), A_mass->1.

Indeed ac<1=lambda(lambda-2)<c(c-2), proving A_mass<1. Since
c^m/lambda^m=(1+1/m)^m->exp(1) and2/lambda<1,

             S_total/lambda^m->exp(1),
             T_mass/lambda^m->exp(1).                 (18)

At n=2^m-1, (17) gives B_n<=1+4lambda^m. Equations(16)--(18) therefore imply

 limsup_(m->infinity) B_n sup_class 2F(x_q-b g_q)
                               <=2/exp(1)<1,          (19)

uniformly for b in[0,2]. This completes a full obstruction at the EXACT
benchmark scale for the selected tilted-projection growing-span class.
It is not a theorem about all legal data in the double-zero quotient(1).

## 6. What survives: positive joint heights and their exact first charge

The collapse proof depends precisely on the value law(6). It does not extend
automatically to arbitrary jointly legal values, including positive offsets.
The surviving interface can be stated exactly without dropping any pairs.

For arbitrary legal values in (2), let

 C_min=f_q-<v,x_q>,
 delta_i=f_i-<v,x_i>-C_min-||R_i||^2/2,
 delta_star=-C_min-||v||^2/2.

The tilted function F(x)-<v,x> has gradient zero at every constant-gradient
suffix query and therefore has global minimum C_min there. Its smooth-convex
optimum inequality implies delta_i>=0 for every query, delta_star>=0, and
delta_i=0 on the constant-gradient suffix. At star set R_star=-v,y_star=v.
The ENTIRE original interpolation system becomes

              delta_i-delta_j+<R_i,y_j-y_i><=0         (20)

for all ordered pairs, together with the stated relation defining
delta_star. The scalar C_min, all these heights and all common vectors belong
to the SAME function. Independent mode-wise values cannot replace them.

Here is a quantitative necessary cost showing how a height can avoid the
zero-height collapse. Suppose some level j>=2 is isolated at the two queries
i=2^(j-1), l=i+1 as in (10), so higher mode vectors are zero. Write

 d=d_j, D=d-1, Delta=d_0, p=p_ij, p'=p(1-Delta/d),
 Vw=<v,w_j>, Bw=||w_j||^2, T_i=S-s_i>d,
 zeta=Vw+(D/d)p Bw.

Expanding (20) for both i,l gives EXACTLY

       Delta p' zeta<=delta_i-delta_l<=Delta p zeta.   (21)

Since p>p'>0, this forces zeta>=0. The actual remote pair i,q gives

              delta_i<=p[T_i zeta-Dp Bw(T_i/d-1)].     (22)

As delta_i>=0, (22) implies
zeta>=(D/d)p Bw(1-d/T_i). Combining with delta_l>=0 and(21) proves

 delta_i>=Delta p'p (D/d)(1-d/T_i)||w_j||^2.           (23)

Every displayed factor in this charge is strictly positive. At zero heights,
it forces w_j=0, agreeing with Section3. With arbitrary positive heights it
is a necessary cost, not an impossibility theorem: that cost could be paid
by the same global function's value budget. No bound on that simultaneous
budget, and no benchmark-scale construction paying it, has yet been proved.

The entire isolated right block gives a stronger, scale-dependent charge.
Let I=2^(j-1), J=2^j-1, assume all higher w_ell vanish, and write
p_t=P_t(mu_j), p_I=P_I(mu_j), p_J=P_J(mu_j), T_J=S-s_J. Every query from I through J has
residual p_t w_j, and all the intervening steps comprise the actual H_(j-1).
For each such step h_t, the same two-pair calculation gives

 delta_t-delta_(t+1)
    >=h_t p_(t+1) [Vw+(D/d)p_t Bw].

The remote inequality J,q and delta_J>=0 give Vw>=-D p_J Bw/T_J.
Since p_t>=p_J and T_J>d, summing yields

 delta_I >= (D/d)(1-d/T_J) Bw sum_(t=I)^(J-1)h_t p_t p_(t+1).

Every intervening h_t/d is at most4/5. Hence p_(t+1)>=p_t/5, while
p_t^2-p_(t+1)^2<=(2h_t/d)p_t^2. The last sum is at least
(d/10)(p_I^2-p_J^2). Therefore

 delta_I >= (d-1)(1-d/T_J)(p_I^2-p_J^2)||w_j||^2/10.  (24)

In particular there is an absolute explicit lower charge at the scale d_j:

                delta_(2^(j-1))>=exp(-28)d_j||w_j||^2/320.  (25)

Here are bounds establishing this constant without a computation. The word
identities in Section5 give S_j< c^j, while d_j>(3/8)c^j; thus S_j/d_j<8/3.
Every step before d_j is at most (4/5)d_j. Integrating the derivative of
log(1-t) gives log(1-t)>=-5t for0<=t<=4/5, so
p_J>=exp(-40/3)>exp(-14). Also
p_J/p_I=P_(H_(j-1))(mu_j)<=exp(-S_(j-1)/d_j)<=exp(-4/25),
because S_(j-1)>=d_(j-2) and d_j<c^2 d_(j-2)<(25/4)d_(j-2).
The elementary bound exp(8/25)>=1+8/25+(8/25)^2/2>4/3 proves
p_I^2-p_J^2>=p_J^2/4>=exp(-28)/4.

The retained suffix after the first d_j includes d_j and at least
S_j-d_0>d_(j-1)>(2/5)d_j of further mass, even if its last d_0 is omitted.
Thus T_J>(7/5)d_j and1-d_j/T_J>2/7>1/4. Finally d_j>2 for j>=2, so
d_j-1>d_j/2. Substituting these three bounds into(24) proves(25).
It charges the largest active isolated level to a positive value height.
It does not yet add charges over lower levels in the presence of higher
nonzero vectors, and it does not bound the initial common value budget.

For clarity, on the constant-gradient suffix all values are exactly
f_i=f_q+(S-s_i)||v||^2, because adding and reversing the interpolation
inequalities between two equal-gradient queries forces equality. For each old
anchor the two directional Q expressions are constant or affine in s_i.
Thus retaining BOTH endpoints of that suffix suffices for its comparisons
with any earlier anchor; every earlier/earlier pair is still required.
The same endpoint statement holds for the maximum of conjugate jet quadratics
in the explicit realization. It does not delete any remote earlier pair.

The original problem remains unresolved. The next concrete question in this
lane is whether nonzero joint heights can pay (21)--(25) through multiple
levels while preserving every old pair and ||u+sum d_jw_j||=1, and produce
the required terminal objective relative to the actual B_n. Another
zero-height or independent-value construction cannot answer that question.

The earlier no-linear-term squared-distance/cone exclusions do not cover
this proposal: the actual gradients here are v+R_i and the minimum belongs
to the tilted function (8). The proof instead uses its exact ordered
interpolation law. Matlas retrieval for collinear normals returned a
self-contracted-curve lemma and cone projection results; none is imported.
No additional external theorem is needed, since the branch reduced to the
already audited all-pair interface in fact1252 and the elementary projection
calculation explicitly proved above. The optional strict-cycle smoothing
source triage in the latest master_guidance was read but not used as a premise.

UNVERIFIED auxiliary result inside joint draft34bf5f19be16ad26; no fact_id and no fact_submit allocation. Define lambda=1+sqrt(2),rho=log_2(lambda), C_0=B_0=1, Omega(a,b)=(a+b+sqrt((a+b)^2+4ab))/2, G(a,b)=2a+b/2+sqrt(b^2+8ab)/2, C_n=max_(0<=k<n)Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)), B_n=max_(0<=k<n)G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)) for every integer n>=1, retaining every integer split. The sole accepted input for the ray corollary is04219554e41d9750: Z_1=lim_m lambda^(-m)B_(2^m-1) exists and is positive finite. The all-n power bounds below are rederived, not read from an unverified saved-endpoint transcription. This is scalar benchmark control only, not a GD certificate or target theorem.

## 9. An elementary all-split scalar envelope and absolute deficit

This section is part of the same JOINT UNVERIFIED argument. Its
scalar bound is proved independently of saved numerical endpoints.
Set rho=log_2(lambda), p=rho/2 and kappa=62/25. One has p>5/8,
because lambda^4=17+12sqrt(2)>32 (use sqrt(2)>5/4).

First the following elementary power inequality holds:

    (25/31)x^(2p)+(1-x)^p<=1,
                0<=x<=1, p>=5/8.                   (9.1)

Increasing p can only decrease the left side, so consider

    psi(x)=(25/31)x^(5/4)+(1-x)^(5/8).

Its endpoint values are 1 and 25/31. An interior critical point obeys

    x^(1/4)(1-x)^(3/8)=31/50,
    x^2(1-x)^3=(31/50)^8.                           (9.2)

The function x^2(1-x)^3 decreases for x>=2/5. For x>=5/8,

    x^2(1-x)^3<=675/32768<21/1000
        <(147/1000)^2<(31/50)^8.                    (9.3)

The fixed rational comparisons use 675000<688128,
21000<21609, and
(31/50)^4=923521/6250000>147/1000 since 923521>918750.
Thus every interior critical point lies below 5/8.

At a critical point, the derivative equation makes

    psi(x)=(1-x/2)/(1-x)^(3/8)=:g(x).

The logarithmic derivative of g has the sign of 5x-2:

    g'(x)/g(x)=(5x-2)/[8(1-x)(2-x)].

Consequently its maximum on [0,5/8] is at an endpoint. Now g(0)=1
and g(5/8)<1, the latter being equivalent to

    11^8=214358881<226492416=27*2^23.

The continuous function psi attains a maximum either at an endpoint
or at such an interior critical point. This proves (9.1).
All these are displayed symbolic rational inequalities, not results
of an executed calculation or a numerical evaluation.

We also give the primitive power inequality rather than assume a
new recurrence fact. For positive a,b let A=a^(1/rho), D=b^(1/rho),
T=(A+D)^rho. Put E(t)=(1+t)^rho-1. The function
z -> log E(exp z) is convex: its second derivative is

    rho*t*(1+t)^(rho-2)
       *[(1+t)^rho-1-rho*t]/E(t)^2 >=0,  t=exp z,

because the bracket vanishes at zero and has positive derivative
for t>0. Convexity at opposite arguments gives

    E(t)E(1/t)>=E(1)^2=(lambda-1)^2=2.

It follows that (T-a)(T-b)>=2ab, or
T^2-(a+b)T-ab>=0. Since T is positive, it lies above the positive
root Omega(a,b). Zero arguments follow by continuity. Induction over
ALL original primitive splits therefore proves

    C_n<=(n+1)^rho.                                 (9.4)

We claim the corresponding dominant bound

    B_n<=kappa(n+1)^rho,              n>=0.          (9.5)

The case n=0 is 1<=kappa. In an arbitrary split at n>=1 put
A=k+1, D=n-k and N=A+D=n+1. By induction and (9.4), the two
arguments of G are at most A^rho and kappa D^rho. The function G
is increasing in both nonnegative arguments, directly from its formula.
Its value G(a,b) is the square of the positive root v of
v^2-sqrt(b)v-2a=0. Therefore it is at most T=kappa N^rho if

    2A^rho+kappa D^(rho/2)N^(rho/2)<=kappa N^rho.

On dividing by kappa N^rho, this is exactly (9.1) with x=A/N
and p=rho/2, since 2/kappa=25/31. It holds for EVERY split, so
taking their original maximum proves (9.5), without balancing or
deleting any split. In particular, (1.1) gives

    Z_1<=62/25.                                    (9.6)
