# C87 complete joint proof: exact surviving interval and its independent rejection

UNVERIFIED joint analytical draft. All component drafts below are supplied as proof text, not accepted premises. The only accepted predecessors are facts04219554e41d9750 and1252b8823bb5a290. No new numerical/model evaluation, word proposal, or verifier call was performed. The original full seed/target remains open. C87 updates the earlier C86 status: the old combined necessary region is now shown nonempty by a whole exact interval, while every member is excluded by a new partial-tail witness. Historical status statements in the appended C86 draft describe its earlier stage and are superseded by C87.

# C87: an exact interval surviving all C82/C83 tests, independently audited

Status: complete joint DRAFT, not verifier-accepted. This proof uses the
shared max2 family as a proposed example, then supplies the needed reasoning
inside the joint proof. It strengthens the previous all-curvature interval
from 10^(-12) to 1/100 and uses no polynomial table or numerical evaluation.
No new schedule is proposed or tested by a discovery model. No fact_submit.

The complete C84--C86 proofs and max2's scalar-test calculations are included
as proof text in the joint artifact. Only facts04219554e41d9750 and
1252b8823bb5a290 are accepted predecessors. The full four-step seed question
and the original asymptotic question remain open.

## 1. Exact survivor interval and elementary margins

Use the already shared family, now on the WHOLE interval

    h(t)=(7/5,9/4,33/25+t,7/4), 0<=t<=1/100.

Its canonical shifts and capacity are

    a=2/5, b=5/4, c=8/25+t, d=3/4,
    C=193/25+t, D=1+h3=11/4, Htail=1+h2+h3=407/100+t.

Thus sum h=168/25+t and all steps lie in the compact domain. The four
base-cut inequalities have strictly positive slack throughout this interval.
In particular the second slack is

    (1+h0)*(1+h2+h3)-C*(h1-1)=59/500+(23/20)*t>0.

The first and last slacks are (3/5)*C-7/5>0 and C/4-7/4>0. At the
third cut, C*c<=25509/10000<64/25 while (1+h0+h1)*D=1023/80>12.
All adjacent triangles hold strictly by the estimates in the appended
max2 proof, in particular b*c<D. Also

    C*c<64/25, a*b=1/2, a*b*c*d=3c/8,
    C*a*b*c*d<24/25<1.                              (1)

These strict inequalities are symbolic bounds on the entire interval.

## 2. C82 and both complete middle-cut families

For the C81/C82 triangle parameters u,z, its admissibility forces u>=5/2.
Because 1-b^2=-9/16, its energy is at least

    q(u)=((7/5)*u-1)^2-(9/16)*(u+1)*((2/5)*u-1)
                           -C^2*(b*c/D)^2.

The leading coefficient is 347/200>0 and
q'(5/2)=(7/5)*(71/16)>0, so it is increasing for u>=5/2. Therefore

    E(u,z)>=q(5/2)=25/4-(5*C*c/11)^2
                         >25/4-25/16=75/16>0.       (2)

For the last comparison use 5*C*c/11<64/55<5/4. This covers every
u,z, not just the minimizing boundary, and proves that all C82 tests pass.

At unit curvature the C83 auxiliary terminal gradients at the two middle
cuts are, respectively,

    q_1=1/(2*Htail), q_2=-c/(2*D).

Their energies are 1-C^2*q_j^2. Both are strictly positive throughout
the interval: 2*Htail-C=21/50+t>0, and C*c/(2*D)<128/275<1.
The C86 theorem is proved as part of this same joint argument: positive
unit auxiliary energy implies positive auxiliary energy at EVERY
curvature 0<k<=1 on the compact domain. Consequently all middle C83
expressions are strictly positive, because each has the form

    beta*V0+eta*(1+eta)*V_k,

where beta>=0, V0>0 by the strict base cuts, eta>0, and V_k>0.
This includes every changing active residual and every beta=0 branch.
The first cut is handled throughout all curvatures by the affine
polynomial argument in C85 Section3. The last cut is checked next.

## 3. Extending a quadratic prefix through a step with k*h<=1

The following identity is useful independently of this example. Compare
the C83 auxiliary energy with a quadratic prefix of mass H followed by
a constant-gradient suffix of mass v+R, with the energy obtained by
extending that quadratic prefix through the next step v and leaving
suffix mass R. Let p be the prefix polynomial at the common boundary,
and write

    xi=k*H, tau=k*v, upsilon=k*R.

Direct subtraction of the two exact C83 energy formulas gives

    V_new-V_old=p^2*Dext,

where

    Dext=[xi^2+xi*(1+2*tau+2*upsilon+k)]
           *[1/(1+tau+upsilon)^2
                       -(1-tau)^2/(1+upsilon)^2]
          +tau*[(1+k-tau)
            -(1+k+tau+2*upsilon)*(1-tau)^2/(1+upsilon)^2]. (3)

If 0<=tau<=1, both brackets are nonnegative. For the first use
(1-tau)*(1+tau+upsilon)<=1+upsilon. The numerator of the second,
after multiplication by (1+upsilon)^2, is exactly

    tau*[2k+(1-k)*tau-tau^2]
       +2*(k+tau-tau^2)*upsilon+(1+k-tau)*upsilon^2>=0,

the sign identity from C85. Thus adding any such step cannot decrease
the auxiliary energy. This is an identity for the two actual energy
formulas; it does not assume either un-repaired array is interpolable.

## 4. Full quadratic, from zero curvature through the mixed-sign region

Write x=7/5, y=9/4, z=33/25+t, w=7/4, and s=sum h<7. Let Vfull(k)
be the actual primitive energy of the scalar quadratic k*x_coord^2/2
started at x_coord=1. It is

    Vfull/k=B4-s*[1+k*(s+1)]*p_4^2,
    B4=sum_(i=0)^3 h_i*[1+k*(1-h_i)]*p_i^2.          (4)

For 0<k<=4/7, the last step satisfies k*w<=1. Apply (3) with old
prefix h0,h1,h2, next step w and R=0. Section2 proved positive energy
for that old C83 auxiliary array at every curvature. Hence Vfull>0.

Next consider 4/7<=k<=1/z. Since 1/z<=25/33, all four coefficients
1-k*(h_i-1) are positive: the smallest possible one is bounded below
by 1-(25/33)*(5/4)=7/132>0. Therefore

    B4>=x*[1-k*(x-1)]>=x*(2-x)=21/25.

On this whole interval, the four factors obey

    |1-x*k|<=1/5,
    |1-y*k|<=31/44<3/4,
    0<=1-z*k<=43/175<1/4,
    |1-w*k|<=43/132<1/3.

For the first bound use the endpoints k=4/7 and k=25/33 of the
larger interval; its values are 1/5 and -2/33. The other three bounds
follow from the same endpoint inequalities and z>=33/25. Thus
|p_4|<1/80. Since s*[1+k*(s+1)]<63, (4) gives

    Vfull/k>21/25-63/6400>0.                         (5)

Every reciprocal-root boundary is retained in this argument.

## 5. Full quadratic on the all-negative-factor interval

Now 1/z<k<=1. Since z<x<w<y, every factor is negative. For any
r in {x,y}, put

    f_r(k)=r*[1-k*(r-1)]/(r*k-1)^2.

Its derivative is r*[r*(r-1)*k-r-1]/(r*k-1)^3. Both f_x and f_y
strictly decrease on this interval, because x^2-2x-1<0 and
y^2-2y-1=-7/16<0. Also f_x>0 since x<2.

Define the normalized three-step prefix expression

    A3(k)=f_x(k)/(y*k-1)^2+f_y(k)+z*[1-k*(z-1)].

Every term decreases: for the first, its numerator is positive and
decreasing while its denominator increases. Thus A3 strictly decreases.
At k=1 the unit-curvature telescoping identity gives

    A3(1)=(1-p_3(1)^2)/p_2(1)^2=4-c^2>0,

since p_2(1)=a*b=1/2 and p_3(1)=-c/2. Consequently A3(k)>0
throughout the whole interval.

Divide (4) by the positive p_3(k)^2. The resulting expression is

    A3(k)/(z*k-1)^2+w*[1-k*(w-1)]
                     -s*[1+k*(s+1)]*(w*k-1)^2.     (6)

It strictly decreases. Its first term has positive decreasing numerator
and increasing denominator, its second term decreases, and its last
term is the negative of a positive increasing product. At k=1 it is
positive because the exact unit quadratic energy is

    Vfull(1)=1-C^2*(a*b*c*d)^2>1-(24/25)^2>0

by (1). Thus (6), hence Vfull(k), is positive for every 1/z<k<=1.
Together with Sections4 and2 this proves strict survival of ALL
C83 curvatures and every full scalar quadratic on 0<=t<=1/100.
No exact coefficient replay from the earlier draft was needed.

## 6. Why this family is nevertheless not a seed

The following different actual witness, also suggested by max2 and
rederived here, separates this whole interval from the full seed cone.
For ANY a,b,c,d>0 put alpha=b/(2+c)>0 and use the single scalar
function with derivative clip(x_coord,-alpha,1), minimum0 at0, and
initial point P=2+a. The original trajectory is

    x=(P,1,-b,-alpha,d*alpha),
    g=(1,1,-alpha,-alpha,min(d*alpha,1)).

The four GD updates have precisely the lengths 1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d.
All values satisfy f_i=x_i*g_i-g_i^2/2. The exact primitive energy
telescopes to P^2-(x4+s*g4)^2, s=C-1. If d*alpha<=1, it is
P^2-C^2*d^2*alpha^2. If d*alpha>1, then x4+s>C>P and it is
strictly negative. Hence EVERY full original seed must satisfy

    C*b*d<=(2+a)*(2+c).                              (7)

Every interpolation comparison is legal because this is one explicit
globally convex differentiable1-smooth function; no sampled pair is
discarded and no converse certificate theorem is needed.

For the family here, d*alpha<15/32<1 and

    C*b*d-(2+a)*(2+c)=3339/2000-(117/80)*t>0

throughout 0<=t<=1/100. Thus every member is excluded by the full
primitive identity in accepted04219554e41d9750. It is a survivor of
the old necessary-test system and a proved negative instance for a
different necessary test, not an actual feasible seed.

## 7. Consequence for the assignment

The intended global contradiction from C82, C83 and the previous
endpoint/slack/triangle list is FALSE at draft level: the whole interval
above satisfies them strictly. The max2 scalar proof in the appendix
checks all its extra lifts on 0<=t<=1/100; Sections2--5 independently
supply the previously missing uniform-curvature interval at that scale.

The complete curvature elimination of C84--C86 remains useful and
correctly cannot reject this interval. Its finite algebraic signs do not
become a global separator. Any further global elimination must use a
new constraint such as (7), or the original coefficient/PSD interface.
The enlarged system including (7) is not proved empty or nonempty here.



# Appendix A: full uniform-domain and C84--C86 proofs

# C84--C86 complete joint draft: compact triangle and all middle curvatures

Status: UNVERIFIED; no fact_submit allowance. This file includes every draft prerequisite as proof text. The only accepted predecessors used are 04219554e41d9750 and 1252b8823bb5a290. Historical appendix labels are local to their appendix; no claim of earlier acceptance is made. No solver/model evaluation, new proposed word, numerical scan, or verifier call was performed.

The global four-step seed decision and original asymptotic target remain OPEN. The completed results eliminate C82 witness parameters and the entire middle-cut curvature quantifiers. The simultaneous sign of the resulting finite tests on the compact domain is the remaining condition, not a claimed solution.



# C84: one triangle boundary on the whole compact seed domain

Status: UNVERIFIED analytical joint proof. No numerical discovery, candidate
word evaluation, solver, or verifier call is made. The joint version appends
the complete compact-domain derivation and the C81 construction. Those drafts
are proof text to be checked jointly, never accepted premises. Accepted facts
used are 04219554e41d9750 (the primitive energy identity) and
1252b8823bb5a290 (the full smooth-convex interpolation construction).

## 1. Canonical coordinates and prerequisites

Use exactly the canonical schedule coordinates

    h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d), C=1+sum h=5+a+b+c+d.

The compact-domain derivation appended to the joint proof establishes, for
any full four-step seed of the requested capacity,

    193/25<=C<87/10,
    0<a,d<77/97<1,
    0<b,c<1552559/1081453<sqrt(3).

It also derives the actual adjacent-triangle necessary inequalities. The
first and middle instances, in these same coordinates, are

    a*b*[1+a*(C+1)] <= C-2-a-b,                       (1)
    b*c*[1+b*(C+2+a)/(2+a)^2] <= 2+d.                (2)

Every denominator is positive. In particular (2) gives b*c<2+d.
No reversal, prefix primitivity, fixed capacity, or multiplier support is
assumed. These are consequences of full actual witnesses in the appendix.

Inequality (1) also forces a*b<1. Indeed it rearranges to

    b <= (C-2-a)/(1+a+a^2*(C+1)).

The denominator minus a times the numerator is

    1+3a+2a^2-C*a*(1-a)
      > 1-6a+11a^2
      =11*(a-3/11)^2+2/11 >0,

where C<9 and 0<a<1 justify the strict first inequality. Thus a*b<1
throughout the remaining full domain, rather than just on a trial word.

## 2. Complete minimization of the C81 family on that domain

Define

    D84=2+d,
    ell84=c*(1+d)*(D84-b*c)/D84^2 >0,
    H84(u)=(u+1)*(a*u-1),
    R84=(1-a+sqrt((1+a)^2+4*a*ell84))/(2*a).

The actual triangle construction in the appendix permits precisely

    u>=b, z>0, ell84<=z^2<=H84(u).

Since H84(1/a)=0 and H84 is strictly increasing for u>=1/a, these
constraints are equivalent to u>=R84 and ell84<=z^2<=H84(u). Here

    R84>1/a>b,

so the competing endpoint u=b and the vanishing-height boundary never
occur in this compact domain. The exact primitive energy is

    E84(u,z)=((1+a)*u-1)^2+(1-b^2)*z^2
                 -C^2*(b*c/D84)^2.                  (3)

For b<=1, minimization first sets z^2=ell84. The remaining square is
strictly increasing for u>=R84>1/a, hence is minimized at R84.

For b>1, minimization first sets z^2=H84(u). Its derivative as a
quadratic in u has positive leading coefficient

    q2=(1+a)^2+a*(1-b^2)
        >(1+a)^2-2a=1+a^2>0.

At u=1/a the derivative equals

    (1+a)*(2/a+1-b^2)>0,

because a<1 and b^2<3. It is increasing afterwards, so again the minimum
is at R84, where H84(R84)=ell84. The case b=1 agrees with both formulas.

Consequently the infimum is attained by the SINGLE actual boundary

    u=R84, z=sqrt(ell84),

and every full seed must satisfy

    E84:=((1+a)*R84-1)^2+(1-b^2)*ell84
                         -C^2*(b*c/D84)^2 >=0.       (4)

Whenever (4) fails, substituting these explicit u,z into the C81 triangle
gives a negative actual witness. Its gradients are

    (u,0), (-1,z), (b,0), (-b*c/D84,0), (-b*c/D84,0),

and its iterates are

    ((1+a)*u-1,z), (-1,z), (b,-b*z),
    (-b*c,-b*z), (-b*c/D84,-b*z).

All original pairs and the optimum comparisons are checked in the
appended C81 construction; no limiting interpretation is needed since
ell84>0. This is an explicit rule throughout the inequality-failure
region, not a finite curvature or schedule test.

## 3. Exact polynomial branch form, including the sqrt(2) boundary

The remaining square root can itself be removed with exact sign branches.
Put

    L84=c*(1+d)*(D84-b*c),
    K84=1-2*a-a^2=2-(1+a)^2,
    Y84=(1+a)^2*D84^2+4*a*L84 >0,
    U84=2*(1+a)*K84*D84,
    T84=D84^2*((1+a)^4+K84^2)
          +4*a*((1+a)^2+a*(1-b^2))*L84
          -4*a^2*C^2*b^2*c^2.

Direct substitution of R84 into (4) gives the identity

    4*a^2*D84^2*E84=T84+U84*sqrt(Y84).               (5)

Thus E84>=0 is exactly the following finite polynomial condition:

* If K84>0: either T84>=0, or T84<0 and T84^2<=U84^2*Y84.
* If K84=0: T84>=0.
* If K84<0: T84>=0 and T84^2>=U84^2*Y84.

For the first branch, a nonnegative T84 makes the sum in (5) positive;
when it is negative both sides of U84*sqrt(Y84)>=-T84 are nonnegative,
so squaring preserves the inequality. For the third branch, (5) requires
T84>=-U84*sqrt(Y84)>0, and squaring is again legitimate. The middle
branch has no radical term. In particular the boundary h0=sqrt(2) is
retained exactly, not allocated to a neighboring sign case by continuity.

## 4. Scope

This removes every u,z quantifier and every C82 minimizer branch on the
whole compact positive domain, and supplies the actual minimizing
trajectory there. It does not show E84<0 for every remaining schedule.
The simultaneous nonnegative region with the other tests is not proved
empty or nonempty here; none of these conditions is a seed certificate.



# C85: exact cancellations and proved harmless curvature intervals

Status: UNVERIFIED analytical joint extension of the full C83 proof,
which is appended in the joint artifact. No unverified claim is used
as an accepted predecessor. No numerical evaluation or verifier call.
The two accepted ingredients remain interpolation fact1252b8823bb5a290
and the primitive identity in fact04219554e41d9750.

## 1. The exact curvature inequality with smaller degrees

Use a fixed nonnegative word, a cut j with h_j>1, and the C83 definitions

    P=sum_(i<j)h_i, R=sum_(i>j)h_i, H=P+h_j,
    s=H+R, C=1+s, eta=(h_j-1)/(1+R),
    V0=(1+P)^2-C^2*eta^2, C0=eta*(1+eta),
    p_i(k)=product_(t<i)(1-k*h_t), E(k)=1+k*R.

Here k is curvature in (0,1]. Put p=p_(j+1) and define

    B(k)=sum_(i<=j)h_i*[1+k*(1-h_i)]*p_i(k)^2,
    Pcut(k)=E(k)^2*B(k)-H*[1+k*(s+R+1)]*p(k)^2.

The polynomial Pcut has a double zero at k=0. To check this, let
P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t. Then

    B(0)=H,
    B'(0)=sum_(i<=j)h_i*(1-h_i-2P_i)=H-H^2,
    p(0)=1, p'(0)=-H.

The derivative of E^2*B at zero is 2R*H+H-H^2. The derivative of
the second term is H*(s+R+1-2H)=H*(2R+1-H), the same number.
Both constant terms are H. Consequently

    Qcut(k):=Pcut(k)/k^2                            (1)

is a polynomial of degree at most 2j+1, including at zero. This is
exact division in the polynomial ring, not a limiting numerical test.

For R>0 define the polynomials

    d_i(k)=[E(k)*p_i(k)-p(k)]/k,
    T_i(k)=2R*p(k)*d_i(k)-(1-k)*d_i(k)^2,
    Tplus(k)=max(0,T_0(k),...,T_j(k)).

Each numerator in d_i vanishes at zero. The complete all-pair C83
calculation gives

    beta=k^3*Tplus/(2R*E^2),
    V_k=k^3*Qcut/E^2.

Since the canceled factor k^3/E^2 is strictly positive for the
original curvature domain, the exact necessary inequality becomes

    V0*Tplus(k)+2R*C0*Qcut(k)>=0, 0<k<=1.            (2)

The two middle cuts of a four-step word therefore have degree at most
THREE and FIVE on every branch, rather than five and seven. Branch
selection and beta=0 are unchanged. If Tplus=0, (2) is exactly
Qcut>=0 because R,C0>0; no division by beta has taken place.

At R=0 there is no repair credit or Tplus definition: the auxiliary
data are legal by themselves and their exact energy is

    V_k=k^3*Qcut(k),

so Qcut>=0 remains the necessary test. This retains the last cut.

## 2. The entire interval before any scaled prefix step exceeds one

Suppose 0<k<=1 and k*h_i<=1 for every i<=j. Then

    V_k>=0.                                         (3)

This statement does not require the base cut inequality or a bound on R.
Here is a direct proof. For i<=j write r=k*h_i and
t=k*sum_(i<l<=j)h_l. All factors 1-k*h_l are nonnegative. The product
of the later factors is at most 1/(1+t): indeed
1-u<=1/(1+u) for 0<=u<=1, and product(1+u_l)>=1+sum u_l.
It follows, without dividing by a possibly zero prefix factor, that

    p(k)^2<=p_i(k)^2*(1-r)^2/(1+t)^2.                (4)

For 0<=r<=1, t>=0 and 0<k<=1 the exact difference

    (1+k-r)*(1+t)^2-(1+k+r+2t)*(1-r)^2
      =r*[2k+(1-k)r-r^2]
         +2*(k+r-r^2)*t+(1+k-r)*t^2                 (5)

is nonnegative. The bracket in the first term is a concave quadratic
in r; its endpoint values are 2k and k, so it is positive throughout
[0,1]. The other coefficients are also nonnegative. Combining (4)--(5)
therefore gives

    [1+k*(1-h_i)]*p_i^2
      >=[1+k+k*h_i+2k*sum_(i<l<=j)h_l]*p^2.

Multiplying by h_i and summing, the identity
sum h_i*(h_i+2sum_(i<l<=j)h_l)=H^2 gives

    B(k)>=H*[1+k*(H+1)]*p^2.                        (6)

Finally

    [1+k*(H+1)]*E^2-[1+k*(H+2R+1)]
      =2k^2*R*(H+1)+k^2*R^2*[1+k*(H+1)]>=0.

This and (6) prove Pcut>=0, hence (3). Every zero product is already
covered by (4); no continuity assumption hides a denominator zero.

For any schedule that also passes the actual base cut, V0>=0. Thus
beta*V0+C0*V_k>=0 automatically throughout

    0<k<=1/max_(i<=j)h_i.                            (7)

This removes a whole interval with a sign proof; checking a finite grid
or one endpoint was not used as a proxy for that interval.

## 3. The first cut adds no curvature constraint beyond its base cut

Take j=0 and write x=h0, R=sum_(i>0)h_i, s=x+R, C=1+s. Direct expansion
of (1) gives the affine polynomial

    Qcut(k)=x*s*(s+2)
        +x*[R^2*(1-x)-x^2*(x+2R+1)]*k.              (8)

Its value at zero is positive. At k=1 it is

    Qcut(1)=(1+R)^2-C^2*(x-1)^2.

When x>1, the first base-cut inequality is C*(x-1)<=1+R, so this
second endpoint is nonnegative as well. An affine polynomial whose
two endpoint values are nonnegative is nonnegative on [0,1]. Therefore
V_k>=0 for every curvature, and the full j=0 C83 family is redundant
once that cut has passed. In the compact positive domain h0>1, so
this argument covers its whole first-cut branch.

## 4. Exact branch identities at the second cut

Take j=1, x=h0, y=h1, H=x+y, R=h2+h3, s=H+R, Z=x*y. Then

    p_1=1-x*k, p_2=(1-x*k)*(1-y*k), E=1+R*k,
    d_0=s-Z*k, d_1=(R+y)*(1-x*k),
    T_1=(R+y)*(1-x*k)^2
                    *[(R-y)-((2y-1)*R-y)*k],        (9)
    T_0=(s-Z*k)*[2R*p_2-(1-k)*(s-Z*k)],             (10)
    T_0-T_1=x*E*J(k),                               (11)
    J(k)=2R*p_2-(1-k)*(d_0+d_1).

On the compact domain, x<2, y>1 and R>2. The constant coefficient
of J is -x-2y<0, its quadratic coefficient is

    x*[R*(2y-1)-2y]>2*x*(y-1)>0,

and J(1)=2R*(x-1)*(y-1)>0. Thus J has exactly one positive root
theta in (0,1), because the product of its two roots is negative.
It is negative below theta and positive above theta. Moreover
theta>max(1/x,1/y). At k=1/x, p_2=d_1=0 and d_0>0, so J<0.
At k=1/y, p_2=0 and

    d_0+d_1=E*y*(2-x/y)>0,

since x<2 and y>1, so again J<0.

For k>=max(1/x,1/y), p_1<=0 and p_2>=0. In this interval d_1<=0,
so T_1<=0 directly from its defining formula. Also d_0>0: the compact
bounds imply x<9/5, y<49/20, s>=168/25, hence
s-x*y>168/25-441/100=231/100>0. Therefore

    Tplus=max(0,T_0)                                (12)

throughout this high-curvature interval. The bracket

    J0(k)=2R*p_2-(1-k)*(s-Z*k)

in (10) has positive leading coefficient Z*(2R-1). It is negative
at both reciprocal roots and positive at 1. There is exactly one
root rho in (max(1/x,1/y),1), its larger real root. Consequently the
zero-credit branch is selected up to rho, and T_0 is selected from
rho to 1. The equality k=rho is preserved and satisfies T_0=0.

The interval between the two reciprocal roots is NOT discarded.
There p_2<=0 and d_0>0, so T_0<=0, but T_1 may still be positive
when x>y. Formula (9), including its zero at k=1/x and its linear
factor, is the exact branch criterion on that interval. When y>=x,
p_1>=0 there and d_1>=0, so T_1<=0 and beta=0 on the whole interval.
Every order case and every credit-zero boundary is retained.

## 5. What has and has not been decided

The complete all-curvature family has lost its first-cut branch and
the harmless intervals (7), by sign proofs. The remaining middle-cut
branches are cubic or quintic, with the exact second-cut classification
above; the last-cut quadratic-family energy is a polynomial of degree
at most seven after the same cancellation. No uniform sign conclusion
is claimed for the remaining middle- or last-cut intervals.

The original missing seed still requires all twenty nonnegative
multipliers and the full five-gradient PSD identity. These necessary
tests have not constructed those multipliers or globally excluded them.



# C86: full elimination of the middle-cut curvature quantifier

Status: UNVERIFIED joint analytical proof. No numerical schedule or model
evaluation, finite grid, optimizer, installation, or fact_submit. C84/C85
and their complete prerequisite proofs form part of the SAME joint draft;
they are not imported as accepted facts.

## 1. Domain and exact statement

Keep h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d) and C=1+sum h. The full compact-domain
derivation in the joint appendix supplies the following coarse consequences:

    1<h0,h3<9/5, 1<h1,h2<49/20,
    193/25<=C<87/10, b*c<2+d.                         (1)

The sharper bounds remain valid but are unnecessary for the estimates here.
Write m=49/20 and m0=9/5. For each middle cut j=1,2 use the C85
polynomials Qcut,T_i and the exact energy V_k, all recomputed for the
SAME original h. The actual base-cut inequality gives V0>=0.

Then every curvature up to

    k_hi=1/min_(0<=i<=j)h_i                          (2)

has V_k>0. Thus all middle-cut tests on (0,k_hi] pass automatically.
On (k_hi,1] every prefix factor 1-k*h_i is negative. There the only
possibly positive T_i is T_0 for j=1 and T_1 for j=2.

Moreover V_k has at most one sign change on (k_hi,1], from positive
to negative. Therefore, if V_1>=0, the ENTIRE cut-curvature family is
automatically satisfied. If V_1<0 its unique zero on this interval is
the unique root of Qcut there. These assertions concern the actual
auxiliary energy, before any repair credit is divided out.

Sections 2--5 prove all the sign claims. Section 6 then replaces the
whole remaining curvature interval by finitely many exact algebraic
evaluations, including every zero-credit and stationary boundary.

## 2. A bound used in the mixed-sign intervals

For a prefix of mass H and a suffix of mass R put

    F_HR(k)=[1+k*(H+2R+1)]/(1+k*R)^2.

It decreases as R increases, since its derivative with respect to R is
-2k^2*(H+R+1)/(1+k*R)^3. When R>=2 and H<=17/4,

    F_HR(k)<3/2,

because after using R=2 the numerator of the positive difference is
(24k^2-13k+2)/4; its discriminant is 169-192<0 and leading
coefficient is positive. When R>=1 and H<=67/10,

    F_HR(k)<3,

because after using R=1 the corresponding numerator is bounded below
by 3k^2-(37/10)k+2, whose discriminant is 1369/100-24<0.

In all cases the auxiliary energy has the exact form

    V_k/k=B(k)-H*F_HR(k)*p_(j+1)(k)^2.               (3)

The C85 no-overshoot proof already handles k<=1/max_(i<=j)h_i.
Only the remaining mixed-sign intervals need estimates.

## 3. The second cut: both possible step orders

Write x=h0,y=h1,H=x+y,R=h2+h3. Here H<17/4 and R>2.

First suppose y>=x and 1/y<=k<=1/x. Put t=1-k*x>=0. Then

    B(k)=1+(x-1)*t+(y/x)*[1+x-y+(y-1)*t]*t^2.

Since t<=1-x/y, x>1 and y<m, this gives

    B(k)>=1-(y-x-1)_+*(y-x)^2/(x*y)
          >1-(9/20)*(841/980)>3/5.                  (4)

The ratio (y-x)^2/(x*y) increases with y/x>=1 and is less than
(m-1)^2/m=841/980, which justifies the bound in (4). The possible
negative lobe of p_2 has absolute maximum (y-x)^2/(4*x*y), hence

    |p_2|<841/3920, p_2^2<1/20.

The last comparison follows from 20*841^2<3920^2. Equation (3) now
gives V_k/k>3/5-(17/4)*(3/2)*(1/20)=9/32>0.

Next suppose x>y and 1/x<=k<=1/y. Both x,y<m0. Each coefficient
1-k*(h_i-1) is positive and exceeds 1/5, so B(k)>1/5 from its
first term alone. The negative lobe now satisfies

    |p_2|<(m0-1)^2/(4*m0)=4/45, p_2^2<1/100.

Also H<18/5. Thus V_k/k>1/5-(18/5)*(3/2)/100=73/500>0.
These two order cases include the reciprocal boundaries. Together
with C85 they prove positivity through k_hi for j=1.

## 4. The third cut: all mixed-sign patterns

Write x=h0,y=h1,z=h2,H=x+y+z,R=h3. Thus H<67/10,R>1,
and F_HR<3. The following cases cover every curvature for which at
least one of the three prefix factors is nonnegative.

(a) Suppose k*x<=1. If all three factors are nonnegative, C85 applies.
Otherwise at least one of the last two factors is negative. Any positive
factor is at most 1-k and the absolute value of a negative factor is
at most m*k-1. The two possible sign patterns therefore yield

    |p_3| <= max((1-k)^2*(m*k-1),(1-k)*(m*k-1)^2)
           <=4*(m-1)^3/(27*m)=24389/132300<3/16.     (5)

The two cubic maxima follow by differentiating in u=1-k: the
expressions are u^2*(m-1-m*u) and u*(m-1-m*u)^2. Their maxima on
0<=u<=1-1/m are respectively 4*(m-1)^3/(27*m^2) and
4*(m-1)^3/(27*m); m>1 selects the latter. Thus (5) is a proof over
the whole interval, not an endpoint estimate.

The first term of B is 1+(x-1)*(1-k*x)>=1. If k<=20/29 then every
coefficient 1-k*(h_i-1) is nonnegative and B>=1. If k>20/29,

    p_1^2<(9/29)^2<1/10, |p_2|<1/4.

For the second bound, a positive p_2 is at most (1-k)^2<81/841<1/4;
a negative p_2 has magnitude at most the maximum of (1-k)*(m*k-1),
namely (m-1)^2/(4*m)=841/3920<1/4. Each possible negative coefficient
h_i*[1-k*(h_i-1)] is greater than -m*(m-2)=-441/400. Consequently

    B>1-(441/400)*(1/10+1/16)
       =1-5733/32000>4/5.

Using (3) and (5), V_k/k>4/5-(67/10)*3*(9/256)=239/2560>0.

(b) Suppose k*x>1 but k*y<=1. Then y<x<m0 and k lies between
1/x and 1/y. The bound from Section 3 gives |p_2|<4/45 and
p_2^2<1/100. The first two coefficients in B are positive, and
its first term exceeds x*(2-x)>9/25. The only potentially negative
third term is greater than -(441/400)*p_2^2. Therefore

    B>9/25-441/40000=13959/40000.

Also |p_3|<(4/45)*(29/20)=29/225, so p_3^2<1/60 because
60*29^2<225^2. Here H<2*m0+m=121/20. Hence

    V_k/k>13959/40000-(121/20)*3/60
           =1859/40000>0.

(c) Suppose k*x>1,k*y>1 but k*z<=1. Necessarily z<x<m0, so
H<121/20. Put u=k*y-1>0 and v=k*z in [k,1]. The contribution
of the last two prefix steps to B, after removing the nonnegative
factor p_1^2, is

    Btail=y*[1-k*(y-1)]+z*[1-k*(z-1)]*u^2.

Since v*(1+k-v)>=k for v in [k,1], expansion gives

    k*Btail=(u+1)*(k-u)+v*(1+k-v)*u^2
          >=k-(1-k)*u*(u+1)
          >=k*[1-m*(1-k)*(m*k-1)]
          >=k*[1-(m-1)^2/4]=759*k/1600>0.

Thus B>x*(2-x)>9/25. In this case

    |p_3|< (m0*k-1)*(m*k-1)*(1-k).

On k>=1/m0 the ratio (m0*k-1)/(m*k-1) increases with k and is
at most (m0-1)/(m-1)=16/29. By the same cubic maximum as in (5),

    |p_3|<(16/29)*(24389/132300)=3364/33075<1/9.

Therefore V_k/k>9/25-(121/20)*3/81=9/25-121/540>0.

Cases (a)--(c), including equality at any reciprocal root, exhaust
all curvatures through k_hi. This proves the claimed uniform
positivity for the entire j=2 mixed-sign range.

## 5. Unique auxiliary-energy sign change at high curvature

Now every prefix factor is negative. Let

    f_x(k)=x*[1-k*(x-1)]/(x*k-1)^2.

Its derivative is

    f_x'(k)=x*[x*(x-1)*k-x-1]/(x*k-1)^3.             (6)

For x=h0<m0 this is strictly negative, and f_x>0.

For j=1, divide V_k/k by (x*k-1)^2. The result is

    L1(k)=f_x(k)+y*[1-k*(y-1)]
                   -H*F_HR(k)*(y*k-1)^2.           (7)

All three terms together have strictly negative derivative. The first
two decrease. For the final term, the logarithmic derivative of the
positive product F_HR(k)*(y*k-1)^2 is

    (H+2R+1)/(1+k*(H+2R+1))
       +2y/(y*k-1)-2R/(1+k*R)>0,

because y/(y*k-1)>1/k>R/(1+k*R). Thus L1 strictly decreases.

For j=2, dividing V_k/k by (x*k-1)^2*(y*k-1)^2 gives

    L2(k)=f_x(k)/(y*k-1)^2+f_y(k)
                 +z*[1-k*(z-1)]
                 -H*F_HR(k)*(z*k-1)^2.             (8)

The last two terms strictly decrease. To control the first two, (6)
and x<m0 imply

    f_x'(k)<-(34/25)/(64/125)=-85/32,
    d/dk[f_x/(y*k-1)^2]
          <-(85/32)*(400/841)<-1.                  (9)

Indeed 1+2x-x^2>34/25, x>1, and 0<x*k-1<4/5. The extra term in
the derivative in (9) is negative because f_x>0. If f_y'<=0 there
is nothing further to bound. If f_y'>0, (6) gives

    y*k-1>2/(y-1)>1,
    y*[y*(y-1)*k-y-1]
       <=y*(y^2-2y-1)<(49/20)*(41/400)=2009/8000<1.

The cubic denominator in (6) is then greater than one, so f_y'<1.
Combining with (9) proves strict decrease of their sum, hence of L2.

Sections 3--4 give positive V_k at k_hi. The positive factors used
in (7)--(8) preserve signs on the open high-curvature interval.
Strict decrease therefore proves the one-crossing assertions in
Section 1, including V_1=0, for which V_k>0 at every k<1.

## 6. One repair branch and an explicit finite curvature rule

The following definitions work for both middle cuts. Put

    u=h_(j-1), v=h_j, R=sum_(i>j)h_i,
    Wpair=u+v+R, Zpair=u*v,
    Kpair(k)=Wpair-Zpair*k,
    Jpair(k)=2R*(1-u*k)*(1-v*k)-(1-k)*Kpair(k).

Kpair is positive on [0,1]. For j=1 this follows from
Kpair(1)=s-h0*h1>231/100 by the compact bounds. For j=2 it follows
from Kpair(1)=1+h3-(h1-1)*(h2-1)>0, the middle-triangle condition.

The leading coefficient of Jpair is Zpair*(2R-1)>0. Its value at
both 1/u and 1/v is negative, and Jpair(1)=2R*(u-1)*(v-1)>0.
Let rho_j be its larger real root. It is unambiguously defined and

    max(1/u,1/v)<rho_j<1.

On k>=k_hi, every inactive T_i is nonpositive, by its original
definition and the signs of p_i and p_(j+1). The active polynomial is

    Tact_1=Kpair*Jpair,
    Tact_2=(1-h0*k)^2*Kpair*Jpair.                   (10)

For j=2 specifically p_0,p_2>0 and p_3<0, so T_0,T_2<=0; also
d_1=(1-h0*k)*Kpair<0. For j=1 p_1<0,p_2>0 gives T_1<=0.
Thus Tplus=max(0,Tact_j) throughout the only interval still relevant.

Define the lower endpoint and the single polynomial

    l_j=max(k_hi,rho_j),
    F_j(k)=V0*Tact_j(k)+2R*C0*Qcut(k).              (11)

The complete j-th C83 family is equivalent, on domain (1) and the
base-cut inequality, to F_j>=0 on [l_j,1]. Indeed below k_hi the
energy is positive; on [k_hi,l_j] when nonempty the credit is zero.
The unique energy sign change shows that nonnegativity at its upper
endpoint suffices on this zero-credit interval. At l_j=rho_j,
F_j(l_j)=2R*C0*Qcut(l_j), so the same endpoint check supplies exactly
that condition. If l_j=k_hi>rho_j the interval is empty and positivity
at k_hi was already proved. On [l_j,1] the active branch in (10)
is the correct one, including every equality boundary.

Finally form the FINITE algebraic set

    A_j={l_j,1} union {k in (l_j,1): F_j'(k)=0}.     (12)

If the derivative is the zero polynomial, use only {l_j,1}; the
polynomial itself is then constant. Repeated derivative roots are
included. A polynomial on a compact interval attains a minimum, and
any interior minimum has zero derivative, by the two one-sided
difference quotients. Hence the entire curvature family is equivalent
to the finitely many sign tests F_j(k)>=0 for k in A_j.

F_1 has degree at most three and F_2 degree at most five. Thus the
derivative equations in (12) have degree at most two and four, with
at most two and four distinct roots, respectively. For F_1=A*k^3+
B*k^2+D*k+E with A!=0, the only possible interior strict minimum is
(-B+sqrt(B^2-3*A*D))/(3*A), provided B^2-3*A*D>0 and the point lies
in the interval. If A=0,B>0 it is -D/(2B); otherwise endpoints suffice.
Keeping all derivative roots as in (12) also handles every lower-degree
case without separate conventions.

This gives a concrete witness rule: choose any member k of A_j with
F_j(k)<0. If k=rho_j and the repair credit vanishes, the C83 auxiliary
jets alone are legal and have negative energy. Otherwise Tact_j>0,
beta=k^3*Tact_j/(2R*(1+k*R)^2)>0, and the C83 orthogonal sum with
weight C0/beta is an actual full-pair negative witness. No division
occurs at a vanishing-credit point. If l_j=k_hi, its positive energy
precludes its being selected as a negative witness.

## 7. Exact remaining global condition

The C82 triangle has the single value E84 and the finite polynomial
branches from C84. The two WHOLE middle-curvature families have the
finite sets A_1,A_2 above. A global exclusion would require proving
that every original schedule in the derived compact domain violates
at least one base/triangle condition, E84>=0, or one of the signs
F_j(k)>=0, k in A_j (or another retained necessary condition).

No such simultaneous sign theorem is proved here. In particular the
finite signs in (12) have not been declared negative on the entire
domain, and no survivor is claimed. The untouched full-quadratic
last-cut condition and all twenty original multiplier/PSD constraints
also remain available; they are not inferred from these finite tests.
This is complete middle-curvature elimination with a witness rule on
every negative branch, and partial progress toward the full seed
decision. It is not a global separator or an actual seed.




# Appendix A: full original compact-domain derivation, reproduced as a joint proof

# Full four-step seed: a compact domain from legal cut functions

Status: complete joint proof DRAFT, not verifier-accepted. This round has no
numerical or fact_submit allowance. No unverified finding is used as a premise.
The accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the interpolation convention Q<=0;
the particular functions and all algebra below are derived explicitly.

## 1. Interface and energy

Let h=(h_0,h_1,h_2,h_3)>=0, s=sum h_i, C=1+s>=193/25.
Indices i,j range over0,...,4. Set p_i=1+sum_(t<i)h_t, so p_0=1,p_4=C.
Let e=(1,1,1,1,1)^T. Define T_ii=1,T_it=h_t for t<i,T_it=0 for t>i,
Lambda_ii=0,Lambda_ij>=0,L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
omega=(-2h_0,-2h_1,-2h_2,-2h_3,2s)^T, and
D=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_3(2-h_3),-s(s+2)).
The full interface is

    L^T e=omega, M=D+(LT+T^T L^T)/2 >=0.                 (1)

No multiplier support, rank, remainder, symmetry, or step order is fixed.
For arbitrary formal GD data x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i, define

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    V=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f_4)+sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
      -s(s+1)||g_4||^2.

Put delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2 and y_i=x_i-g_i=x_0-(Tg)_i.
Then Q_ij=delta_i-delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i>, while
V=-omega^T delta+sum_i D_ii||g_i||^2. Value flow in(1) therefore gives

    V+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij = sum_(i,j)M_ij<g_i,g_j>. (2)

In particular every feasible full certificate implies V>=0 on every legal
convex differentiable1-smooth trajectory with a minimizer. This direction
requires neither certificate necessity nor any duality assertion.

## 2. Legal cut witnesses at every position

Fix j<4, put b=h_j,P=sum_(i<j)h_i,R=sum_(i>j)h_i, and first assume b>1.
Set a=(b-1)/(R+1)>0. Define the scalar function

    f(x)=x-1/2                     if x>=1,
         x^2/2                     if -a<=x<=1,
         -a*x-a^2/2                if x<=-a.

Its derivative is clamp(x,[-a,1]), which is continuous, nondecreasing,
and1-Lipschitz. The function is convex1-smooth and has a minimizer at0.
Start at x_0=P+1. Before and at j all gradients are1, and x_j=1.
After step j, x_(j+1)=1-b=-a(R+1). All subsequent gradients equal-a,
since the remaining nonnegative steps move monotonically to x_4=-a.
Every sampled value obeys f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2, and x_4=g_4.

For any vector-valued data obeying these two identities, GD telescoping gives

    2sum_(i<4)h_i f_i+sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
      =||x_0||^2-||x_4||^2,
    V=||x_0||^2-C^2||g_4||^2.                         (3)

Applied to this scalar function, nonnegative V implies

    C(h_j-1)_+ <= (1+P)(1+R), for every j=0,...,3.      (4)

If h_j<=1 the same inequality is automatic. Thus(4) holds on every
zero-step boundary too. Failure of any inequality is a specific legal
negative-V function, hence rules out the full interface(1).

## 3. Prefix bounds and compact capacity

For fixed C>1 define the increasing rational map

    R_C(z)=1+2Cz/(C+z), z>=0,
    r_k=R_C iterated k times at1, k=0,1,2,3.

Rearrangement of(4) gives p_(i+1)<=R_C(p_i). Conversely this inequality
is equivalent to(4) on the nonnegative schedule domain. Induction yields
p_i<=r_i. The cut constraints are invariant under reversing the four
steps, regardless of whether the original certificate admits reversal.
Applying the same induction to the reversed cut inequalities gives

    p_i >= C+1-r_(4-i).                               (5)

Only the cases i=1,2,3 are needed; r_0=1. Explicitly,

    r_1=(3C+1)/(C+1),
    r_2=(7C^2+6C+1)/(C^2+4C+1),
    r_3=(15C^3+23C^2+9C+1)/(C^3+11C^2+7C+1).          (6)

Taking i=2 in(5) and p_2<=r_2 gives C+1<=2r_2, hence

    C^3-9C^2-7C-1<=0.                                (7)

This polynomial is strictly increasing for C>=7: its derivative
3C^2-18C-7 is positive there and increasing. At C=39/4 its value is
131/64>0. Since C>=193/25>7, we conclude

    193/25 <= C < 39/4.                               (8)

In particular this argument covers all larger candidate sums; it did not
replace the target by the slice sum h=168/25.

## 4. All four steps are uniformly positive and bounded

The first and last cut inequalities give

    h_0,h_3 <= 2C/(C+1) < 78/43.                      (9)

For the lower bounds, subtract the upper prefix bound from the lower
bound for the next prefix in(5). This gives

    h_0,h_3 >= C-r_3 = N(C)/(C^3+11C^2+7C+1),
    h_1,h_2 >= C+1-r_1-r_2 = N(C)/((C+1)(C^2+4C+1)), (10)
    N(C)=C^4-4C^3-16C^2-8C-1.

The endpoint lower bound exceeds1/2 exactly when

    A(C)=2C^4-9C^3-43C^2-23C-3>0.

The middle lower bound exceeds4/5 exactly when

    B(C)=5C^4-24C^3-100C^2-60C-9>0.

At C=77/10 these polynomials respectively equal1922412/10000 and
2197285/10000. They are strictly increasing for C>=7: their derivatives
are bounded below respectively by29C^2-86C-23 and68C^2-200C-60,
both positive and increasing on that interval. Since193/25>77/10,

    h_0,h_3>1/2, h_1,h_2>4/5.                         (11)

For completeness, the middle upper bound uses the same cuts, without
assuming either middle step exceeds1. Write

    G_C(z)=R_C(z)-z=1+z(C-z)/(C+z).

For1<=z<=r_1<3 its derivative is
(C^2-2Cz-z^2)/(C+z)^2>0: its numerator is at least C^2-6C-9,
which is positive for C>=15/2. Thus h_1<=G_C(p_1)<=G_C(r_1), and
the reversed cut constraints give the same bound for h_2. Calculation yields

    G_C(r_1)=4C^3/((C+1)(C^2+4C+1)).                  (12)

The right side is strictly increasing in C>0: its logarithmic derivative
has positive numerator5C^2+10C+3 after multiplication by positive
denominators. At39/4 its value is237276/92923<13/5, with the difference
21619/464615. Consequently

    1/2<h_0,h_3<78/43, 4/5<h_1,h_2<13/5.             (13)

This excludes every zero-step boundary for this threshold by actual
witnesses. It does not assert h_i>1, ordering, or reversal of certificates.

## 5. Full multiplier compactness from the common-gradient kernel

This step bounds all20 original multipliers, including every cycle.
The vector p=Te has entries p_i. Since

    sum_i D_ii=-s^2-sum_(i<4)h_i^2,
    omega^T p=s^2+sum_(i<4)h_i^2,

equations(1) give e^T M e=0. Positive semidefiniteness implies Me=0.
For clarity this implication follows by inserting e+t v into the quadratic
form: a nonzero linear coefficient would violate nonnegativity for one
sign of sufficiently small t. Calculation of each component gives

    2De+T^T omega=-diag(p)omega,
    Lp=diag(p)omega.                                  (14)

Define A_ij=Lambda_ij p_j for i!=j and A_ii=0. Since omega_i=out_i-in_i,
equation(14) says sum_j Lambda_ij p_j=p_i in_i. Therefore

    sum_j A_ij=sum_j A_ji for every i.                 (15)

Thus A is a nonnegative circulation. Write c_i=1/p_i. Substituting
Lambda_ij=A_ij c_j into the original value flow and using(15) gives

    sum_i c_i omega_i
      =sum_(i,j) A_ij(c_i c_j-c_i^2)
      =-1/2 sum_(i,j)A_ij(c_i-c_j)^2.

Equivalently, the entire circulation has the fixed Dirichlet energy

    sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij (p_i-p_j)^2/(p_i^2 p_j)
       =4[sum_(i<4)h_i/p_i-s/C] <4s<35.              (16)

All steps exceed1/2 by(13). Consequently |p_i-p_j|>1/2 for i!=j.
Also every p_i<10 by(8), so p_i^2 p_j<1000. Every coefficient of
Lambda_ij on the left of(16) exceeds1/4000. Hence

    sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij < 140000.                      (17)

This is a bound on the complete multiplier vector, not a choice of sparse
support. No skew freedom or PSD remainder is fixed. In particular all
possible solutions of(1) at the requested threshold belong to the closed
bounded box obtained by replacing the strict inequalities in(8),(13),(17)
by non-strict ones. The equations and PSD conditions are continuous, so
their solution set in this box is compact (possibly empty).

## 6. A suffix witness and a full two-coordinate mixed witness

The first new constraint is

    C |(1-h_2)(1-h_3)| <= 1+h_0+h_1.                  (18)

To prove it, take the1-smooth convex function with derivative min(x,1),
whose values are x^2/2 for x<=1 and x-1/2 for x>=1. Start at
x_0=1+h_0+h_1. The first two gradients are1, x_2=1, x_3=1-h_2<=1,
and x_4=(1-h_2)(1-h_3). Every sample satisfies f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2.
Without imposing x_4=g_4, the same telescoping calculation as(3) gives

    V=||x_0||^2-||x_4+s g_4||^2.                     (19)

If x_4<=1, then g_4=x_4 and(19) is precisely(18). If x_4>1,
g_4=1 and x_4+s>C>x_0, since h_2+h_3>0 by(13), giving V<0.
This latter case is excluded, proving(18) for every candidate.

Here is a second necessary inequality with its complete interpolation
proof. Suppose u=h_0>1, b=h_1>1, put a=u-1,A=1+u,
R=h_2+h_3,D_tail=1+R, eta=(b-1)/D_tail, and assume a*eta<1.
Then every feasible seed satisfies

    H_mix(u,C,eta)=u^2+u-1+u(2-u^2)eta
                   -(u-1)C^2 eta^2 >=0.             (20)

Take the base cut data from Section2 with j=1. Its gradients are1
at i=0,1 and-eta at i=2,3,4; its initial point is A and its final
point and gradient are-eta. Its proximal points y_i=x_i-g_i are
y_0=u,y_1=0 and y_i=-eta*sum_(t=i)^3 h_t for i>=2.
Its energy is F_base=A^2-C^2 eta^2.

Define auxiliary scalar data by x_0=g_0=1, x_1=g_1=-a,
g_i=a*eta for i>=2, and actual GD recursion for x_i. Thus
x_2=a(b-1)=a*eta*D_tail, x_4=g_4=a*eta; all earlier auxiliary
proximal points are0 and y_i=a*eta*sum_(t=i)^3 h_t for i>=2.
Assign f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2, and include the zero optimum jet.

For any data with this value assignment, direct expansion gives
Q_ij=(g_i-g_j)y_j. Optimum residuals are Q_(i,star)=0 and
Q_(star,i)=-g_i y_i. All auxiliary optimum residuals are nonpositive.
The only positive auxiliary finite residuals are, for j=2,3,

    Q_aux,(0,j)=(1-a*eta)a*eta*sum_(t=j)^3 h_t.

Every other finite residual is nonpositive: target j=0,1,4 has y_j=0;
two post-cut gradients agree; the pre-cut gradient-a is smaller than
a*eta. The corresponding base residuals are

    Q_base,(0,j)=-(1+eta)eta*sum_(t=j)^3 h_t.

With epsilon=(1+eta)/(a(1-a*eta))>0, take the orthogonal sum of the
base and sqrt(epsilon) times the auxiliary positions and gradients,
and the sum of their function values with weight epsilon. All Q add;
these two possible positive residuals cancel, and every other one remains
nonpositive. The full finite array, including the optimum, is realized
by one globally convex1-smooth function by fact1252b8823bb5a290.
Its energy is

    F_base+epsilon(1-C^2 a^2 eta^2)
       = u*H_mix(u,C,eta)/(a(1-a*eta)).                (21)

This equality follows by expanding both sides and using
1-a^2 A^2=u^2(2-u^2). All denominators are positive, proving(20).
Every original directed interpolation pair is included in this argument.

## 7. Both middle steps are strictly greater than one

For this section write a=h_0-1,b=h_1-1,c=h_2-1,d=h_3-1.
The previously proved bounds imply

    a,d<9/11; b,c<14/9; a+b+c+d=C-5>=68/25.           (22)

Indeed78/43<20/11 and237276/92923<23/9; the latter difference is
1745/836307. These bounds allow negative a,b,c,d initially.

First suppose h_1<=1, so b<=0. Then

    c+d >=68/25-a-b >68/25-9/11=523/275.

As c,d<14/9 and523/275>14/9, both c and d are positive.
The identity (14/9-c)(14/9-d)>0 implies

    cd > (14/9)(523/275-14/9)=11998/22275.

But(18) requires Ccd<=3+a+b<42/11. This is impossible, since

    (193/25)(11998/22275)-42/11=189364/556875>0.

Hence h_1>1.

Next suppose h_2<=1, so c<=0. From(22),
b>=68/25-a-d>68/25-18/11>0, so the second-step cut is available.
Its inequality is

    Cb <= (2+a)(3+c+d).

If a<=3/5, its left side is at least
(193/25)(68/25-3/5-9/11), while its right side is at most
(2+3/5)(3+9/11). The first exceeds the second by844/6875>0.
Thus a>3/5 and u=h_0 lies in(8/5,20/11).

Let C_0=193/25,D_0=42/11 and

    eta=b/(3+c+d)=(C-1-u)/(3+c+d)-1,
    eta_0(u)=(C_0-1-u)/D_0-1.

The denominator3+c+d is positive and at most D_0, while C-1-u>0.
Therefore eta>=eta_0(u)>0 on this interval. The cut itself yields

    a*eta <= a(1+u)/C=(u^2-1)/C<1.

Consequently the mixed necessary inequality(20) applies. Since
u>=8/5>sqrt(2), its coefficient u(2-u^2) is negative and u-1>0.
H_mix decreases as eta>0 increases and as C>0 increases. Thus

    0 <= H_mix(u,C,eta) <= H_mix(u,C_0,eta_0(u)).       (23)

The final expression is a rational quartic in u. Put
u=8/5+(20/11-8/5)z for0<=z<=1, and define
B_(4,k)(z)=binomial(4,k)z^k(1-z)^(4-k). Direct multiplication of the
displayed definition of H_mix gives

    H_mix(u,C_0,eta_0(u))=sum_(k=0)^4 beta_k B_(4,k)(z),

where the exact coefficients in increasing k are

    -124646528/95703125,
    -3776352043/3158203125,
    -1682896352/1654296875,
    -98764097577/127380859375,
    -36497765912/76428515625.

All are strictly negative. The Bernstein basis terms are nonnegative
and sum to1; hence this quartic is strictly negative throughout the
whole interval, contradicting(23). This proves

    h_1>1 and h_2>1.                                  (24)

No conclusion h_0>1 or h_3>1 has been assumed or obtained here.
The fixed quartic expansion is small exact algebra, not a numerical
parameter scan or a solver certificate.

## 8. General cut/quadratic repair and two additional tests

The mixed construction has a useful full-pair version at either middle
cut, retaining the entire quadratic prefix. Fix j in{1,2}, let h_j>1,
P=sum_(i<j)h_i,R=sum_(i>j)h_i,eta=(h_j-1)/(1+R), and define

    z_0=1,z_i=product_(t<i)(1-h_t) for1<=i<=j,
    q=-eta*z_j,
    beta=max(0,max_(0<=i<=j) q(z_i-q)).

Then another necessary inequality is

    beta*((1+P)^2-C^2 eta^2)
       +(1+eta)eta*(1-C^2 q^2) >=0.                  (25)

Here is a joint derivation, including beta=0. Use the cut function of
Section2 as the base. For auxiliary data set x_i=g_i=z_i for i<=j,
set g_i=q for i>j, and follow GD. The first post-cut point is
x_(j+1)=q(1+R), and x_4=q. All auxiliary proximal points before or
at j are0; later they equal q times the remaining step sum. Assign
f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2 and include the zero optimum. The optimum residuals
are nonpositive. A possible positive finite residual has source i<=j
and target k>j and equals q(z_i-q) times that remaining step sum.
The corresponding base residual is-(1+eta)eta times that sum.

If beta>0, multiply the auxiliary Gram/values by
epsilon=(1+eta)eta/beta and orthogonally add to the base. Every original
residual is nonpositive; fact1252b8823bb5a290 gives one legal function.
Its energy is the left side of(25) divided by beta, proving the inequality.
If beta=0 the auxiliary data alone are legal, and their energy is
1-C^2 q^2. This proves(25) in this case too, without division by zero.
No Gram from a different schedule was reused.

Full-curvature quadratic data give the additional necessary condition

    C*|product_(i=0)^3(1-h_i)| <=1.                   (26)

This is the actual function f(x)=x^2/2 started at1; equation(3) applies.

There is also a nonlinear scalar condition that is stronger than merely
imposing(26). Since both middle steps exceed1 by Section7, put
q_mid=(h_1-1)(h_2-1)>0. Then

    C*|q_mid-h_3*min(q_mid,1)| <=1+h_0.              (27)

Use again the function with derivative min(x,1), this time started at
x_0=1+h_0. Its first step reaches1; the next two points are
x_2=1-h_1 and x_3=q_mid. Thus x_4=q_mid-h_3*min(q_mid,1).
All values have the saturated form, so(19) holds. If x_4>1 then
x_4+s>C>1+h_0 and V<0. Otherwise g_4=x_4 and(19) gives(27).
The inequality in(27) itself forces |x_4|<1, because 1+h_0<C.

The cuts yield a quantitative endpoint-sum constraint as well. Put
A=1+h_0,D_end=1+h_3,E_end=A+D_end and b=h_1-1,c=h_2-1.
Rearranging the cuts at1 and2 gives

    b<=A(C-A)/(C+A), c<=D_end(C-D_end)/(C+D_end).

The function z(C-z)/(C+z) has second derivative-4C^2/(C+z)^3<0.
Adding these two inequalities and using concavity at their midpoint yields

    C-E_end-1=b+c<=E_end*(2C-E_end)/(2C+E_end),
    E_end >= 2C(C-1)/(3C+1).                         (28)

In particular at least one endpoint exceeds1. Indeed if both were at
most1 then E_end<=4, and(28) would imply C^2-7C-2<=0. That polynomial
is positive at193/25 (value2224/625) and strictly increasing thereafter.
Thus a remaining candidate has three or four steps>1; the possible step
at most1 can only be one endpoint. No endpoint has been deleted.

## 9. A sharper capacity cap from cut products and the actual quadratic

The preceding constraints improve the capacity bound further to

    193/25<=C<87/10.                                 (29)

For a contradiction suppose C>=87/10. Define the following scalar
functions (distinct from the matrix and interpolation notation):

    d_cap(C)=(C+1)(C^2+4C+1),
    r_cap(C)=(C-1)/(C+1),
    t_cap(C)=4C^3/d_cap(C)-1,
    ell_cap(C)=2C(C-1)/(3C+1)-4,
    k_cap(C)=C-5-2r_cap(C).

Write a=h_0-1,b=h_1-1,c=h_2-1,d=h_3-1 as before. Equations(9),(12),(28)
give a,d<=r_cap, b,c<=t_cap, a+d>=ell_cap, and b+c>=k_cap.
Multiplication of the nonnegative factors r_cap-a,r_cap-d and
t_cap-b,t_cap-c yields

    ad>=r_cap*(ell_cap-r_cap),
    bc>=t_cap*(k_cap-t_cap).                         (30)

All four factors on the right are positive and increasing for C>=87/10.
Here are detailed sign bounds. Clearly r_cap>0,r_cap'>0 there, and
t_cap'>0 follows from

    t_cap'=4C^2(5C^2+10C+3)/d_cap(C)^2.

For C>=8, d_cap(C)>C^3 gives t_cap'<403/1024, while
k_cap'=1-4/(C+1)^2>=77/81; hence k_cap-t_cap is strictly increasing.
Also

    ell_cap'=(6C^2+4C-2)/(3C+1)^2>384/625,
    r_cap'=2/(C+1)^2<=2/81

for C>=8, proving ell_cap-r_cap is strictly increasing. The first lower
bound uses3C+1<=(25/8)C and6C^2+4C-2>6C^2. At C=87/10 the exact
values of r_cap,t_cap,ell_cap-r_cap,k_cap-t_cap are respectively

    77/97,
    1552559/1081453,
    19728/131435,
    7318711/10814530,

which are all positive. Positivity in(30) also forces a,d,b,c positive:
their pairwise sums are positive, and their pairwise products are positive.
Consequently the full-curvature constraint(26) requires C*ad*bc<=1.
But the increasing lower bound from(30), evaluated at87/10, exceeds1 by

    9782467331765167/1375525927835685125>0.

This proves(29) for every larger sum, without assuming saturation in(26).

The monotonic formulas(9),(12) now refine the step upper bounds to

    1/2<h_0,h_3<174/97,
    1<h_1,h_2<2634012/1081453.                        (31)

Finally p_i<9 and4s<154/5 in the exact circulation identity(16). Its
coefficients still exceed1/(4*9^3)=1/2916, so

    sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij<449064/5<90000.               (32)

Thus(29),(31),(32) supersede the earlier coarse compact box; the sharper
pointwise rational cut bounds remain available inside that box.

## 10. Why the clipped three-step test cannot be omitted

An exact scalar example separates(27) from the preceding cuts, the
full-curvature test, the suffix pair test, and the two middle-cut repairs.
It is an explicitly constructed counterexample to the sufficiency of
that smaller necessary-condition list, not a candidate seed.

Take

    h=(1,217/100,41/20,3/2), s=168/25, C=193/25.

All four cuts(4) hold. At j=0 the left side is0. At j=1 the two sides
are22581/2500 and91/10; at j=2 they are4053/500 and417/40;
at j=3 they are193/50 and311/50. The suffix pair constraint(18)
has left side4053/1000 and right side417/100. The full-curvature
product in(26) vanishes. At both middle cuts the auxiliary prefix
product z_j is zero since h_0=1, so q=beta=0 and(25) is positive.
The first mixed test(20) requires h_0>1 and does not apply here.

Nevertheless the legal function with derivative min(x,1), started at2,
has the exact trajectory

    x=(2,1,-117/100,2457/2000,-543/2000),
    g=(1,1,-117/100,1,-543/2000).

Its energy equals

    V=4-(104799/50000)^2<0,

because104799>100000. This is precisely a violation of(27), arising
from the return to the affine part at x_3>1. It demonstrates the need
to retain nonlinear chronological transitions even after compactness,
original coefficient signs, and full-curvature screening are imposed.

## 11. Adjacent triangle witness, rederived in this joint argument

The shared UNVERIFIED C80 finding7b447329aac48edd suggested this construction.
It is awareness only: the geometry, every interpolation normal, and the
energy identity needed here are rederived in this section. The only fact
used for realization is1252b8823bb5a290.

Write h=(a,b,c,d), where b,c>1 and a,d>=0. Define

    P=a+1, B=b-1, E=c-1, D=d+1,
    U=b+c+d, C=P+U.

First suppose D>B*E and set

    Kappa=B^2*E+U*(D-B*E), t=B^2*E/Kappa,
    u=(1,0), w=(t,sqrt(t(1-t))), v=-(B*u+D*w)/c.

Kappa>0 and0<t<1. The convex hull of u,v,w contains0, since
B*u+c*v+D*w=0 with all three coefficients positive. Set

    x=(P*u,u,-B*u,D*w,w), g=(u,u,v,w,w),
    f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2,

and include the zero optimum jet. The four GD identities follow from
c*v=-B*u-D*w and D=d+1. Direct inner products give

    ||u||^2=1, <u,w>=||w||^2=t,
    <u,v>=-(B+D*t)/c, <v,w>=-(B+D)*t/c.

All sampled interpolation inequalities follow by checking that x_i-g_i
is an outward normal to this triangle at g_i. At i=0 that normal is
a*u, with nonpositive pairing with v-u and w-u. At i=1,4 it is zero.
At i=2 put y=-B*u-v. Expanding the displayed inner products gives

    <w-v,y>=(-B^2*E+Kappa*t)/c^2=0,
    <u-v,y>=-B*(1-t)*E/c<0.

For the first expansion, the coefficient of t is
D(2B+D)+c(D-BD+B)-B*c^2, which equals Kappa after substituting
E=c-1,U=B+c+D. For the second expansion the difference between the
two sides, multiplied by c^2, is-B^2*E+Kappa*t=0. At i=3 the normal
d*w pairs with u-w to zero and with v-w to
-d*t*(1+(B+D)/c)<=0. This exhausts the vertex comparisons. As0 is in
their convex hull, it also exhausts the optimum comparisons.

Indeed for these assigned values Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,x_j-g_j>, so all the
original ordered Q are nonpositive, Q_(i,star)=0, and Q_(star,i)<=0.
Fact1252b8823bb5a290 realizes one global convex1-smooth function with
a minimizer at0 and precisely these GD data. The terminal x_4=g_4=w,
so(3) gives V=P^2-C^2*t. A direct expansion gives

    C^2 B^2 E-P^2 Kappa
       =U*{B*E[P^2+B(P+C)]-P^2 D}.

Consequently feasible full certificates must satisfy

    B*E*[1+B(P+C)/P^2] <= D.                         (34)

This condition applies without imposing D>B*E in advance. If instead
D<=B*E, the legal function with derivative min(x,1), started at P,
has x=(P,1,-B,B*E,B*E-D+1) and g=(1,1,-B,1,1). Its final point
is at least1, and(19) gives

    V=P^2-[P+(B+1)(E+1)]^2<0.

Thus the complementary regime is impossible, proving(34) for every
candidate with b,c>1. The inequality itself implies D>B*E. No previous
word's Gram, an assumed primitive subword, or a numerical proposal has
been adopted in this argument.

The same construction proves(34) at any adjacent pair of steps>1, with
P=1+the sum of all earlier steps, D=1+the sum of all later steps,
B=the first selected step minus1, E=the second selected step minus1,
and C=1+the sum of the whole word. Before the selected pair every
gradient is u and each iterate is(1+the remaining prefix sum)u; its
proximal point is a nonnegative multiple of u, so the same two vertex
normal comparisons hold. After the selected pair every gradient is w
and each iterate is(1+the remaining suffix sum)w, with the same
nonnegative-multiple normal argument. All original intermediate samples
and all directed Q are thus retained. The initial point is P*u and the
terminal point is w, giving the same energy and(34). This paragraph
justifies applying the construction to the first pair as well as the
middle pair of the four-step word, without assuming either unselected
block is primitive.

## 12. The first endpoint also exceeds one

Suppose for a contradiction h_0<=1. The endpoint-sum consequence(28)
forces h_3>1. Since h_0-1<=0 and h_3-1<=r_cap(C), the same consequence
forces ell_cap(C)<=r_cap(C), or2C^3-15C^2-16C-3<=0. This polynomial
equals11/2 at17/2 and its derivative is positive for C>=7. Therefore
C<17/2. In the notation of Section11,

    1<P<=2, 2<D<3, B=h_1-1>0, E=h_2-1>0.

Rearranging the second-step cut and using monotonicity in P<=2 gives

    B<=P(C-P)/(C+P)<=2(C-2)/(C+2)<26/21.             (35)

The derivative with respect to P of P(C-P)/(C+P) has numerator
C^2-2CP-P^2>0 on C>=193/25,P<=2; the last expression increases in C.
Also(31) gives E<1552559/1081453<23/16, with the latter positive
difference32475/17303248. Thus both B and E are less than23/16.

We first show D<13/5. The suffix-pair inequality(18) becomes

    E <= (C-D)/(1+C(D-2)),                            (36)

since P+1+B=C-D-E and D>2. By the capacity identity and(35),

    E=C-P-D-1-B >= H(C)-D-1,
    H(C)=C(C-2)/(C+2).

Therefore feasibility requires

    F_sep(C,D)=H(C)-D-1-(C-D)/(1+C(D-2))<=0.           (37)

For C>=193/25 and13/5<=D<=3, this separation expression strictly
increases with C. Indeed H'=1-8/(C+2)^2>73/81, while the derivative
of the final rational term with respect to C is
(D-1)^2/[1+C(D-2)]^2<=25/169; their difference is positive.
For C_0=193/25 the expression is concave in D: its second derivative
is-2C_0(C_0-1)^2/[1+C_0(D-2)]^3<0. Its endpoint values are

    F_sep(C_0,13/5)=2269/66825>0,
    F_sep(C_0,3)=1166/662175>0.

Concavity puts its value above the chord joining these positive values.
It is therefore positive throughout this interval, contradicting(37).
This proves D<13/5.

Now B+E=C-P-D-1>53/25. Since B,E<23/16, multiplication of
(23/16-B)(23/16-E)>0 yields

    B*E>6279/6400, B>273/400.

For P<=2 and C>=193/25, (P+C)/P^2>=243/100; the expression decreases
with P>0 and increases with C. Consequently

    B*E*[1+B(P+C)/P^2]
       >(6279/6400)*(106339/40000)
       =13/5+2102581/256000000 >D,

contradicting the full triangle condition(34). Hence

    h_0>1.                                           (38)

Together with Section7, the first THREE steps exceed1. No reversal of
the schedule or symmetry of the certificate was used.

## 13. The last endpoint also exceeds one

Suppose now h_3<=1. Set alpha=h_0-1>0,b=h_1-1>0,c=h_2-1>0,
delta=h_3-1<=0. As in Section12, the endpoint-sum bound gives C<17/2.
The last-middle cut and the capacity identity imply

    c<=2(C-2)/(C+2),
    b=C-5-alpha-delta-c>=L(C,alpha),
    L(C,alpha)=C-7-alpha+8/(C+2).

Apply the adjacent triangle(34) to the first two steps, with prefix

    b<=J(C,alpha),
    J(C,alpha)=(C-2-alpha)/(1+alpha+alpha^2(C+1)).       (39)

We claim alpha<7/10. If7/10<=alpha<77/97<4/5, put
R=1+alpha+alpha^2(C+1) and N=C-2-alpha. On the rectangle

    J_C=[1+alpha+alpha^2(3+alpha)]/R^2<1/5,
    L_C=1-8/(C+2)^2>73/81.

Thus L-J increases with C. It also increases with alpha. Indeed

Hence-J_alpha>1 and(L-J)_alpha>0. Finally the exact lower corner value is

For the middle adjacent pair now put P=1+h_0=2+alpha<27/10,
D=1+h_3<=2, and x=b+c=C-P-D-1. Then

    x>101/50, c<26/21,
    821/1050<b<783/560,
    (P+C)/P^2>=1042/729.                             (40)

The lower bound on b is x-c. For the upper bound use the cut
b<=P(C-P)/(C+P); this function increases in P<=27/10 and C>=193/25

Define the fixed cubic

    F_pair(b)=b*(101/50-b)*(1+(1042/729)b).

Since b<783/560<101/50, (40) implies

    b*c*[1+b(P+C)/P^2]>=F_pair(b).

The second derivative of F_pair is strictly decreasing in b and at

    F_pair(821/1050)-2=211017368/4219543125>0,

Thus the middle-pair left side in(34) is greater than2>=D, a contradiction.
This proves h_3>1. All four original steps therefore satisfy

    1<h_0,h_3<174/97,
    1<h_1,h_2<2634012/1081453.                        (41)

This conclusion is derived; it was not assumed during the search-domain

All prefix differences |p_i-p_j| now exceed1. Since p_i<9 and4s<154/5,

    sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij <112266/5<22500.              (42)

The necessary cut domain is not asserted sufficient. The full original

    h=(1+alpha,1+b,1+c,1+delta),

together with both repaired-cut inequalities(25), the triangle

For each remaining schedule all20 multipliers remain nonnegative with

Neither a feasible seed nor a global separator has been produced.
