# Nested terminal triangles exclude the entire small-second-step region

Status: COMPLETE JOINT PROOF DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. Max4 round40, the last
of the two analytical rounds under master_guidance78e668c9782fc528.
Completed ceiling40, attempted ceiling41 including the historical
interruption. No formal gate is allocated. This is not an accepted fact
or a solution of the original exact-profile Goal.

## 1. Statement, scope, and accepted inputs

The regional assertion proved in this draft is:

For every four-step word h=(u,w,z,v) with nonnegative entries,

    u+w+z+v >= 168/25,       w <= 4/3,                 (1)

there is no original primitive certificate with all twenty nonnegative
finite multipliers, exact flow, and a matrix positive semidefinite on
the full five-gradient space. The first, third, and last steps have no
additional prescribed intervals. No ordering, reversal, or coordinate
permutation is used.

If the four old cut conditions and the old terminal-triangle condition
hold, the proof supplies one explicit static function depending on h,
of dimension at most three, whose primitive energy is

    V_h < -961/4375.                                  (2)

This removes the previous restriction 6w+7v<=18 from the round39 draft.
It covers its actual complement within w<=4/3, and does not merely fix
the first step or perturb the former physical box.

Only accepted04219554e41d9750, Section10, and accepted1252b8823bb5a290
are inputs. The first supplies the exact original-array identity; the
second supplies the interpolation orientation. All functions, every
physical update, all directed rows, and all parameter comparisons
needed below are proved jointly here. Neither unverified8d4369993526363e
nor any earlier unverified triangle assertion is a correctness premise.

Audit8d78e0975df8400a and guidance1501c682ec2aff49 have been read. The
original scalar draft43a616da7da451c7 remains defective as printed; its
original file and hash are preserved. No scalar first-increment bound,
global domain reduction, Bernstein table, broad row-redundancy claim,
or whole-box assertion from that package is used here. This document
does not purport to repair or accept that separate scalar package.

## 2. The full original certificate and a realization device

Put s=u+w+z+v and C=1+s. Original finite indices are0,1,2,3,4.
Let Lambda_ij>=0 for all twenty distinct finite pairs and Lambda_ii=0.
Let e_all=(1,1,1,1,1)^T and define

    T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,
    L_flow=diag(Lambda e_all)-Lambda,
    omega=(-2u,-2w,-2z,-2v,2s)^T,
    Dprim=diag(u(2-u),w(2-w),z(2-z),v(2-v),-s(s+2)),
    M=Dprim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2.

The proposed certificate requires L_flow^T e_all=omega and M PSD on
all of R^5. Original gradients at zero steps are not identified or
deleted. For actual positions X_i, gradients G_i, and values F_i set

    Q_ij=F_i-F_j+<G_i,X_j-X_i>+||G_i-G_j||^2/2,
    V_h=2sum_(i<4)h_i(F_i-F_4)
         +sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||G_i||^2-sC||G_4||^2.

The accepted exact identity is

    V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M,Gram(G_0,...,G_4)>.(3)

The inner product of matrices is trace of the transpose times the
second matrix. Every actual smooth convex trajectory has Q_ij<=0.
Thus a negative actual V_h contradicts the entire original certificate.

All witnesses here use saturated values

    F_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2.                       (4)

Their residuals and primitive energy simplify exactly to

    Q_ij=<G_i-G_j,X_j-G_j>,
    V_h=||X_0||^2-||X_4+sG_4||^2.                  (5)

For the second identity, 2sum h_i<X_i,G_i>-sum h_i^2||G_i||^2
telescopes to ||X_0||^2-||X_4||^2 under the original updates; the
remaining terms are -2s<X_4,G_4>-s^2||G_4||^2.

Here is the static realization used below, including the optimum.
Suppose K is the convex hull of the displayed gradients, contains0,
and every source vertex satisfies <G-G_j,X_j-G_j><=0. Define

    F_K(X)=max_(G in K)(<X,G>-||G||^2/2).             (6)

Completing the square identifies the unique maximizer as the Euclidean
projection P_K(X). The normal inequalities imply P_K(X_j)=G_j, so
the function has precisely(4) at each original query. Two projection
inequalities, added, give

    <X-X',P_K(X)-P_K(X')> >= ||P_K(X)-P_K(X')||^2.

Hence P_K is1-Lipschitz. The maximizing inequalities give

    0<=F_K(X')-F_K(X)-<P_K(X),X'-X>
       <=<P_K(X')-P_K(X),X'-X><=||X'-X||^2.

This proves differentiability and gradient P_K. The function is convex
as a maximum of affine functions, and finite because K is compact.
Since0 is in K, F_K>=0=F_K(0). In particular it has a true minimizer.
No uniqueness or normalized objective payoff is asserted. For(4), all
Q_(i,star) vanish; Q_(star,i)=-<G_i,X_i-G_i> will also be checked.

## 3. The older cuts and the terminal triangle, freshly derived

For j in{0,1,2,3}, let Pj=sum_(i<j)h_i and Rj=sum_(i>j)h_i.
Every full certificate obeys

    C(h_j-1)_+ <= (1+Pj)(1+Rj).                     (7)

When h_j>1, put A=1+Pj,B=1+Rj,r=(h_j-1)/B. The function(6) with
K=[-r,1], started at X_0=A, has gradient1 throughout the prefix up
to j. The selected update moves X_j=1 to -Br. The remaining gradients
are all -r, and X_4=G_4=-r. Every original zero step is included.
Equation(5) gives V_h=A^2-C^2r^2, proving(7). When h_j<=1 the cut
is automatic.

Write

    m=1+w, N=u+m, D=1+v, c=z-1, d=v-1.

Then C=N+D+c. In our threshold region, the endpoint cuts give

    u,v <= 2C/(C+1) < 2.                            (8)

If an endpoint is at most1 its bound is automatic; otherwise(8) is
the rearranged corresponding cut. Consequently

    z>C0-19/3=104/75>1,   C0:=193/25,               (9)

using w<=4/3. Thus c>0 is derived, not imposed as a reduction.

When v>1, the terminal adjacent pair also requires cd<1. If cd>=1,
use the one-dimensional function with derivative min(x,1), namely
F(x)=x^2/2 for x<=1 and F(x)=x-1/2 for x>=1. Its initial point N
gives the exact queries

    X=(N,m,1,-c,cd),       G=(1,1,1,-c,1).

All values are(4), the function has minimum0, and(5) gives

    V_h=N^2-(N+zv)^2<0.                             (10)

Now assume v>1 and cd<1. Define

    E=Dd=v^2-1,
    Ktri=D+c(1-E)=c^2 d+(c+D)(1-cd)>0,
    Rtri=c^2 d/Ktri,   t=c/D,
    e=(1,0), r=(Rtri,sqrt(Rtri(1-Rtri))),
    p=-(c e+r)/v,   n=-c e-p,
    Btri=(cd-Rtri)/v.                               (11)

Here0<Rtri<1 and Btri>0: Ktri-c=D(1-cd)>0 implies cd>Rtri.
Direct inner products give

    <e,r>=||r||^2=Rtri,  <e,p>=-(c+Rtri)/v<0,
    <e,n>=-Btri,   <p,n>=<r,n>=t Btri.               (12)

For the last identity, expand p and n and use Ktri Rtri=c^2 d;
equivalently <p,n>=Rtri(1-cd)/v and
Rtri(1-cd)/(cd-Rtri)=c/D=t.

Take the original base trajectory

    Xbase=(N e,m e,e,-c e,r),
    Gbase=(e,e,e,p,r).                               (13)

All physical updates hold because -c e-vp=r. Its nonzero target
normals are (N-1)e at0, w e at1, and n at3. At0 and1 the e-component
of each other vertex is at most1. At3, the comparisons from e and r
are respectively -(1+t)Btri and0 by(12). The other target normals are
zero. This checks all ordered finite rows. Also
c e+v p+r=0, with positive coefficients, so0 belongs to conv{e,p,r}.
The optimum products are N-1,w,0,t Btri,0, all nonnegative.
Thus(6) is one legal static function realizing(13), and(5) yields

    V_h=N^2-C^2 Rtri.

The older terminal-triangle necessity, on its proved domain, is

    cd<1,       C^2 Rtri<=N^2       when v>1.         (14)

Nothing from an unexamined global domain reduction was used.

## 4. The enlarged nested family and every original row

This section is valid for all u>1,w>=0,z>1,v>1 with cd<1, without
assuming a threshold. Put

    a=u-1, q=a/m>0, t=c/D>0,

and use all triangle quantities from(11). Given rho>=0, form vectors
in the direct sum of two Euclidean planes:

    X_0=(N e,sqrt(rho)e),       G_0=(e,sqrt(rho)e),
    X_1=(m e,-sqrt(rho)a e),    G_1=(e,-sqrt(rho)q e),
    X_2=(e,-sqrt(rho)q e),      G_2=(e,-sqrt(rho)q e),
    X_3=(-c e,sqrt(rho)cq e),   G_3=(p,-sqrt(rho)q p),
    X_4=(r,-sqrt(rho)q r),      G_4=(r,-sqrt(rho)q r). (15)

All vectors lie in the sum of the two-dimensional subspace
{(y,-sqrt(rho)q y): y in R^2} and the line generated by(e,sqrt(rho)e).
Thus the function and trajectory can be placed in dimension at most3.
The use of direct-sum coordinates is just explicit notation, not a
restriction on the full original certificate.

The first auxiliary update is1-u=-a, the second is-a+wq=-q because
a=mq, the third is-q+zq=cq, and the fourth iscq e+vq p=-q r.
The base updates were checked in(13). Every step retains its original
length. Assign the values(4).

The complete target normals are

    Y_0=((N-1)e,0), Y_1=(w e,-sqrt(rho)wq e),
    Y_2=0, Y_3=(n,-sqrt(rho)q n), Y_4=0.

Here is the exhaustive finite-row list, grouped by target:

* Target0: sources1,2 give0; source3 gives
  (N-1)(<e,p>-1)<0; source4 gives(N-1)(Rtri-1)<0.
* Target1: source0 gives-rho*w*q*(1+q)<=0; source2 gives0;
  sources3,4 respectively give
  w*(1+rho*q^2)(<e,p>-1)<=0 and
  w*(1+rho*q^2)(Rtri-1)<=0.
* Targets2 and4: every source gives0.
* Target3: sources1,2 give-(1+rho*q^2)(1+t)Btri<0;
  source4 gives0; source0 gives precisely

      Q_03=Btri[-(1+t)+rho*q*(1-qt)].                (16)

Thus all twenty original finite rows hold if and only if

    0<=rho<=(1+t)/(q(1-qt))       when qt<1,
    arbitrary rho>=0             when qt>=1.         (17)

This includes qt=1 and rho=0. Necessity is the single row(16), since
Btri>0. No other pair is deleted. The optimum products <G_i,Y_i> are

    N-1, w(1+rho*q^2), 0, t Btri(1+rho*q^2), 0.

They are nonnegative; hence every optimum row holds too. Moreover
cG_1+vG_3+G_4=0, so the static function(6) for

    K_nested=conv{G_0,G_1,G_3,G_4}                  (18)

has minimum0 and realizes every sample. It is one fixed function for
this word and this amplitude, not a different function at each query.
It is not asserted to be a common function independent of the word.

Its exact energy for every allowed amplitude is

    V_h(rho)=N^2-C^2 Rtri+rho*(1-C^2 q^2 Rtri).     (19)

This is the complete amplitude test for this explicit family on the
stated domain. At qt<1 it suffices and is necessary to test both
endpoints of(17), since(19) is affine. At qt>=1 it is equivalent to
N^2-C^2 Rtri>=0 and1-C^2 q^2 Rtri>=0. These equivalences concern
this family alone, not all legal functions or all certificates.

For qt<1 choose the upper endpoint in(17), and define

    k=(qN^2-1/q)/(1+q)
      =u*(N-m/(u-1))=u^2+m*u-m-m/(u-1).

Expanding(19) after multiplication by1-qt gives

    V_h=(1+q)/(1-qt) * Gtri,
    Gtri=N^2-k*(1+t)-C^2 Rtri.                      (20)

Indeed the coefficient of C^2 Rtri becomes-(1+q), and the remaining
numerator is (1+q)N^2-(1+t)(qN^2-1/q).

## 5. The planar member needed below v=sqrt(2)

For u>1,w>=0,z>1,v>=0, put q=(u-1)/m,t=c/D and use scalar base data

    Xbase=(N,m,1,-c,-t), Gbase=(1,1,1,-t,-t),
    Xaux=(1,-a,-q,cq,tq), Gaux=(1,-q,-q,tq,tq),
    X_i=(Xbase_i,sqrt(rho)Xaux_i),
    G_i=(Gbase_i,sqrt(rho)Gaux_i).

The four updates are N-u=m,m-w=1,1-z=-c,-c+vt=-t, and
1-u=-a,-a+wq=-q,-q+zq=cq,cq-vtq=tq. Use(4).
The base normals are(N-1,w,0,-vt,0), and the auxiliary normals are
(0,-wq,0,vtq,0). The complete finite rows are:

* Target0: sources1,2 give0 and sources3,4 give-(1+t)(N-1).
* Target1: source0 gives-rho*w*q*(1+q); source2 gives0;
  sources3,4 give-w*(1+t)(1+rho*q^2).
* Targets2,4: all sources give0.
* Target3: sources1,2 give-vt*(1+t)(1+rho*q^2); source4 gives0;
  source0 gives vt[-(1+t)+rho*q*(1-tq)].

All optimum products are N-1,w(1+rho*q^2),0,
vt^2(1+rho*q^2),0. Also G_4=-tG_1, so0 is in the hull of
G_0,G_1,G_4. The function(6) realizes these data.

The full allowed amplitude is(17) when v>0 and tq<1, and arbitrary
rho>=0 when v=0 or tq>=1. In particular, whenever0<tq<1 the capped
rho=(1+t)/(q(1-tq)) is legal even at v=0. At that amplitude(5) gives

    V_h=(1+q)/(1-tq) * Gcut,
    Gcut=N^2-k*(1+t)-C^2 t^2.                       (21)

This is rederived here; no fixed trajectory is extrapolated across
an unchecked phase.

## 6. Two elementary tail formulas

Put lambda=1+sqrt(2), so lambda(lambda-2)=1, and let L=C-N denote
the sum c+D in this section only. At fixed D and c=L-D, write

    R(L,D)=c^2(D-2)/[D+c(1-D(D-2))].                (22)

For D>=lambda and cd<1 its denominator is positive. With
E=D(D-2)>=1 and chi=1-(E-1)t in(0,1], equation(22) reads

    R=t^2 E/chi,
    R_c=E*t*(1+chi)/(D*chi^2) >= 2t/D >0.           (23)

For the inequality, (1+chi)/chi^2>=2 follows from
1+chi-2chi^2=(1-chi)(1+2chi)>=0. In the planar case Rcut=t^2,
the corresponding derivative is exactly2t/D.

For any fixed0<rbar<1, the inequality R(L,D)>rbar is equivalent,
on a positive denominator, to

    F_rbar(D):=(D-2)(L-D)(L-(1-rbar)D)-rbar*L >0.   (24)

If L>=18/5 and lambda<=D<=14/5, its second derivative satisfies

    F_rbar''=6(1-rbar)D-2L(2-rbar)-4(1-rbar)
        <=[64(1-rbar)-36(2-rbar)]/5
         =-(8+28rbar)/5<0.                         (25)

Therefore positive endpoint values imply positivity throughout the
closed interval, by concavity. This avoids assuming R is monotone
in D; it need not be. At D=lambda, its exact value is

    R(L,lambda)=(L-lambda)^2/lambda^2.               (26)

## 7. Every possible old survivor in(1) enters the proved phase

Assume all cuts(7) and the terminal condition(14) hold. First v<9/5.
Indeed if v>=9/5, the last cut implies C>=v/(2-v)>=9. By(8),
N<13/3 and D<3, so c=C-N-D>5/3 and d>=4/5. Thus cd>4/3,
contradicting(14). This used no capacity upper bound from elsewhere.

Next N>4, so u=N-m>5/3. If v<=sqrt(2), the third cut gives

    N>=C(C-D)/(C+D).

For C>=C0,D<=lambda<17/7, this is greater than
C0(C0-17/7)/(C0+17/7)=89359/22200>4. The monotonicities follow from
partial_C[C(C-D)/(C+D)]=1-2D^2/(C+D)^2>0 for C>D, and
partial_D=-2C^2/(C+D)^2<0.

For sqrt(2)<=v<9/5, suppose instead N<=4. Then L=C-N>=Lstar:=93/25.
Use(22) at the virtual Lstar. Its denominator stays positive because

    (Lstar-D)(D-2) <= (Lstar-2)^2/4
                  =1849/2500<1.

The monotonicity R_c>0 in(23), with the actual cd<1, gives
R(L,D)>=R(Lstar,D). Take rstar=16/C0^2, which lies in(0,1/3).
At D=lambda, using lambda<17/7,

    C0*(Lstar/lambda-1)
       >C0*(Lstar/(17/7)-1)=43618/10625>4.

Thus R(Lstar,lambda)>rstar. At D=14/5, direct substitution gives

    R(Lstar,14/5)=2116/5185>1/3>rstar.

Equations(24),(25) with L=Lstar show R(Lstar,D)>rstar for the whole
interval. Consequently C^2 R(L,D)>16>=N^2, contradicting(14).
This proves N>4 also on the higher-terminal branch.

We have therefore derived, for every old survivor in(1),

    5/3<u<2, 1<=m<=7/3, c>0,
    q=(u-1)/m in(0,1), 0<t<=N/C<1.                (27)

The last inequality is the third cut. Thus qt<1. All zero and small
step cases were handled before choosing a family; none was discarded.
For v<=sqrt(2) use the planar function in Section5. For v>=sqrt(2)
use the nested triangle in Section4, whose cd<1 was proved above.

## 8. Reduce the whole region to fixed first step and prefix mass

On the bounding rectangle5/3<u<2,1<=m<=7/3, the coefficient k obeys

    k_m=u-1-1/(u-1)<0,
    k_u=2u+m+m/(u-1)^2>0,
    k>=k(5/3,7/3)=5/6>0.                           (28)

Both energies have the form

    G=N^2-k*(C-N)/D-C^2 R,

where R=t^2 in the planar case and R is(22) in the triangle case.
Where c>0 and the triangle denominator, if present, is positive,

    G_C=-k/D-2C R-C^2 R_c<0,
    G_m=2N-k_m*(1+t)+k/D+C^2 R_c>0.                (29)

For the planar case additionally

    G_D=(k+2C^2 t)*(C-N)/D^2>0.                    (30)

In the planar branch increase D to lambda using(30). In either branch
increase m to7/3 and decrease C to

    Cmin(u)=max(C0,u/(2-u)),

which is permitted by the original first cut. These comparisons only
increase G. They need not preserve every other old predicate, since
they are comparisons of the explicit expressions, not new candidate
certificates. Their necessary sign conditions do hold: c stays positive,
since C0-2-7/3-14/5=44/75>0; k stays positive by(28). In the triangle
case m increasing and C decreasing only decrease c and hence preserve
cd<1 and positivity of the denominator.

We are left with m=7/3 and lambda<=D<=14/5; the planar case has just
D=lambda. For each such D the maximum over u is at

    U0=2C0/(C0+1)=193/109.                          (31)

To prove this, first take5/3<u<=U0. Here Cmin=C0, N>4, U0<9/5, and

    k_u<18/5+7/3+21/4=671/60<45/4,
    7/25<t<3/5,
    C0^2>59.

For the lower t bound, c>59/75 gives t>59/210>7/25. For the upper
bound use N>4 and lambda>12/5, giving t<(93/25)/(12/5)-1=11/20.
By(23) or its planar equality,

    G_u=2N-k_u*(1+t)+k/D+C0^2 R_c
        >8-18+59/5=9/5>0.                          (32)

Next take U0<=u<2 with Cmin=C_u=u/(2-u). Since U0>7/4,
C_u'=2/(2-u)^2>32. Let L0=C0-U0-7/3. It exceeds18/5. Along this
branch c=C_u-u-7/3-D increases with u. Therefore

    t>2/7, R_c>=2t/D>10/49, C_u^2 R_c>10.

The total derivative is

    2N-k_u*(1+t)-k*(C_u'-1)/D
        -2C_u C_u'R-C_u^2 R_c*(C_u'-1)
      <26/3-310<0.                                 (33)

This proves(31). All comparison paths in u toward U0 decrease c:
below U0, increase u at fixed C0; above U0, decrease u with C=C_u.
Thus a triangle denominator that was positive remains positive. No
unproved phase or physical reversal is used in the reduction.

## 9. Uniform terminal comparison at the remaining corner

At u=U0,m=7/3,C=C0 the exact constants are

    N0=1342/327<411/100,
    L0=C0-N0=29561/8175,
    723/200<L0<11/3, in particular L0>18/5,
    k0=815039/427716>19/10.                          (34)

For example N0-m/(U0-1)=1342/327-109/36=4223/3924,
which gives the displayed k0 after multiplication by193/109.

We claim, uniformly for lambda<=D<=14/5, that

    C0^2 R(L0,D) > (383/100)^2.                     (35)

Its denominator is positive throughout, since
(L0-D)(D-2)<(11/3-2)^2/4=25/36<1. Put
rbar=(383/772)^2; then0<rbar<1/4 and C0=772/100.
At D=lambda, use sqrt(2)<99/70, hence lambda<169/70, and(34):

    C0*(L0/lambda-1)
      >(193/25)*[(723/200)/(169/70)-1]
       =324433/84500 > 383/100.

At D=14/5, c=L0-D>4/5 and

    Ktri=14/5-(31/25)c<226/125,
    c^2 d>64/125,
    R(L0,14/5)>32/113>1/4>rbar.

Thus(24),(25) prove(35) over the entire interval. The planar energy
at D=lambda has exactly the same R by(26). Combining(29)--(35)
now gives for either branch

    G < (411/100)^2
         -(19/10)*(18/5)/(14/5)
         -(383/100)^2
      = 2779/1250-171/70
      = -961/4375.                                  (36)

Every quantity bounded below in the subtracted products is positive.
The multiplier in(20) or(21) is(1+q)/(1-qt)>1 at the original word.
Hence its actual static witness satisfies(2).

## 10. Conclusion, exact comparison scope, and remaining task

If any old cut fails, Section3 gives an actual scalar V_h<0. If the
terminal condition fails at v>1, its scalar or planar function in
Section3 gives V_h<0. Otherwise Sections7--9 apply the explicitly
legal nested family and give the strict uniform margin(2). In every
case(3) excludes all original twenty-sign, exact-flow, full-R5 arrays.
This proves the regional assertion(1) as a joint unverified draft.

The explicit old inventory N_pub, including every N1--N8 predicate on
its conditional domain, is retained. This exclusion needs only its
four cuts and terminal adjacent triangle; no omitted old predicate is
asserted redundant. S_scalar remains the entire specified proportional-
gradient polynomial geometry with all legal values, not all nonlinear
one-dimensional trajectories. No smaller list is substituted for it.

More precisely, the conjunction of the full old inventory, S_scalar,
and the full planar-plus-terminal-triangle nested families just derived
is empty in(1). This is a correctly scoped enlargement of the nested
family. It is not a claim that the previously published planar test
alone already excludes every new point here. No independent-test
separation from the complete previous conjunction is asserted: that
would require an actual survivor of that previous conjunction. Nor is
one inferred from a virtual parameter used in the comparisons.

This complete regional exclusion is the output. The only surviving
threshold possibility left by it has second physical step w>4/3, still
subject to every old predicate and original-array requirement. There
is no justification for swapping w and z or reversing the word.

No exact h,Lambda,M seed, global four-step impossibility, negative
objective error, or original asymptotic profile theorem has been
established. The sufficient lift04219554e41d9750 remains conditional.
The original Goal remains OPEN. Formal verification is not authorized
in this allocation; all new assertions here remain UNVERIFIED.

Resource use: zero numerical evaluations, solver calls, machine-algebra
jobs, replay, formal-verifier calls, installations, consult calls, and
subagents. Only symbolic reasoning, textual retrieval, and persistence
were used. No historical object, truth store, or runtime counter was
edited. This round's handoff does not claim that the external worker
loop has already terminated.

# Arbitrary-template primitive inflation: exact interface and scope

Status: joint analytical DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. This is max4's one authorized
round50 under TASK.md and guidance1c516691cc4c3011. No mathematical evaluator,
solver, replay, machine algebra, formal submission, consult, or subagent is
authorized or used. The unrestricted inflation claim is not established by
the results below. The original exact-profile Goal remains separate and open.

The mathematical predecessors are accepted04219554e41d9750, proof
Sections10--13, and accepted4c92d3fc4eb6153e, statement and proof Section6.
The former supplies the exact primitive identity and full binary join;
the latter uses arbitrary seeds inside those basic trees. Neither accepted
statement asserts arbitrary-template inflation. Every new calculation below
is reproduced jointly; no unverified shared finding is used as a premise.

## 1. Original full certificate and the physical word

For a nonnegative word w of length n, W_n(w) is the supremum of
2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), over all finite Euclidean
dimensions, all convex differentiable functions with L-Lipschitz
gradient and a minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, under
x_(t+1)=x_t-(w_t/L)grad f(x_t). Here L>0 and the empty word is allowed.
This is the project's W=2R normalization. The letter q below follows
TASK.md and means the child WORD, not a scalar derivative from another
fact. Other locally defined letters carry the scopes explicitly given.

Normalize the smoothness to1. For a word w=(w_0,...,w_(n-1)), put
S_w=sum w_t, K_w=1+S_w, x_(t+1)=x_t-w_t g_t, and

 Q^w_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
 V_w=2 sum_(t<n) w_t(f_t-f_n)
       +sum_(t<n)w_t(1-w_t)||g_t||^2-S_w(S_w+1)||g_n||^2.

All finite ordered interpolation residuals Q^w_ij are nonpositive on an
actual convex differentiable1-smooth function. Let Lambda^w have zero
diagonal and nonnegative entries on every ordered distinct pair. With
e the all-ones column, T^w_tt=1, T^w_tu=w_u for u<t, zero for u>t, set

 L^w=diag(Lambda^w e)-Lambda^w,
 omega^w=(-2w_0,...,-2w_(n-1),2S_w)^T,
 D^w=diag(w_0(2-w_0),...,w_(n-1)(2-w_(n-1)),-S_w(S_w+2)),
 M^w=D^w+(L^w T^w+(T^w)^T(L^w)^T)/2.

The assumed input conditions are (L^w)^T e=omega^w and M^w PSD on the
whole R^(n+1). Accepted04219554e41d9750 gives the polynomial identity

 V_w+sum_(i!=j)Lambda^w_ij Q^w_ij=<M^w,Gram(g_0,...,g_n)>.       (1)

There is no quotient at a zero step, no restriction to a gradient rank,
and no support, product, reflection, or multiplier symmetry assumption.

Let h have length r, h_i>=1, capacity A=1+sum h_i, and a full input
certificate as above. Let q have length s, nonnegative steps, capacity
C=1+sum q_a, and an arbitrary full input certificate. Define

 beta_i=1+C(h_i-1),
 H=(q,beta_0,q,beta_1,...,q,beta_(r-1),q).                     (2)

There are r+1 child copies, r bridge steps, and exactly

 N=(r+1)s+r=(r+1)(s+1)-1

physical steps. There are N+1=(r+1)(s+1) formal gradients. Index a node
as (i,a), where 0<=i<=r, 0<=a<=s, at physical index i(s+1)+a. These
disjoint child node sets partition the entire formal space. The endpoints
p_i=i(s+1)+s are the bridge queries for i<r and the final query for i=r.
The full array has N(N+1) ordered off-diagonal slots. In particular, all
gradients at zero child steps are retained.

Every physical step is nonnegative because beta_i>=1. Direct summation gives

 1+sum H=1+(r+1)(C-1)+r+C(sum h_i-r)=AC.                     (3)

If r=0 the output is q; if s=0 it is h. An all-zero but nonempty q
still contributes all its formal gradients and zero physical steps.

## 2. Exact virtual data and energy decomposition

All child copies are segments of ONE actual GD trajectory for ONE static
function. For block i write z_i for its starting position, y_i=x_(p_i)
for its endpoint, e_i=g_(p_i), f_i^e=f(x_(p_i)), and

 P_i=sum_(a<s)q_a g_(i,a),
 dinf_i=P_i-(C-1)e_i,
 Dinf_0=0,  Dinf_i=sum_(k=1)^i dinf_k for i>=1.

Thus y_i=z_i-P_i and z_(i+1)=y_i-beta_i e_i. Define formal virtual jets

 X_i=[y_i+(C-1)e_i+Dinf_i]/C,
 F_i=f_i^e/C+(C-1)||e_i||^2/(2C).                            (4)

Their positions obey EXACTLY X_(i+1)=X_i-h_i e_i: indeed the change in
the numerator is

 -beta_i e_i-P_(i+1)+(C-1)(e_(i+1)-e_i)+dinf_(i+1)
 =-[beta_i+C-1]e_i=-C h_i e_i.

This is a formal algebraic GD relation; no interpolating virtual function
is asserted. Define the virtual residual

 Qhat_ij=F_i-F_j+<e_i,X_j-X_i>+||e_i-e_j||^2/2.

Expanding (4) cancels every extra norm and endpoint inner-product term:

 C Qhat_ij=Q^H_(p_i,p_j)+<e_i,Dinf_j-Dinf_i>.                     (5)

Let V_i denote the ORIGINAL primitive energy V_q of block i, relative to
its own actual endpoint. The following identity is exact on every formal
gradient and function-value coordinate:

 V_H=sum_(i=0)^r V_i+C^2 V_h(F_0,...,F_r;e_0,...,e_r).       (6)

Here the final energy uses the original steps h and the virtual data (4).
To check (6), subtract the child energies. The coefficient of f_i^e-f_r^e
for i<r is 2[(C-1)+beta_i]=2Ch_i. The coefficient of ||e_i||^2 is

 beta_i(1-beta_i)+C(C-1)
 =C^2h_i(1-h_i)+C(C-1)h_i.

The final coefficient is C(C-1)-(AC-1)AC. Since sum h_i=A-1, this equals

 -C(C-1)(A-1)-C^2 A(A-1),

which is precisely the final coefficient obtained from C^2 V_h and (4).
All internal child coefficients agree without alteration. These checks
also apply at zero child steps.

## 3. A full positive-flow candidate, and the exact missing payment

Let E_i be the coordinate restriction from all physical gradients to
the s+1 gradients of block i. Let E extract the r+1 endpoint gradients.
Embed child arrays in their original coordinate sets and put

 Lambda^0=sum_i E_i^T Lambda^q E_i+C E^T Lambda^h E.           (7)

All off-diagonal entries are nonnegative; coinciding contributions add.
The flow is exactly the required physical flow. Internal child nodes
have flow -2q_a. At a nonterminal block endpoint, its flow is

 2(C-1)-2C h_i=-2 beta_i.

At the final endpoint it is 2(C-1)+2C(A-1)=2(AC-1).

Let G be the full physical gradient Gram matrix, and define B as the
quadratic form

 B=C sum_(i!=j)Lambda^h_ij <e_i,Dinf_j-Dinf_i>.                    (8)

Combining (1), (5), and (6), the full remainder of (7) is

 V_H+sum Lambda^0_ab Q^H_ab
 =sum_i <E_i^T M^q E_i,G>+C^2<E^T M^h E,G>-B.              (9)

The first two terms are full-coordinate PSD congruences. The last term
has no established sign. Dropping it is an error. Thus (7) is an explicit
positive-flow lift, but NOT a proved PSD lift.

It actually fails for the M=0 inputs (13). In their full physical space
R^4 choose scalar formal gradients (g_0,g_1,g_2,g_3)=(0,1,1,0).
Then e_0=1, e_1=0, dinf_1=a, and (9) equals -2Ca<0. These are formal
coordinates used to disprove PSD of (7); they are NOT asserted to be
an actual function trajectory. A different array certifies the physical
word, as Section4 shows.

Here is a precise additional sufficient collective payment condition.
Put K=C^2 sum Lambda^h_ij Qhat_ij. Suppose there are real numbers
0<=theta_i<=1, an actual ordered array Gamma_ab>=0 with zero diagonal,
and a matrix P PSD on all physical gradients, such that the following
POLYNOMIAL identity holds after the original physical GD substitutions:

 K=sum_i theta_i V_i+sum_(a!=b)Gamma_ab Q^H_ab-<P,G>.         (10)

Then a full certificate is explicitly

 Lambda^out=sum_i(1-theta_i)E_i^T Lambda^q E_i+Gamma,
 M^out=sum_i(1-theta_i)E_i^T M^q E_i+C^2 E^T M^h E+P.        (11)

Indeed substitute (10) into (6) and use both input identities (1).
Every array coefficient in (11) is nonnegative and every matrix summand
is PSD on the unchanged physical coordinates. Cancellation of all formal
function values implies the exact original flow; the matrix equals the
original D+sym(LT) by (1). This is an EXTRA sufficient matching condition,
not a consequence proved from the stated input primitive conditions.

For an exact formulation with no sufficient-cone narrowing, any array
with the physical flow equals Lambda^0+Xi, where Xi is a real zero-
diagonal array with zero divergence at every node. Full feasibility is
equivalent to existence of such Xi with Lambda^0+Xi entrywise nonnegative
off diagonal and the full quadratic form

 sum_i E_i^T M^q E_i+C^2E^T M^h E-B+sum Xi_ab Q^H_ab          (12)

PSD. The last sum is purely quadratic because Xi has zero divergence.
In (12), the embedded matrices are evaluated against G; B and the last
sum denote scalar quadratic forms on the same full gradient coordinates.
This is a useful exact residual interface, not a solution of that
feasibility problem. It does not discard any original array.

## 4. The virtual-oracle shortcut is false even for two M=0 inputs

Put a=sqrt(2), C=1+a, d=a-1=1/C, and take h=q=(a). Both words have
the ORIGINAL full certificate

 Lambda_01=2, Lambda_10=2C, Lambda_00=Lambda_11=0, M=0.       (13)

For completeness, the flow is (2-2C,2C-2)=(-2a,2a).
With T=[[1,0],[a,1]], the first diagonal of D+sym(LT) is
a(2-a)+2(1-a)=2-a^2=0; the second is
-a(a+2)+2C=0; and twice the off-diagonal is -2+2C(a-1)=0.
Thus (13) verifies the entire two-gradient matrix and both ordered signs.

The physical output is H=(a,2,a), with length3 and capacity C^2.
Use the single static convex1-smooth function f(x)=x^2/2, minimizer0,
and initial point x_0=-C. Its whole actual run is

 x_0=-C,  x_1=1,  x_2=-1,  x_3=d,
 g_t=x_t,  f_t=x_t^2/2.

Block0 ends at e_0=y_0=1. Block1 starts at -1, ends at e_1=y_1=d,
and has P_1=-a, dinf_1=-a-a d=-2. Consequently the virtual data (4) are

 (X_0,F_0,e_0)=(1,1/2,1),
 (X_1,F_1,e_1)=(-d,d^2/2,d).

They obey X_1=X_0-a e_0, but

 Qhat_01=-2d<0,      Qhat_10=2d^2>0.                       (14)

For the second equality, expansion gives
Qhat_10=(d^2-1)/2+a d+(d-1)^2/2=d(d+a-1)=2d^2.
For the first, Qhat_01=(1-d^2)/2-a+(1-d)^2/2=1-d-a=-2d.

Thus these virtual jets are NOT smooth-convex interpolable. Both input
certificates are M=0 and satisfy the balanced product relation. Neither
of these additional restrictions rescues this virtual-oracle assertion.

Both actual child energies are zero: on the unit quadratic the one-step
energy is x_start^2-C^2 x_end^2, and C d=1. Every actual Q is zero.
Therefore even allowing nonnegative payments by child energies cannot
make each virtual interpolation residual nonpositive separately: the
positive Qhat_10 in (14) has zero child payment available. By contrast,
the template-weighted sum is

 2 Qhat_01+2C Qhat_10=-4d+4C d^2=0.

This demonstrates why a potentially viable theorem must control the
COLLECTIVE template-weighted expression, not certify a fictitious
smooth-convex oracle pair by pair.

This example is explicitly NOT a counterexample to the physical lift.
H=(a,2,a) is the accepted binary primitive join of q with itself: the
join surplus is1, since 1(2C+1)=C^2. Its full lifted certificate exists
by accepted04219554e41d9750 Section11. Its actual V_H is zero on this run.

## 5. A sufficient closure in the proved, proper template subclass

Call a template BASIC here if it is obtained by primitive--primitive
binary joins starting from empty primitive leaves, with surplus d_v>0
at every node satisfying d_v(A_left+A_right+d_v)=A_left A_right.
This is precisely the primitive tree construction of the accepted fact,
not a definition imposed on arbitrary primitive inputs.

For every such basic template h and every arbitrary full-certificate
nonnegative child q, the word (2) DOES have a full certificate. Replace
each of the r+1 empty leaves of its tree by q. At each original node,
its two child capacities and its surplus are all multiplied by C:

 (A_left,A_right,d_v) -> (C A_left,C A_right,C d_v).

The defining surplus equation and its positive root are preserved.
The bridge becomes exactly 1+C(h_i-1), in its original inorder position.
The resulting inorder word is (2), and its length and capacity are (3).

Here is the full array transformation, including the sign proof. At
each transformed node set kappa=((A_left+A_right+d_v)/A_right)^2,
which is unchanged under the common scaling. Embed the complete left
array with factor1 and right array with factor kappa. Write p for the
bridge query, z for the node's final query, and b_j for each physical
right-child step at its nonterminal node j. Add the actual ordered edges

 (j,p): (kappa-1)b_j,    (z,p): kappa-1,
 (p,z): (kappa-1)C d_v,  (j,z): (kappa-1)b_j.                (15)

There are no omitted finite coordinates. All coefficients are
nonnegative since kappa>1, d_v>0, and the physical b_j>=0. The accepted
identity gives exactly

 M_parent=E_left^T M_left E_left
                 +kappa E_right^T M_right E_right,         (16)

with the original flow and D+sym(LT). Induction proves the full matrix
PSD and every original ordered sign. This uses one same function on
one actual GD word throughout; there is no oracle reset.

The template subclass is proper EVEN INSIDE the full M=0 class. For
h=(3/2,3/2), a full M=0 array is

 Lambda=[[0,1,1/2],[7/2,0,17/2],[1,14,0]].                 (17)

Its row-minus-column flow is (-3,-3,6). For
T=[[1,0,0],[3/2,1,0],[3/2,3/2,1]] and D=diag(3/4,3/4,-15),
the diagonal entries of LT are (-3/4,-3/4,15), and the paired
off-diagonal sums are

 (-1-3/4)+(7/4)=0,
 (-1/2)+(1/2)=0,
 (-17/2)+(3/2+7)=0.

Thus D+sym(LT)=0 on all three formal gradients; all six ordered entries
are positive. But every length2 basic primitive tree contains a length1
empty-leaf join with step sqrt(2), whereas (17)'s word has neither step
equal to sqrt(2). Hence basic-template closure says nothing by itself
about using (17), or every other M=0 word, as an arbitrary template.

## 6. Repetition and the distinction between seed and template

For a FIXED basic template h of length r>=1 and capacity A, start with
any fixed nonnegative full primitive child q^(0) of length s and capacity
C. Recursively use (2) with q=q^(k). Equations(15)--(16) give actual full
certificates at every depth; all physical steps remain predetermined
and nonnegative. The exact counts and capacities are

 length(q^(k))+1=(r+1)^k(s+1),
 capacity(q^(k))=A^k C.                                    (18)

Accepted04219554e41d9750 Section11 makes a primitive word dominant with
weights (w,sum w+1). Its full objective certificate and the accepted Huber
witness therefore give, at every depth and in the original W=2R units,

 W_(length(q^(k)))(q^(k))=1/(2A^k C-1).                     (19)

The upper bound is over every finite dimension and every admissible
smooth convex objective. It has no multiplicative loss depending on k.
Rescaling f by L restores arbitrary L>0 and leaves W unchanged.

The exponent of this family depends on A and r, NOT on the arbitrary
leaf capacity C. Thus an exponent lower bound applied to (18)--(19)
can restrict A as a TEMPLATE capacity in a recursively closed class.
It cannot restrict C as an arbitrary fixed SEED capacity. To put C in
the exponent one would have to prove the missing theorem allowing that
seed itself to serve as the template, with all the requisite hypotheses.

Moreover the accepted basic recurrence already gives
A<=C_r<=(r+1)^rho, rho=log_2(1+sqrt(2)), for basic h. No external exponent
bound is needed for that existing basic-template restriction. In
particular, (18)--(19) place no new ceiling on an arbitrary length5/6
primitive seed or on the full M=0 subclass.

## 7. Source-dependent exponent check, after the sufficient basic closure

This section assumes an EXTERNAL premise; it is not a Danus fact import.
Source: Yuhan Ye and Kaizhao Liu, *Silver Rate Is (Almost) Optimal for
Gradient Descent*, arXiv:2609.09152v2 (2026), Theorem1.1 and definition(1),
https://arxiv.org/html/2609.09152v2 . Its applicable statement is:
there are absolute K_YL>0 and n_YL such that every integer n>=n_YL
and every predetermined h in[0,infinity)^n satisfy

 W_n(h)>=n^(-rho-K_YL sqrt(log log n/log n)).                 (20)

Its R_n divides the objective gap by (L/2)||x_0-x_star||^2,
so R_n there is EXACTLY this project's W=2R. The supremum includes
all finite dimensions and all minimizers. At L=1 its GD convention
coincides with ours. Zero and arbitrarily large nonnegative steps are
allowed; the theorem is uniform over horizon-dependent words. This is
the non-anytime theorem, not the infinitely-many-horizons anytime result.
The primary definition, statement, Section3.2 proof and its uniform
constant passage were read, including the finite-dimensional construction
and its distance normalization in Section3.1. Exact HTML/PDF/text copies
are in downloads/ye_liu_2609.09152v2.*. No formal acceptance is asserted.

Here is the deduction, applied only when actual recursive feasibility
and (19) have been proved. Put m=r+1>1 and n_k=m^k(s+1)-1. The lower
bound (20) and (19) would imply

 log(2CA^k-1)/log(n_k)
 <=rho+K_YL sqrt(log log(n_k)/log(n_k)).                      (21)

For fixed finite s,C and A>1, the left side tends to log(A)/log(m),
while the additional term on the right tends to zero. Therefore such
a recursively certified template necessarily obeys

 A<=m^rho.                                                  (22)

This implication assumes (20). It uses every depth of the ACTUAL
finite-horizon family, not an anytime embedding or a formal scalar
capacity recursion. Fixed prefactors, including C and s+1, disappear
only after division by log(n_k); no depth-dependent loss is discarded.
For the basic templates proved here, (22) is already contained in the
accepted scalar bound, so it is not a new restriction. Applying (22)
to an arbitrary length5/6 seed, or to all M=0 inputs such as (17), would
assume the very template-closure theorem this pilot has not established.

The primary background source on composition was also downloaded and
read: Grimmer, Shu, Wang, *Composing Optimized Stepsize Schedules for
Gradient Descent*, arXiv:2410.16249v2, definitions in Section2.1 and
Proposition6 with its proof. Its positive s-composable schedules include
an additional product equality. Those definitions are not silently
identified with the general full primitive input (1), and no result
from that source is a new proof premise here.

## 8. A second nonnegative-flow formula fails even with M=0 inputs

The first candidate (7) is not the only tempting generalization of the
binary join. The following chronological fan formula has exact physical
flow and nonnegative entries, and agrees with the accepted lift for the
one-step saturated template. For a general input array Lambda^h put

 b_i^back=sum_(j<i)Lambda^h_ij,    mu_i=1+b_i^back.

Scale the full child array in block i by mu_i. For each i<j add
C Lambda^h_ij on the endpoint pair (p_i,p_j). For each i>j add
Lambda^h_ij times each of the two fans

 sum_(a<s)q_a Q^H_((i,a),p_j)+Q^H_(p_i,p_j),
 sum_(a<s)q_a Q^H_((i,a),p_i).                              (23)

All coefficients are nonnegative. The nonterminal child flow is
-2mu_i q_a+b_i^back q_a+b_i^back q_a=-2q_a. The total flow of
block i is C omega_i^h, so the endpoint flow is precisely that of (7).
This verifies every required flow equation and sign, without a PSD claim.

Take the M=0 template (17) and the M=0 child q=(sqrt(2)) from (13).
Their physical word is

 H=(a,(3+a)/2,a,(3+a)/2,a),  a=sqrt(2).

Set all scalar formal endpoint gradients e_0,e_1,e_2 to zero, and let
the child starting gradients be u_0,u_1,u_2. Set every formal value to
zero. Thus dinf_i=a u_i and V_i=a(1-a)u_i^2. Because bridge gradients
vanish, z_1=y_0 and z_2=y_1=y_0-a u_1.

For the backward edge1->0, the sum of the two fans in (23) equals V_1:
the residual from the block1 start to y_0 is u_1^2/2, and that to its
own end is (1/2-a)u_1^2, both multiplied by a. The same calculation
gives V_2 for edge2->1. For edge2->0, the earlier endpoint is
y_0=z_2+a u_1, so the fan sum is instead

 V_2+a^2 u_1u_2=V_2+dinf_1dinf_2.

All forward endpoint residuals vanish on this formal assignment. The
child M matrices and the template M matrix are zero. Identity(6) gives
V_H=sum V_i. Therefore the complete remainder of (23), after the
child scalings cancel, is exactly

 Lambda^h_20 dinf_1dinf_2=dinf_1dinf_2.

Choosing (u_0,u_1,u_2)=(0,1/a,-1/a) makes this remainder -1. Hence the
second formula fails full PSD even for the explicitly positive M=0
template (17) and saturated M=0 child (13). This is another array-local
failure, NOT a valid static negative-energy witness for the physical H.
It does not establish nonexistence of a different full array.

## 9. Remaining question

The arithmetic, full energy decomposition, positive-flow candidate,
proper basic-template closure, and virtual-oracle counterexample above
are complete joint analytical drafts. They do not prove that every
full primitive h is a valid template, and they do not supply an actual
negative-energy witness for a lifted word with valid inputs. The exact
unpaid term is (8); (10) is one fully specified sufficient collective
payment hypothesis, and (12) retains the entire unrestricted array
problem. A failure of (7), or of pairwise virtual interpolation, cannot
be promoted to failure of every array for the physical word.

No new fact has been submitted or accepted in this pilot. Sections3 and8
are two distinct failed full-array constructions; Section4 is a genuine
static counterexample to their tempting oracle premise, but not to the
unrestricted inflation claim. The latter remains unresolved, including
for arbitrary full M=0 templates.

Under the exact physical h from x0=7/3, positions are (7/3,1,-7/5,-3/5,21/25,-7/25), gradients (1,1,-3/5,-3/5,21/25,-7/25), values (11/6,1/2,33/50,9/50,441/1250,49/1250). Each adjacent pair obeys x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i. The exact initial distance to the minimizer is7/3. At every scalar point F(u)=u*g(u)-g(u)^2/2. For the original orientation Q_ij=f_i-f_j+g_i(x_j-x_i)+(g_i-g_j)^2/2, substitution gives Q_ij=(x_j-g_j)(g_i-g_j). The only nonzero query normals are4/3 at j0 and-4/5 at j2; all gradients are in[-3/5,1]. Thus ALL30 ordered query rows and both optimum orientations are nonpositive. Real scalar gradients give a PSD Gram. For any actual run with this value identity, the original primitive energy V_h=2sum h_i(f_i-f5)+sum h_i(1-h_i)g_i^2-s(s+1)g5^2 telescopes to x0^2-(x5+s*g5)^2. Here s=44/5, A=49/5, so V_h=49/9-(343/125)^2=-293216/140625<0. The original identity in accepted fact04219554e41d9750 Sections10--11, V_h+sum Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M,Gram>, contradicts every Lambda>=0 with exact flow and M>=0 on all six formal coordinates. The single-cut tests are A(h_j-1)<=(1+prefix sum)(1+suffix sum); their positive gaps, for endpoint a, b and central a, are78/15,98/225,4306/225, with the remaining gaps given by reflection. The unit-quadratic test has A*abs(product(1-h_i))=2401/3375<1. This is one complete newly derived construction; it does not depend on the unverified general-family theorem.
