# Growing all-cut dictionaries with synchronized full-prefix completion

ONE JOINT UNVERIFIED analytical draft, max3 invocation71, 2026-10-02.
Assignment TASK.md; master_guidance 0bfc668f1c92a6d1; plan 2e2aed472da8eced.
No verifier call is authorized. No claim below is a new fact. All local
lemmas, formulas, and comparisons form one candidate proof, rather than
unverified intermediate facts adopted as independent premises.

## 1. Outcome and exact scope

This draft opens ONE new law. Its dimension is the number of original
steps exceeding1, plus1. It places EVERY accepted single-cut Huber
witness, together with the canonical Huber witness, in an actual real
orthogonal coordinate dictionary. It then takes ONE synchronized maximum
of ALL ORIGINAL PREFIX conjugate planes. This is a coupled complete-prefix
height program, not the separable sum of independently completed functions.
It has a finite explicit terminal formula involving known anchor subsets,
not unknown interpolation data, a PSD feasibility oracle, or a projected
trajectory whose terminal value is unspecified.

The construction works on every finite nonnegative word, including empty
words, zero repeats, arbitrarily large and unordered steps, and any last
step. It specifies one convex differentiable1-smooth function BEFORE GD.
The new function has complete minimum set {0}, initial squared radius1,
explicit actual prefix jets, all ordered/optimum rows, and a full real Gram.
The attained lower functional is denoted V_sync(h).

The diagnostic yields a WHOLE-LAW obstruction, not just a failure of uniform
weights. For any finite coordinate subset/repetition of these scalar
channels, any positive weights summing to1, and its synchronized maximal
prefix completion F_w, the candidate proof gives

    B_(2^m-1) * 2F_w(x_(2^m-1)) < 19/20

on the ENTIRE SAME-enclosing-m H_(m,1/4), for every m>=1536. The nearest
initial radius for EVERY one of these completed functions is exactly1.
For the specified uniform ALL-cut selector the stronger conclusion is

    V_sync(H_m)<H_base(H_m),
    max{K_(2^m-1)(H_m),V_sync(H_m)}=H_base(H_m),
    B_(2^m-1) H_base(H_m)<237/250.

Adding the exact accepted special-word recognizer of Section10 preserves
this diagnostic because those special words have a different final step.
This does NOT upper-bound unrestricted W_n, all growing dictionaries,
nonorthogonal mixtures, all nonlinear scalar functions, or all smooth
completions with potentially different nearest radii. It does not resolve
the original target in either direction.

## 2. Normalization, accepted inputs, and exact benchmark

For a finite word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) of nonnegative real numbers, W_n(h)
is the supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), over the original
finite-dimensional convex differentiable L-smooth class with an attained
minimum, and GD x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). We use L=1. Multiplying
our final objective by any L>0 preserves the actual points and the
normalized payoff under the original steps h_i/L.

Only these accepted inputs are used:

* 1252b8823bb5a290: signed rows Q_ij and the explicit maximal prefix
  conjugate construction, especially statement(a), proof paragraphs1--2.
  Its full-cone optimality and multiplier selection do not choose our prefix.
* b98bb807d514e80d: explicit single-cut instances and K_n lower bound;
  exact original capacity notation. We also print the needed scalar proof.
* 04219554e41d9750: ORIGINAL all-integer-split capacity definitions and
  unconditional benchmark facts only. No feasible seed is assumed.
* 5fd396eba75651e7: Section10 equation(77), namely the exact same-word
  B_(2^m-1)H_base<237/250 at tau=1/4,m>=1536. We import no spiral
  witness as a member of the present family.
* c3bfc54321ddec65: its exact two special physical word families, attained
  terminal laws, complete minimum sets, radius1 and benchmark comparison,
  solely in Section10 of this report and the exact-word selector.

Write s=sum_(i<n)h_i and

    P_j=sum_(i<j)h_i, T_j=sum_(i>j)h_i,
    K_n(h)=max{1/(1+2s),
          max_(0<=j<n) (h_j-1)_+^2/[(1+P_j)^2(1+2T_j)]}.

The inner maximum is omitted for n=0. Retain the exact benchmark

    Omega(a,b)=(a+b+sqrt((a+b)^2+4ab))/2,
    G(a,d)=2a+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8ad)/2,
    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_n=max_(0<=k<n) Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=k<n) G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

All split indices are integers; no balanced restriction is imposed on B.
The desired separate comparison would be inf_(length(h)=n)B_n V(h)>=1-o(1)
with schedule-independent loss. The construction alone does not assert it.

## 3. Scalar channels from the complete original word

Let J_cut={j:0<=j<n and h_j>1}. Introduce one additional channel label b0,
which is a label, not an original physical index or a bridge value. Let
R(h)={b0} union J_cut, in that order with J_cut increasing.

For b0 set

    c_b0=a_b0=1/(1+2s).

For each j in J_cut set

    c_j=1/(1+P_j),
    a_j=c_j(h_j-1)/(1+2T_j)>0.

The symbols a_j,c_j in Sections3--8 are these scalar positive slope
magnitudes only; they are not benchmark capacities or other fact-local a_j.
Define the scalar function phi_r by phi_r(0)=0 and

    phi_r'(z)=clip(z,[-a_r,c_r]).

Equivalently it is z^2/2 on [-a_r,c_r], c_r z-c_r^2/2 on z>=c_r,
and -a_r z-a_r^2/2 on z<=-a_r. The derivative is continuous,
nondecreasing and1-Lipschitz. The function is convex1-smooth and its
unique minimizer is0. Its conjugate is v^2/2 on [-a_r,c_r] and infinity
outside. Both endpoints are actual real slopes; all support offsets are0.

Put S_i=sum_(t<i)h_t. The canonical channel, started at1, has for EVERY
0<=i<=n

    z_i^b0=1-c_b0 S_i,
    gamma_i^b0=c_b0,
    z_i^b0>=c_b0(s+1)>=c_b0,
    phi_b0(z_i^b0)=c_b0 z_i^b0-c_b0^2/2.

At s=0 this includes the threshold equality z=1=c_b0. Its terminal
normalized payoff is

    v_b0=2phi_b0(z_n^b0)=1/(1+2s).

For a cut j, put U_(j,i)=sum_(j<t<i)h_t for i>=j+1. For ALL physical
queries and the terminal point use the closed expressions

    i<=j: z_i^j=1-c_j S_i, gamma_i^j=c_j,
    i>=j+1: z_i^j=-a_j(2T_j+1)+a_j U_(j,i), gamma_i^j=-a_j.

The first region has z_i^j>=1-c_j P_j=c_j. At the selected query
z_j^j=c_j and its actual update is

    z_(j+1)^j=c_j(1-h_j)=-a_j(2T_j+1).

All subsequent original partial sums satisfy 0<=U_(j,i)<=T_j, so

    z_i^j<=-a_j(T_j+1)<=-a_j.

The formulas therefore give every actual update, with no deleted
ordinary entry, reset, chronological reordering or executed recurrence.
They remain valid when a selected bridge is the final step (T_j=0),
when ordinary entries vanish, and when other entries are unbounded.
The terminal payoff of this channel is

    v_j=2phi_j(z_n^j)=a_j^2(2T_j+1)
       =(h_j-1)^2/[(1+P_j)^2(1+2T_j)].              (3.1)

Define scalar proximal coordinates and heights at every point by

    u_i^r=z_i^r-gamma_i^r,
    delta_i^r=phi_r(z_i^r)-(gamma_i^r)^2/2.

In the positive region u_i^r>=0 and delta_i^r=c_r u_i^r; in the
negative region u_i^r<=0 and delta_i^r=-a_r u_i^r. Consequently

    delta_i^r>=0, and delta_i^r=0 iff u_i^r=0.      (3.2)

All scalar values can be printed as delta_i^r+(gamma_i^r)^2/2.

## 4. Real growing dictionary and the synchronized completion

The specified selector uses every r in R(h), dimension d=|R(h)|, and
uniform weights w_r=1/d. For the whole-law boundary we simultaneously
allow any nonempty finite list of labels drawn from R(h), including
repetitions, and arbitrary positive weights w_r summing to1. A zero
weight is simply omitted, so boundary subsets are included. Coordinates
are separately indexed even when their labels repeat. Every choice is
made from the complete known word BEFORE the run.

Write alpha_r=sqrt(w_r) and let e_r be the orthonormal basis of R^d.
Use the identity metric I_d, unit affine-plane weights, and initial point

    x_0=sum_r alpha_r e_r, ||x_0||^2=1.

The auxiliary separable support box is

    D_box=product_r[-alpha_r a_r,alpha_r c_r],
    F_box(x)=sum_r w_r phi_r(x_r/alpha_r).

Its complete finite real vertex dictionary is

    {sum_r alpha_r t_r e_r: t_r in {-a_r,c_r} for each r}.

It has 2^d vertices, zero support offsets, and positive definite metric
I_d. For d>=2 these real vertices are noncollinear. No expected Gram,
random trajectory, or dimension-free cost assumption is involved.

For all 0<=i<=n the actual box points and jets are already given explicitly:

    x_i=sum_r alpha_r z_i^r e_r,
    g_i^box=sum_r alpha_r gamma_i^r e_r,
    f_i^box=sum_r w_r phi_r(z_i^r),
    y_i=x_i-g_i^box=sum_r alpha_r u_i^r e_r,
    delta_i=f_i^box-||g_i^box||^2/2=sum_r w_r delta_i^r.  (4.1)

For each coordinate, u_i^r>=0 exposes its endpoint c_r and u_i^r<=0
exposes -a_r. More explicitly, for EVERY other box slope v,

    <y_i,v-g_i^box>
      =sum_r alpha_r u_i^r(v_r-alpha_r gamma_i^r)<=0.  (4.2)

This checks every vertex and all convex combinations at once: past,
future, remote or repeated vertices have no omitted guard. The origin
lies in the box. Also <y_i,g_i^box>=delta_i. Thus (4.1) really is one
static box run, not a list of independently prescribed incompatible jets.

Now discard the box as the objective to be run and specify the final
objective directly from its CLOSED PREFIX FORMULAS. Retain EVERY i<n
in its original position, plus an optimum anchor star with y_star=0 and
delta_star=0. No terminal box jet is used to constrain the completion.
Set A_pre={star,0,...,n-1} and

    ell_i(v)=<y_i,v>-delta_i,
    M_w(v)=max_(i in A_pre) ell_i(v),
    H_w(v)=||v||^2/2+M_w(v),
    F_w(x)=sup_(v in R^d){<x,v>-H_w(v)}.           (4.3)

The actual new dictionary consists of the n+1 known affine conjugate
planes with real slopes y_i, offsets -delta_i and weights1. Repeated
anchors are retained harmlessly. This is one globally fixed function,
defined before any of its GD queries. The auxiliary box formulas are an
analytic construction of the input data; no box run was executed.

It matters that (4.3) synchronizes the physical index:

    max_i sum_r w_r[u_i^r(v_r/alpha_r)-delta_i^r]

is generally a different expression from

    sum_r w_r max_i[u_i^r(v_r/alpha_r)-delta_i^r].

The second expression permits a different prefix anchor in every
coordinate. We use the first, so the construction really includes
coupled terminal height choices. No equality between these two maxima
is assumed in the proof or the diagnostic obstruction.

## 5. Global smoothness, every original query, and the complete minimum

H_w is finite, continuous and1-strongly convex, since M_w is a finite
maximum of affine functions. It grows quadratically. Therefore each
supremum in (4.3) has a unique maximizer g(x). If x belongs to the
subgradient of H_w at g(x) and x' to its subgradient at g(x'), summing
their strong subgradient inequalities gives

    <x-x',g(x)-g(x')>>=||g(x)-g(x')||^2.

It follows that g is1-Lipschitz. Comparing the two maximizing formulas
at x,x' shows differentiability of F_w with gradient g; the error after
the linear term is bounded by ||x-x'||^2. Thus F_w is finite, convex,
continuously differentiable and1-smooth. This also reproves the portion
of accepted1252 needed for legality.

For original queried i,j, the scalar support identity gives

    ell_j(g_i^box)=<y_j,g_i^box>-delta_j<=0,
    ell_i(g_i^box)=0.                                  (5.1)

Indeed g_i^box is in the entire box, whereas y_j exposes its own box
gradient, whose inner product with y_j is delta_j. This is an exhaustive
old/future/remote guard, not an adjacent-pair test. The optimum plane0
has the same value0. Thus M_w(g_i^box)=0 and y_i is an active-plane
subgradient of M_w there. Hence

    x_i=g_i^box+y_i in partial H_w(g_i^box),
    grad F_w(x_i)=g_i^box,
    F_w(x_i)=<x_i,g_i^box>-||g_i^box||^2/2=f_i^box.    (5.2)

Every original queried point, gradient and value is therefore retained.
By the actual GD update, the resulting terminal position is exactly
the x_n in (4.1). Its gradient and value are computed in Section6,
without equating them to the old box terminal gradient or value.

At v=0 every plane has value -delta_i<=0. By (3.2), positive weights
give delta_i=0 iff all u_i^r=0, which is equivalent to y_i=0. Thus
EVERY active slope at v=0 is the zero vector, including repeated
zero-height anchors. Consequently partial H_w(0)={0}. The condition
grad F_w(x)=0 is equivalent to x in partial H_w(0), so

    argmin F_w={0}, min F_w=F_w(0)=0,
    dist(x_0,argmin F_w)=||x_0||=1.                    (5.3)

This is an independent proof for the completed function. It does not
carry the box radius forward without examining its new minimum set.
When n=0, H_w=||v||^2/2 and (5.3) remains valid.

## 6. Explicit actual unqueried terminal, all ordered rows, and full Gram

Let q=x_n from (4.1). The following is a finite algebraic selection rule
in the KNOWN anchors. It is not an interpolation feasible-set selector.
Order A_pre by star first, then physical indices. Enumerate nonempty
subsets I={i_0,...,i_k}, in increasing-size then lexicographic order,
with k<=d and affinely independent slopes y_(i_0),...,y_(i_k).
For k>0 define

    D_I=[y_(i_1)-y_(i_0),...,y_(i_k)-y_(i_0)],
    e_I=(delta_(i_1)-delta_(i_0),...,delta_(i_k)-delta_(i_0))^T,
    K_I=D_I^T D_I,
    theta_I=K_I^(-1)[D_I^T(q-y_(i_0))-e_I],
    g_I=q-y_(i_0)-D_I theta_I.                       (6.1)

For a singleton let theta_I,D_I,e_I be empty and g_I=q-y_(i_0).
Admit the subset exactly when

    theta_I>=0 componentwise, sum theta_I<=1,
    <y_b-y_(i_0),g_I><=delta_b-delta_(i_0)
                         for EVERY b in A_pre.       (6.2)

No unknown point, height or Gram is selected. All entries in (6.1)
are explicit finite sums, square roots of chosen weights, and inverses
of positive definite matrices of known slope differences. Singular
subsets are skipped using affine independence, not regularized or
assigned an artificial inverse. The conditions in (6.2) include every
remote and optimum plane. Choose the first admitted I.

Existence follows directly from the minimizer condition in (4.3):
q-g(q) lies in the convex hull of the slopes active at g(q). To see that
an affinely independent subset suffices, take any convex representation
with a minimal number of positive coefficients. An affine dependence
among its slopes permits adding a nonzero signed dependence with sum0
to the coefficients until a positive coefficient first becomes0,
preserving nonnegativity and the represented point. That contradicts
minimality. The selected slopes are therefore affinely independent,
at most d+1. Their equal plane values and their convex coefficients
give (6.1)--(6.2). Conversely those conditions prove
q-g_I in partial M_w(g_I), hence g_I=g(q) by strict convexity.
Every admitted subset yields the SAME terminal gradient and value.

Put lambda_0=1-sum theta_I, lambda_a=(theta_I)_a, and define

    g_n=g_I,
    y_n=sum_(a=0)^k lambda_a y_(i_a)=q-g_n,
    delta_n=sum_(a=0)^k lambda_a delta_(i_a)
             =delta_(i_0)+e_I^T theta_I,
    f_n=F_w(q)=||g_n||^2/2+delta_n,
    V_w(h)=2f_n=||g_n||^2+2delta_(i_0)+2e_I^T theta_I. (6.3)

This is the quantitative ACTUAL terminal law for the entire word.
It is finite even when gradients or positions repeat, the last step
is0, or the terminal is a minimum. If h_(n-1)=0, (5.2) and q=x_(n-1)
force (6.3) to equal that retained query's gradient/value. The empty
word gives g_n=x_0 and V_w=1 directly. No final query is erased.

For every actual sample i, including n and star, define its plane
ell_i(v)=<y_i,v>-delta_i. The terminal plane is the convex combination
of its active prefix planes, so ell_n<=M_w everywhere with equality
at g_n. The same two properties hold for every prefix plane at its
own g_i, by (5.1). Direct expansion of the original signed row gives

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =delta_i-delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i>
        =ell_j(g_i)-ell_i(g_i)<=0.                    (6.4)

This covers EVERY distinct ordered pair in {star,0,...,n}, both
terminal orientations, all remote pairs, and zero duplicates. In
particular Q_(i,star)=-M_w(g_i)<=0 and
Q_(star,i)=-delta_i<=0. It also supplies a complete original-array
certificate of legality, alongside the one fixed global function.

The actual full Gram of (x_0,g_0,...,g_n) has the following entries;
no rank constraint or expected matrix is substituted:

    <x_0,x_0>=1,
    <x_0,g_i>=sum_r w_r gamma_i^r,                  i<n,
    <g_i,g_j>=sum_r w_r gamma_i^r gamma_j^r,       i,j<n,
    <x_0,g_n>=sum_r alpha_r(g_n)_r,
    <g_i,g_n>=sum_r alpha_r gamma_i^r(g_n)_r,       i<n,
    <g_n,g_n>=sum_r(g_n)_r^2.                       (6.5)

Symmetry gives reversed entries. These are inner products of the real
vectors already printed, and so the matrix is PSD on the full actual
array. Their positions satisfy x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_tg_t. This remains
the original physical array when zero steps duplicate actual vectors.

The specified all-word scalar law is V_sync=V_w with ALL labels and
uniform weights. By (5.3)--(6.3), W_n(h)>=V_sync(h). One can retain
the independently accepted K_n via the attained finite maximum

    V_0(h)=max{K_n(h),V_sync(h)}.                    (6.6)

The maximizing function is fixed before GD after evaluating these
explicit complete-word formulas; ties use K first. No expected
trajectory or normalization of an expectation appears.

### 6.1 An exact same-law example with strictly coupled terminal gain

The synchronization is substantive even though it fails the later
hierarchy. Take the two-step word h=(3,3) and the declared uniform
three-coordinate law, ordering channels b0,0,1. Its complete prefix
anchors and terminal position are

    y_0=(12/13,0,3/4)/sqrt3,
    delta_0=(12/169+3/16)/3,
    y_1=(9/13,-12/7,0)/sqrt3,
    delta_1=(9/169+24/49)/3,
    q=(7/13,-8/7,-1/2)/sqrt3.

Define the explicit scalar

    theta=(54/169+72/49)/(81/169+144/49).

Multiplying by the positive denominator shows 1/2<theta<2/3.
The active subset {star,1} in (6.1) yields

    g=( (7-9theta)/13, (-8+12theta)/7, -1/2 )/sqrt3,
    y_1.g=delta_1,
    ell_0(g)=[(72-108theta)/169-9/16]/3<0.

The last strict inequality follows from theta>1/2 and18/169<9/16.
Thus all three planes have been checked, and (6.3) gives the exact
completed payoff

    V_sync(3,3)
     =[49/169+64/49+1/4]/3
       -(54/169+72/49)^2/[3(81/169+144/49)].        (6.7)

The three independently completed scalar channels still have terminal
gradients (1/13,-2/7,-1/2) and scalar normalized payoffs1/13,4/7,1/4.
For the canonical channel its terminal point lies in the retained
affine segment; for cut0 the terminal proximal point is half its
last retained negative anchor; for the final cut the terminal is the
threshold -a. These observations follow directly from the two scalar
planes and include each channel's optimum0. Their orthogonal sum has
terminal gradient g_old=(1/13,-2/7,-1/2)/sqrt3 and payoff

    V_sep=(1/13+4/7+1/4)/3.

Both g and g_old lie on y_1.v=delta_1, and q-g=theta y_1. Hence the
difference of their conjugate objective values is half||g-g_old||^2.
Equivalently,

    V_sync(3,3)-V_sep
       =[(6-9theta)^2/169+(-6+12theta)^2/49]/3>0.  (6.8)

Strictness holds because the first square could vanish only at2/3
and the second only at1/2. This is a symbolic example of the SAME
law, not a second law, an executed trajectory or a claim that the new
payoff exceeds K_n on this word. It rules out dismissing the analysis
as the identity for an uncoupled average of scalar payoffs.

## 7. A whole-family upper from the actual last queried anchor

This section analyzes the constructed witnesses, not W_n. Let n>=1
and d_last=h_(n-1), a physical stepsize rather than a dimension.
Since F_w preserves the last queried jet and is1-smooth,

    V_w<=2f_(n-1)^box+(d_last^2-2d_last)||g_(n-1)^box||^2
        =sum_r w_r U_r,                            (7.1)

where U_r is the same scalar expression for the r-th known last jet.
This follows either from the descent lemma proved in accepted1252 or
by replacing H_w by its retained last quadratic in the supremum.
It pays the full terminal step, however small or large it is.

For the canonical channel its affine region persists through terminal,
so the explicit expressions give

    U_b0=v_b0+d_last^2 c_b0^2
         =v_b0[1+d_last^2/(1+2s)].                 (7.2)

For a selected j<n-1 the negative affine region persists, giving

    U_j=v_j+d_last^2 a_j^2
       =v_j[1+d_last^2/(1+2T_j)].                 (7.3)

For j=n-1, which is present only when d_last>1, the last prefix point
is z_j=c_j with gradient c_j and value c_j^2/2. Therefore

    U_j=c_j^2(1-d_last)^2=v_j.                    (7.4)

These formulas also handle n=1 and a last selected bridge without a
postbridge query. They show that arbitrary weight optimization,
coordinate repetitions, subsets, and horizon-dependent dimension
cannot exceed max_r U_r in this family. This does not identify the
joint completion with a sum of separately completed functions.

We do not extend (7.1) to the true-radius normalized payoffs of ALL
other interpolants of this prefix: another interpolant could have a
larger minimum set and a different nearest radius. The completed F_w
here has its OWN unique minimum, proved in (5.3).

## 8. Entire SAME-enclosing-m hierarchy, all cuts and the terminal

Fix ONE integer m>=1536 and set, throughout every child,

    lambda=1+sqrt2, beta=1-1/(4m),
    c_h=lambda(1+1/(4m)), a_h=beta/c_h,
    H_0=(), H_(l+1)=(H_l,1+a_h c_h^l,H_l), 0<=l<m,
    n_m=2^m-1, S_l=sum H_l, X_l=1+S_l,
    A_l=a_h c_h^l, H_base=1/(1+2S_m).

No enclosing parameter is reset at an internal child, and H_m means
the entire actual list. A level-l step occurs at the root of an
H_(l+1) child, has value1+A_l, and has an adjacent complete H_l on
each side. All levels 0,...,m-1 and all their occurrences are included.
The actual last physical step is

    d_last=1+a_h.                                    (8.1)

We first establish simple rational bounds WITHOUT a numerical replay.
The inequality sqrt2<283/200 follows by squaring; hence
lambda<483/200<5/2. Since 1/(4m)<=1/6144,

    c_h<483/200+5/12288<483/200+1/600=29/12.

Also beta>29/30. Therefore a_h>2/5. On the other hand c_h>lambda>2
and c_h(c_h-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1>beta, which implies

    0<a_h<c_h-2<5/12,
    7/5<d_last<17/12,
    X_1=2+a_h>12/5.                                 (8.2)

The exact mass identity, obtained from X_(l+1)=2X_l+A_l by substitution,
is

    v_h=a_h/(c_h-2) in (0,1),
    X_l=v_h c_h^l+(1-v_h)2^l,
    A_l<=(c_h-2)X_l<(5/12)X_l.                     (8.3)

The sequence X_l increases because it is1 plus the mass of a growing
nonnegative word. Its identities are printed symbolically, not executed.

Consider ANY original cut at a level-l occurrence. Write p=1+P_j and
t=1+T_j. The physical neighboring H_l children give p,t>=X_l. The
ratio of this scalar cut payoff to the SAME whole-word Huber payoff is

    v_j/H_base
      =A_l^2(2p+2t+2A_l-1)/[p^2(2t-1)]
      =(A_l/p)^2[1+2(p+A_l)/(2t-1)].               (8.4)

For fixed A_l,t this decreases with p: it is a positive linear
combination of p^(-1) and p^(-2). For fixed p it decreases with t.
Thus, if l>=1, (8.2)--(8.3) imply

    v_j/H_base
      <(25/144)[1+(17/6)X_l/(2X_l-1)]
      <=(25/144)(1+34/19)=1325/2736<1/2.          (8.5)

Here X_l>=X_1>12/5 and X/(2X-1) decreases for X>=1.
Every cut of level l>=1 is before the final level0 step, so (7.3)
applies and its correction is bounded by

    1+d_last^2/(2T_j+1)
      <1+(289/144)/(19/5)
      =1+1445/2736<8/5.                            (8.6)

Together (8.5)--(8.6) give U_j<(4/5)H_base for all these cuts.

For a level0 cut BEFORE the final step, the suffix contains that
actual final step. Therefore t>=1+d_last=X_1>12/5, while p>=1
and A_0=a_h<5/12. The monotonicities in (8.4) give

    v_j/H_base
      <(25/144)[1+(17/6)/(19/5)]
      =4975/16416<1/3.                             (8.7)

The correction is again less than8/5 by the same actual suffix
bound, so U_j<(8/15)H_base<(4/5)H_base. This argument covers
every lower-level occurrence, even those nearest the end; it does
not assign to them a fictitious large trailing child.

For the LAST cut j=n_m-1, T_j=0, p>=1 and A_0=a_h<5/12.
The exact last-bridge formula (7.4), rather than (7.3), gives

    U_j/H_base=v_j/H_base
       =a_h^2(2p+2a_h+1)/p^2
       <(25/144)(3+5/6)=575/864<4/5.              (8.8)

All original cut channels, including the actual last one, thus obey

    U_j<(4/5)H_base, j in J_cut.                    (8.9)

Since v_j<=U_j, this also establishes anew that

    K_(n_m)(H_m)=H_base.                            (8.10)

The canonical last-anchor bound is

    U_b0=H_base[1+d_last^2 H_base].                 (8.11)

All entries of H_m exceed1, so S_m>n_m. Since m>=1536, in particular
2S_m+1>2048 and d_last^2<3. Consequently

    d_last^2 H_base<3/2048<1/474,
    U_b0<(475/474)H_base<(6/5)H_base.              (8.12)

For the declared uniform all-cut selector, all n_m cuts are present,
so the real dimension is n_m+1=2^m and all weights are1/(n_m+1).
The WHOLE-word estimate (7.1) and (8.9),(8.12) yield

    V_sync(H_m)
      <[(6/5)+(4/5)n_m]/(n_m+1) H_base
      <=H_base,                                   (8.13)

where the last inequality is 6+4n_m<=5+5n_m, valid for n_m>=1.
Thus (6.6) and (8.10) give the exact declared-selector identity

    V_0(H_m)=H_base.                               (8.14)

For the ENTIRE family of arbitrary positive weights, subsets,
repetitions and dimensions, (7.1),(8.9),(8.11) instead give

    0<=V_w(H_m)<=U_b0<(475/474)H_base.             (8.15)

The original benchmark comparison is now a SEPARATE step. By accepted
fact5fd396eba75651e7, Section10 equation(77), at exactly this enclosing
m,tau=1/4 and actual n_m,

    B_(n_m)H_base<237/250.

Consequently

    B_(n_m)V_0(H_m)<237/250,
    B_(n_m)V_w(H_m)<(237/250)(475/474)=19/20.       (8.16)

The second inequality is uniform in all choices defining this family,
including those depending arbitrarily on m and the entire word. It is
not a statement about W_(n_m)(H_m). It includes the explicit terminal
law and the actual last-query constraint; no snapshots, forced resets,
missing last step or surrogate horizon enter the comparison.

In particular, even optimizing the entire weight/subset family before
the run cannot supply the original all-word comparison. Choosing
epsilon=1/25 gives 1-epsilon=24/25>19/20. For every proposed uniform
N_epsilon, an actual n_m>=N_epsilon violates that comparison for this
family. This disproves a proposed lower functional, not the original
minimax conjecture.

## 9. What a combinatorial selector can and cannot inherit

A finite ordered tree that only assigns these COMPLETE-WORD scalar
channels to leaves, combines them by orthogonal direct sums, and then
applies one synchronized full-prefix completion at the root, is
contained in the analyzed family. Indeed each binary orthogonal sum
multiplies squared initial radii by its two nonnegative weights.
Flattening the tree yields their leaf weight products, summing to1;
associativity of orthogonal sums yields exactly (4.1). The root
completion is exactly (4.3), regardless of tree shape. Zero products
are discarded. Hence (8.16) includes all such finite tree selectors.

This assertion does NOT include a tree that changes a child's physical
prefix, applies intermediate completions that change subsequent
gradients, uses nonorthogonal coordinates, or starts a child at a new
point. Each of those would be a different construction requiring its
own global realization and initial radius proof.

For a plain orthogonal sum even before completion, normalized payoffs
are sums sum_r w_r v_r and cannot exceed their largest leaf payoff.
The root completion can exceed that sum, because its plane maximum is
synchronized, but the original final query yields (7.1). Thus the
nontrivial coupling is accounted for and still cannot manufacture
lossless Omega/G composition on the hierarchy. No scalar capacity
identity supplies missing physical cross-coordinate transport.

The required change is therefore in the earlier physical prefix:
it must escape this convex squared-radius allocation of single-switch
scalar trajectories, or use additional inter-sector geometry that
alters the last jet while paying all its remote guards. Merely adding
all cuts, optimizing their weights, repeating channels, increasing
dimension, changing the tree shape, or maximizing the synchronized
terminal extension does not do that. This is the precise scope of the
whole-law obstruction. A globally paid nonorthogonal or multi-switch
prefix remains open; no impossibility for those mechanisms is claimed.

## 10. Accepted special-word tests retained at their OWN horizons

This section is a regression interface to accepted c3bfc54321ddec65,
not a new law or a new theorem. It records enough of its exact function,
terminal and radius data to prevent conflating its special words with
H_m or with an arbitrary complete-word cover.

For an integer N>=2^60 define

    M=2N(N+1)+1, A=M^(1/12), B=M^(1/120),
    epsilon=M^(-1/3), a=1-1/M, C=2B+1.

These letters are LOCAL to this section. Let Chi_N consist of
sec^2((2j-1)pi/[2(2N+1)]), 1<=j<=N. Let L_up be THREE consecutive
increasing copies and L_down THREE consecutive decreasing copies.
Their masses are M-1. The original base words are

    h_three=(L_up,AM+1,L_down,BM+1,L_down,1+M epsilon),
    h_return=(L_up,AM+1,L_down,BM+1,L_down,CM+1,
                                     L_down,1+M epsilon).

Their lengths are9N+3 and12N+4. For a padded three-stage word of length n
use N=floor((n-3)/9)>=2^60 and add n-(9N+3) LEADING zeros. For a padded
return word use N=floor((n-4)/12)>=2^60 and add n-(12N+4) leading zeros.
No deletion of a physical zero or exchange of N between the two words
is made in this interface.

In R^3, with standard basis e1,e2,e3, the accepted data are

    p=e1/M, q=(-2e1+sqrt(2A-4)e2)/M,
    Q=||q||^2=2A/M^2,
    r=-q/2+sqrt((B/2-1/4)Q)e3, R=||r||^2=BQ/2,
    y=-Ae1-Mq, z=y-BMq-Mr, w=z-CMr-Mq,
    tP=a e1, deltaP=a/M,
    tQ=y+(M-1)q, deltaQ=(M-1)Q,
    tR=z+(M-1)r, deltaR=(M-1)R,
    tD=w+(M-1)q, deltaD=(M-1)Q.

The accepted fixed conjugates are

    Hthree(v)=||v||^2/2+max{0,tP.v-deltaP,y.v,
                              tQ.v-deltaQ,z.v,tR.v-deltaR},
    Hreturn(v)=||v||^2/2+max{0,tP.v-deltaP,y.v,
                        tQ.v-deltaQ,z.v,tR.v-deltaR,w.v,tD.v-deltaD}.

Their conjugates Fthree,Freturn start at e1. The parameters satisfy
B>=2,A>=4B+4 and M epsilon>=2B^2, as checked in the accepted fact.
Every original queried gradient is p,q,r, or p,q,r,q on the indicated
ordinary blocks and following bridge queries. The complete minimum sets
are conv{0,y,z} and conv{0,y,z,w}, respectively. In BOTH cases0 is the
unique closest minimizer to e1 and the true radius is1. The initial
proximal point is e1-p, not e1+p. All ordered/optimum/terminal guards
are supplied by that accepted fact, including the retained first r query.

For the three-stage terminal put

    E3=M(B+a)q+Mr,
    D3=||E3||^2=2A[(B+a)^2-(B+a)+B/2],
    b3=Aa(epsilon-2/M), theta3=(b3)_+/D3.

Then the ACTUAL unqueried terminal is

    x_n=z-M epsilon r,
    grad Fthree(x_n)=-M epsilon r-theta3 E3,
    V_three=2Fthree(x_n)=AB epsilon^2-(b3)_+^2/D3.

For the return terminal put

    dR=C+a, ER=Mq+M dR r,
    DR=||ER||^2=2A[1-dR+(B/2)dR^2],
    bR=2A[(B-1/(2M))epsilon-Ba/(2M)], thetaR=bR/DR.

Then

    x_n=w-M epsilon q,
    grad Freturn(x_n)=-M epsilon q-thetaR ER,
    V_return=2Freturn(x_n)=2A epsilon^2-bR^2/DR.

On each word's OWN actual horizon the accepted lower comparison is

    B_n V >= [3lambda^3/(2*20^(7/12))]
                   (lambda/2^(7/6))^(floor(log_2 N))>1,

and that ratio diverges. No scope exchange with the hierarchy occurs.

Define V_special(h) by exact recognition of these padded words using
their own lengths: return the respective displayed payoff if identical,
take the larger if both match, and0 otherwise. At most two exact word
comparisons are needed for a given length. This is a mathematical finite
rule for real inputs, with no claim about decidability of arbitrary real
input representations. Then define the attained portfolio

    V(h)=max{K_n(h),V_sync(h),V_special(h)}.          (10.1)

The witness for the largest positive entry is fixed before GD; use the
printed tie order K,sync,special. Each special witness is used solely
under the hypotheses of its accepted fact. Thus W_n(h)>=V(h).

The hierarchy has final step1+a_h<17/12, whereas either special word
has final step1+M^(2/3)>2. Leading zeros do not change that last step.
Therefore V_special(H_m)=0, and the exact deficit of (8.14),(8.16)
still holds for (10.1). On the recognized special words the accepted
channel retains B_nV>1 at every declared own horizon. Those exact
successes do not repair arbitrary-word coverage.

No claim that V_sync is small on these special words is made here.
Their growing last step makes (7.2)--(7.3) a different comparison than
the hierarchy. The complete formula (6.1)--(6.3) still applies to them.

## 11. Literature grounding and proof status

The active program was a mutation of the failed fixed small-palette
selector into a growing real dictionary with a synchronized complete
prefix. The missing mechanism was globally retained terminal height
after accounting for all radius shares and the real final query.

Matlas was queried for explicit smooth interpolation from a strongly
convex piecewise-quadratic conjugate. Its three hits concerned
quasi-strong convexity, Hamiltonian methods or augmented Lagrangians;
none supplied the needed mechanism. A web background search identified
the current long-step lower-bound literature but supplied no imported
claim. The exact own local paper

    downloads/taylor-hendrickx-glineur-1502.05666.txt

was read at Section2.4 and Lemma2, including both proof directions:
A. Taylor, J. Hendrickx, F. Glineur, "Smooth Strongly Convex Interpolation
and Exact Worst-case Performance of First-order Methods", arXiv:1502.05666.
The definitions there swap points/gradients under conjugation and trade
smoothness for strong convexity; that proof pattern motivates the known
anchor construction. All needed correctness input is instead the
accepted1252 fact and the elementary derivations here. No external
theorem is imported as a new unchecked premise.

The new law and its WHOLE-family deficit are UNVERIFIED. No independent
verifier, formal call, numerical check, executed trajectory, arithmetic
replay, optimizer, solver, benchmark DP or mathematical program was used.
The finite subset enumeration is a printed symbolic definition, not a
program executed in this round. Administrative serialization/readback
and ordinary source retrieval do not evaluate it.

## 12. Handoff

The declared law is quantitatively attainable for arbitrary words and
has a fully explicit terminal formula. Its synchronized completion
genuinely retains a growing number of planes and pays their full global
geometry; it is stronger in definition than a bare direct sum. Nevertheless
its complete same-m diagnostic fails uniformly, even allowing all weight
choices, all subsets/repetitions and unbounded finite dimensions.

The single concrete obstruction is (7.1): after the original prefix is
fixed, its final queried value and squared gradient impose a convex
combination of scalar last-anchor ceilings. On H_m every cut ceiling is
strictly below4H_base/5, and the canonical ceiling is only
H_base(1+d_last^2 H_base). No terminal anchor choice can evade that
constraint. The exact B_n comparison is a separately cited accepted
inequality, with no new recurrence or numerical benchmark run.

The next unresolved interface is a physically changed prefix with paid
cross-coordinate or repeated-sector transport, not another root-only
terminal completion of these scalar runs. Its required all-word
selection and schedule-independent vanishing loss remain at the
strength of the original problem. This draft does not claim that merely
introducing nonorthogonality solves that interface.

One law was investigated under the authorization of up to three; no
claim that every possible new law was exhausted is made. This is the
complete result of the one authorized analytical invocation. The
original target remains OPEN. No fact was submitted, no new fact_id
exists, and there is no automatic renewal or additional allocation.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001132 -->
# Exact tests of two terminal-game closures

Status: self-contained UNVERIFIED candidate counterexamples. No verifier request
is allocated. Only fact 1252b8823bb5a290 is used as a mathematical predecessor;
the new minimax candidate 40601f4c6f496ab8 is not a premise.

## 1. Three anchors are necessary even after optimizing the final step

The claim tested here is that, for every legal fixed prefix, its exact optimized
terminal potential can be evaluated with a mixture supported on at most TWO
prefix anchors, counting the optimum anchor. This claim is false already for
one queried GD step in dimension three. The example keeps both orientations of
every queried-prefix interpolation constraint.

Work with L=1 in R^3. Use the optimum jet (0,0,0), the positive first step
h_0=2, and the following prefix:

    x_0=(8,9,3),  g_0=(0,2,1),  f_0=19/2,
    x_1=(8,5,1),  g_1=(1,0,0),  f_1=11/2.

Thus x_1=x_0-2g_0 and ||x_0||^2=154. The proximal points and offsets are

    y_0=x_0-g_0=(8,7,2),    delta_0=f_0-||g_0||^2/2=7,
    y_1=x_1-g_1=(7,5,1),    delta_1=f_1-||g_1||^2/2=5.

Using the signed Q from the predecessor, ALL six prefix residuals are

    Q_(0,star)=-9,   Q_(star,0)=-7,
    Q_(1,star)=-2,   Q_(star,1)=-5,
    Q_(0,1)=-3,      Q_(1,0)=-1.                         (1)

For example the identity Q_ij=delta_i-delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i>
gives Q_(0,1)=2-<(0,2,1),(1,2,1)>=-3. Hence the predecessor constructs
the pointwise maximal legal prefix interpolant as the conjugate of

    H(v)=||v||^2/2+
             max{0, <(8,7,2),v>-7, <(7,5,1),v>-5}.       (2)

Call it F. It is one fixed everywhere-finite convex differentiable 1-smooth
function, with minimum F(0)=0, and it interpolates exactly the jets in (1).
For any nonnegative final step b, its terminal point is

    x_2(b)=(8-b,5,1).

Put v_*=(0,1,0). All three affine planes inside the maximum in (2) are
zero at v_*. Thus H(v_*)=1/2, and the conjugate definition gives

    F(x_2(b)) >= <x_2(b),v_*>-H(v_*)=9/2
                         for EVERY b>=0.                   (3)

At b=3, let alpha_star=alpha_0=alpha_1=1/3. The weighted affine bound on
H and completion of a square give

    F(x_2(3))
      <= (7+5)/3 + (1/2)||x_2(3)-(y_0+y_1)/3||^2
       =4+(1/2)||(0,1,0)||^2=9/2.                         (4)

Consequently the exact final-line-search value of this legal fixed prefix is

    min_(b>=0) 2F(x_2(b))/||x_0||^2 = 9/154.               (5)

The minimizing step and mixture are UNIQUE, and all three weights are
strictly positive. Here are two direct ways to check the asserted necessity.

First, equality in (3) makes v_* the unique conjugate maximizer. The
subgradient condition for (2) then requires

    x_2(b)-v_* = alpha_0 y_0+alpha_1 y_1,
    alpha_0,alpha_1>=0,  alpha_0+alpha_1<=1.               (6)

The subgradient condition follows by separating from the convex hull of
the active plane slopes: a point outside that hull gives a direction in
which <x,v>-H(v) increases. Thus it uses only the finite maximum in (2).
The third and second coordinates of (6) read

    2alpha_0+alpha_1=1,
    7alpha_0+5alpha_1=4.

Their unique solution is alpha_0=alpha_1=1/3. The first coordinate then
gives b=3. The remaining weight on the optimum is 1/3 as well.

Second, the full quadratic mixture expression can be checked directly:

    J(b,alpha_0,alpha_1)
      =2(7alpha_0+5alpha_1)
        +(8-b-8alpha_0-7alpha_1)^2
        +(5-7alpha_0-5alpha_1)^2
        +(1-2alpha_0-alpha_1)^2.                          (7)

It is the upper bound on 2F supplied by the weighted conjugate quadratics.
At (b,alpha_0,alpha_1)=(3,1/3,1/3), its value is 9 and all three derivatives
are zero. Its Hessian is positive definite because the three vectors
g_1,y_0,y_1 are independent; their determinant has absolute value three.
Thus the minimizer of (7) on b>=0 and the simplex is unique. Equations
(3)-(4) show that this bound is exact at the minimizing point, independently
of any general hull or minimax theorem.

## 2. Exact loss from using at most two anchors

The failure has an explicit quantitative size. For every feasible mixture,
the best b in (7) is

    b=8-8alpha_0-7alpha_1>=0.

After optimizing it, set

    Y=(7alpha_0+5alpha_1, 2alpha_0+alpha_1).

The remaining expression is precisely

    9+||Y-(4,1)||^2.                                    (8)

The simplex maps bijectively to the triangle with vertices
O=(0,0), A=(7,2), B=(5,1), and the unconstrained target (4,1) has the
three barycentric weights 1/3. A support of size at most two places Y on
the boundary of this triangle. Its distances to the three edges are:

* To [O,A], squared distance 1/53. The projection coefficient is 30/53,
  lying strictly between zero and one; the determinant is 4*2-1*7=1.
* To [O,B], squared distance 1/26. The projection coefficient is 21/26;
  the determinant is 4*1-1*5=-1.
* To [B,A], the projection coefficient from B is -2/5, so the nearest
  point of that segment is B and the squared distance is one.

Therefore optimizing BOTH the last step and all mixtures of support at
most two gives J=9+1/53, strictly larger than its exact value 9. In the
original normalized objective this is

    9/154 + 1/8162,                                     (9)

whereas the true value is 9/154. No count of active anchors in this
example is inferred from a numerical solution.

This refutes a universal two-anchor formula for the fixed-prefix terminal
game even when the final step is optimized. It does NOT assert that this
particular prefix maximizes the full PEP at h=(2,3), and it does NOT refute
the distinct, unproved statement that some globally worst prefix always
admits a smaller terminal support. The full selected certificate of an
entire PEP is also a different object from the fixed-prefix alpha here.

## 3. The whole schedule value is not jointly convex

For EVERY nonnegative schedule all of whose steps satisfy h_t>=2,

    W_n(h)=product_t(h_t-1)^2.                            (10)

Here is a complete elementary proof. Normalize L=1 and the minimizer to
zero. Adding the two interpolation inequalities between x and zero gives
<x,g>>=||g||^2, and smoothness gives ||g||<=||x||. Thus for h>=2,

    ||x-hg||^2
      =||x||^2-2h<x,g>+h^2||g||^2
      <=||x||^2+h(h-2)||g||^2
      <=(h-1)^2||x||^2.

Iterate this inequality and use 2f(x_n)<=||x_n||^2. This proves the upper
bound in (10) in every finite dimension. The scalar function f(x)=x^2/2
starting at x_0=1 has exactly that normalized error, proving equality.

In particular

    W_2(2,4)=W_2(4,2)=9,
    W_2(3,3)=16 > (9+9)/2.                              (11)

Thus the exact unrestricted value is not jointly convex in all step
coordinates, even on strictly positive schedules. This is a value-level
obstruction, stronger than exhibiting a nonconvex response on one fixed
function. It prevents replacing the proven final-step convexity by an
unqualified joint-convexity argument for all earlier steps. It does not
by itself disprove every possible all-step minimax identity.

## 4. Scope for continued proof search

The three-anchor obstruction is inside the full smooth-convex prefix class
and uses a finite, explicit legal function. It is not caused by dropping
incoming queried-prefix inequalities; every such inequality is strictly
satisfied in (1). It shows that any terminal state compression must retain
enough geometry to represent this triangle after projection along the
last gradient. One scalar value and an assumed pair of active anchors
cannot reproduce the fixed-prefix game.

The current global two-step calibration in finding 81a9c03cf73670f3 remains
separate and unverified: its unavoidable adjacent forward multiplier rules
out universal triangular certificate re-selection. Nothing in the present
example contradicts it or removes that edge. No new claim about remote
forward edges, global Huber tightness, or the asymptotic exact profile is made.

Targeted memory/fact searches found no existing version of (1)-(9). The
Matlas query returned unrelated general convexity statements; no external
result is used. This is the already audited finite conjugate-quadratic
regime of the accepted terminal fact, so no new literature layer is needed
to construct or evaluate these explicit examples.

# An actual threshold survivor of the published tests, and its full-cone obstruction

Status: ONE JOINT PROOF DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. Round37 of max4, under
master_guidance2765a48d4ff7259e and TASK.md. No numerical evaluation,
solver, machine algebra, replay, formal verifier, installation or subagent
was used. Accepted inputs are04219554e41d9750, Section10 (the full
primitive certificate identity), and1252b8823bb5a290 (interpolation).
Every other proof needed below is included in this text or its appended
proof transcripts. Those transcripts remain unverified parts of THIS
joint draft, never accepted predecessors.

The new object is at EXACT sum168/25, capacity193/25. It satisfies the
published cut/return/clipping/triangle/curvature/2948 conjunction specified
below, including the older quantitative cut-slack/prefix conditions and
the later row-calibration conditions on their actual domains. Nevertheless
one explicit static two-dimensional smooth convex function has negative
primitive energy for this word. Thus it has NO full primitive array.

This is not a seed, a global no-seed theorem, or the original exact-profile
answer. The separate COMPLETE value-optimized scalar channel is defined
and its actual static attainment proved in Section8; this round makes no
claim whether the displayed word satisfies that additional channel.

## 1. Original problem and precise comparison inventory

For h=(h0,h1,h2,h3)>=0 put s=sum h and C=1+s. With original indices0,...,4,
write e=(1,1,1,1,1)^T, T_ii=1,T_it=h_t for t<i and0 for t>i,
L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
omega=(-2h0,-2h1,-2h2,-2h3,2s)^T, and
Dprim=diag(h0(2-h0),h1(2-h1),h2(2-h2),h3(2-h3),-s(s+2)).
The sought seed has all20 off-diagonal Lambda_ij>=0, diagonal0,
L^T e=omega and M=Dprim+(LT+T^T L^T)/2 PSD on ALL of R5.

For actual GD data x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i define

 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
 V_h=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f4)+sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
                                      -sC||g4||^2.

Accepted04219554e41d9750 gives
V_h+sum Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M,Gram(g0,...,g4)>.
Actual smooth convex data obey Q_ij<=0. A negative actual V_h therefore
rules out EVERY original array, independently of its rank or support.

Use canonical excesses h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d) when all steps exceed1.
Put P=2+a,N=3+a+b,Dtail=2+d,Htail=3+c+d,eta=b/Htail,t=c/Dtail.
The published remaining-domain reduction is

 193/25<=C<87/10, 0<a,d<77/97,
 0<b,c<1552559/1081453, bc<1.

The reduction of nonnegative words, including zero and small steps, is
reproduced in Appendix A; the bc>=1 exclusion is reproduced there too.
We do NOT claim global emptiness on this or any unexamined domain.
The constructed word will satisfy these inequalities directly.

Here is the exact inventory N_pub tested in this document. Its proof
provenance is recorded explicitly; no full-array feasibility predicate is
inserted into this list.

**N1: all four cuts, all quadratics, and the scalar returns.** For j=0,...,3,
with Pj=sum_(i<j)h_i and Rj=sum_(i>j)h_i, require

 C(h_j-1)_+ <= (1+Pj)(1+Rj).

For k in(0,1] set p_i(k)=product_(r<i)(1-k h_r),p_0=1 and require

 B_full(k)=k sum_(i<4)h_i[1+k(1-h_i)]p_i(k)^2
                         -s k(1+Ck)p_4(k)^2 >=0.

In particular Qunit=1-C^2 a^2 b^2 c^2 d^2>=0. Also retain
Ccd<=N and, for the complete lower-clipping family,

 Cb/Htail<=P, Cbd/(2+c)<=P,
 C|bc-(1+d)min(bc,1)|<=P,
 Cd<=P IF bc>=1.

The last condition is genuinely conditional. The clipping equivalence
uses cd<1; this is derived from N2, rather than presumed at zero or
negative excesses. Provenance:6695d6fd665245ef,6a3a13d94112727b and
the complete joint554ec17f7f590bf0. Appendix A reproduces the legal
functions and reduction. The implied bounds on endpoint sums, product
caps, and the endpoint-interval elimination are consequences of these
and N2--N3, not omitted additional tests.

**N2: all adjacent triangles and their retained lifts.** Define

 kfirst=max((1+a)(a^2+2a-1)/a,0), Rfirst=P^2-kfirst,
 Jfirst=Rfirst-kfirst*eta.

Require the mixed second cut C^2 eta^2<=Jfirst, all three triangles

 ab[1+a(C+1)]<=Htail,
 bc[1+b(P+C)/P^2]<=Dtail,
 cd[1+c(N+C)/N^2]<=1,

and the lifted middle triangle

 bc[Rfirst+b(P+C)]<=Dtail*Jfirst.

For the terminal triangle define

 Ktail=c^2 d+(c+d+2)(1-cd), rtail=c^2 d/Ktail,
 Atail=1+a+b=N-2, Hpre=2+a+b=N-1,
 Ztail=(c+rtail)/(1+d),
 qlate=[1+(1-b)t]/b,
 qearly=[t+(Atail*t-1)Ztail]/Atail,
 qpre=max(qlate,qearly),
 Jpre=1-2Hpre*t+Hpre^2*qpre,
 Jquad=[(1-bt)N^2+(1+t)/(a^2 b)]/(1+b).

Retain BOTH C^2 rtail<=min(Jpre,N^2) and
C^2 max(t^2,rtail)<=Jquad. All denominators are positive on the
stated remaining domain: cd<1,bt<1 follow from its old triangle tests.
Provenance:554ec17f7f590bf0, Appendix A Sections11,15--17; full
normal checks and formulas are reproduced in Appendix A here.

**N3: lifted return.** Require

 C^2 b^2 d^2<=Rfirst(2+c)^2-kfirst*b(2+c).

This includes the unlifted partial-tail return when a<=sqrt2-1.
Its actual first-gradient-lift construction is reproduced in Appendix A;
provenance554ec17f7f590bf0, Sections2--3. The consequences
Cbcd<=1+sqrt2 and Cbd<=(1+sqrt2)^2 remain included as consequences.

**N4: the entire C82/C84 triangle, INCLUDING its value price.** Put

 ell82=c(1+d)(Dtail-bc)/Dtail^2>0,
 H82(u)=(u+1)(au-1),
 R82=[1-a+sqrt((1+a)^2+4a ell82)]/(2a).

For EVERY u>=b and z>=0 with ell82<=z^2<=H82(u), require

 E82(u,z)=((1+a)u-1)^2+(1-b^2)z^2-C^2(bc/Dtail)^2>=0.

In addition retain the stronger, published all-value version

 E82(u,z)-2(1+a)min((u-b)(au-1),(u+1)(au-1)-z^2)>=0.       (1)

At b<=1 the complete minimum of (1) is the clipped-stationary formula
in cbf9d8608baa8867; here we prove (1) for ALL legal parameters directly,
so no inactive branch or boundary is discarded. This is stronger than
only requiring the C84 attained minimum E82(R82,sqrt(ell82))>=0.
Provenance:6d992d34120a0c16,19f1d27c0eb8a588,cbf9d8608baa8867.

**N5: every C83 curvature and repair branch.** For each j with h_j>1,
put Rj=sum_(i>j)h_i,Hj=sum_(i<=j)h_i,
etaj=(h_j-1)/(1+Rj),V0j=(1+Pj)^2-C^2 etaj^2,C0j=etaj(1+etaj),
E(k)=1+kRj,p=p_(j+1)(k), and

 B_j(k)=sum_(i<=j)h_i[1+k(1-h_i)]p_i(k)^2,
 Pcut_j(k)=E(k)^2 B_j(k)-Hj[1+k(s+Rj+1)]p^2,
 Qcut_j(k)=Pcut_j(k)/k^2.

The last quotient is a polynomial: both its constant and first-order
numerator coefficients vanish. If Rj>0 put

 d_i(k)=[E(k)p_i(k)-p]/k,
 T_i(k)=2Rj*p*d_i(k)-(1-k)d_i(k)^2,
 Tplus(k)=max(0,T_0(k),...,T_j(k)).

Require, for EVERY0<k<=1,

 V0j*Tplus(k)+2Rj*C0j*Qcut_j(k)>=0.                     (2)

If Rj=0 require Qcut_j(k)>=0. The complete continuum is retained,
including zero credit, reciprocal roots, both middle-step orders and
the last cut. The equivalent C86 finite polynomial-root tests add no
other inequalities. The k=1 repaired-middle-cut tests are included.
Provenance32380fa0da39d710 and19f1d27c0eb8a588; the full-curvature
construction and continuum sign proof used here are reproduced in
Appendix B. No sampling of k substitutes for (2).

**N6: FULL2948, not just its weaker strict corollary.** If a>sqrt2-1 put

 u0=1+a, delta0=u0^2-2,
 nu=(1+eta)(1-a eta)/a,
 Erow=a+3+1/a-(u0 delta0/a)eta-C^2 eta^2,
 Wrow=max(1+b,1+bcd), Krow=max(u0,a Wrow).

Retain Erow>=0,Qunit>=0,nu>0 and

 u0 delta0<=Krow Erow/nu+2sqrt(Qunit Erow/nu).             (3)

When ab<=1 and cd<=1, retain also the strict equivalent consequences
Erow>nu delta0^2/u0^2 and
b<(a+3)(C-a-2)/[(a+1)(C+a+2)]. Conditions (3) imply these
on their stated domains. At a<=sqrt2-1 they are NOT imposed as
positive-gap claims. Provenance2948d4cd3660413f; its complete original
first-row proof is reproduced in Appendix C.

**N7: the earlier cut-slack and prefix conditions.** These are important;
omitting them falsely admits a saturated version of our word.
For every positive step define beta_j by

 beta_j=[(1+Pj)^2(1+Rj)^2-C^2(h_j-1)^2]/(Rj+h_j)^2
                                                      when h_j>=1,
 beta_j=[(1+Pj)^2-(1-h_j)^2(1+Pj+h_j)^2]/h_j^2
                                                      when0<h_j<=1.

Set beta_(-1)=beta_4=0,Drow_i=sqrt(beta_(i-1))+sqrt(beta_i).
Retain beta_j>=0 and

 0<=Qunit<=(sum_(i<4)|h_i p_i(1)|sqrt(beta_i))^2.          (4)

For a selected h_j>1, all steps positive, use LOCAL constants
A=1+Pj,B=1+Rj,dcut=h_j-1,acut=dcut/B,wcut=1+acut,
delta_cut=beta_j,mstep=min_i h_i,Delta_cut=AB-C*dcut,

 Fbd=wcut*delta_cut/(acut*mstep) if j<3, otherwise0,
 Bbd=wcut*delta_cut/mstep if j>0, otherwise0,
 Ubd=[2sqrt(delta_cut)sum_(i<j)Drow_i+Pj*Bbd]/B,
 eta_bd=Fbd+Bbd+Ubd,
 Ebd=2sqrt(delta_cut)sum_(k>j)Drow_k+2C(4-j)*eta_bd,
 Lbd=[2B/(B-dcut)]*(eta_bd+Ebd/dcut),
 Rbd=(A+dcut)*Delta_cut/(B-dcut),
 Kbd=2Pj+h_j+1/2.

Retain the ENTIRE published prefix consequence

 V_(h0,...,h_(j-1)) >= -(Rbd+Kbd Lbd)*Gmax^2             (5)

for every actual prefix trajectory, where Gmax=max_(i<=j)||g_i||.
The empty prefix has energy0. We shall prove (5) for ALL such prefix
trajectories by a uniform elementary bound, not by selecting one
favorable prefix. Thus we retain also every concrete prefix witness
inequality previously extracted from (5), including its3/2 example.
When beta_j=0 the earlier exact prefix-heredity condition is retained;
our word has every beta_j>0. Provenancef7cee3034df8cb7f and
e3b6ace910af2568, whose complete algebra is reproduced in Appendix D.

**N8: later original-row remainders, caps, mixtures and terminal Psi.**
These have not been silently forgotten. Appendix E contains their exact
formulas, all cap domains, and their full original-array derivations.
On the domain used here,

 a,b,c>0,bc<=1,sqrt2<=v=1+d<2,
 sqrt2-1<=a<1,ab<=1,c(v-1)<=1,a eta<1,

their ENTIRE conjunction is implied by N1,N5 and FULL N6. This is
the precise upper-first-step portion of the earlier joint comparison
1f8676462d9ee6b5; Section6 below supplies a shorter direct proof for
the PRESENT word, not an appeal to that unverified result. This includes eps>=0,
every price and lambda>=0 mixture, the previous capped/uncapped
remainders, and the full terminal Psi constraint. On other domains the
formula families in Appendix E remain conditional additional tests;
no global redundancy is claimed. Scoped earlier word exclusions
(both endpoints in[7/5,3/2], and the stated C83 prism) do not apply
to the word below; its first step is7/4 and its second excess is<1/3.
The excluded eps=0 locus is also avoided: (3) and the upper-terminal
comparison give a strictly positive calibrated eps here.

The complete value-optimized scalar channel S_scalar is a DIFFERENT
predicate, specified in Section8. No implication N_pub=>S_scalar or
its converse is assumed. No original multiplier, support ansatz,
rank condition, equality of steps, reversal or original feasibility
is hidden inside N_pub.

## 2. The exact threshold word and rational bounds

Define, exactly,

 C=193/25, lambda=1+sqrt2, epsilon=1/1000,
 t0=(C-lambda)/(C+lambda), N0=C*t0,
 a=3/4, b=N0-15/4+epsilon,
 c=lambda*t0-epsilon, d=sqrt2-1=1/lambda.                (6)

Then N=3+a+b=N0+epsilon and Dtail=lambda. Since
N0+lambda*t0=C-lambda, the sum identity is
C=5+a+b+c+d. Thus the ACTUAL physical word h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d)
has sum168/25. Epsilon is a rational symbolic perturbation, not a
tolerance or a numerically selected parameter.

The elementary bounds7/5<sqrt2<10/7 give12/5<lambda<17/7.
The function C(C-z)/(C+z) decreases in z>0. Therefore

 4<N0<25669/6325<65/16,
 4<N<407/100, 1/4<b<8/25<1/3.                         (7)

For the lower bound evaluate at z=17/7: its value is178718/44400>4.
For the last upper bounds use65/16+1/1000<407/100.
The function z(C-z)/(C+z) increases for12/5<=z<=17/7:
its derivative numerator C^2-2Cz-z^2 is positive there, for example
using C>15/2,z<5/2 gives C^2-2Cz-z^2>25/2>0.
Hence

 5/4<c<9/7.                                          (8)

At12/5 the unperturbed lower value is1596/1265; its excess over5/4
is59/5060>1/1000. At17/7 the upper comparison with9/7 is
8C<442/7, true for C=193/25. In particular all steps exceed1 and
lie in the published compact box. Also

 ab<6/25, bc<72/175<1/2, cd<27/49<1,
 a*b*N<(3/4)(8/25)(407/100)=1221/1250<1,
 b*N<814/625<4/3.                                    (9)

The exact third-cut difference is

 N*lambda-C*c=(C+lambda)*epsilon>0.                   (10)

Thus C*t<N, where t=c/lambda. Since d=1/lambda,

 C a b c d=a b C t<a b N<1.                          (11)

All four cut gaps are STRICTLY positive. For the first cut
C*(3/4)<C-7/4 is equivalent to C>7. For the second,
Cb<8/3<P*Htail since P=11/4 and Htail>9/2. The third is(10).
For the last, Cd<4 while C-1-d>6, using d<1/2,C>15/2.
In particular beta_j>0 for every j: no hidden saturated-cut or
zero-gradient boundary is being used.

## 3. Direct checks of cuts, returns, triangles, C82 and2948

Equations(10)--(11) give the unit quadratic and suffix pair:
Ccd=Ct<N, Qunit>0. The complete clipping list follows from
Cb/Htail<P and, using b<1/3,d<1/2,C<8,
Cbd<4/3<P(2+c). Since bc<1, its remaining nontrivial condition
is Cbcd=b Ct<bN<4/3<P. The bc>=1 implication is inapplicable.

Here eta=b/Htail<1/13 because Htail>9/2 and b<1/3.
For a=3/4,

 P=11/4,Rfirst=61/12,kfirst=119/48,
 Jfirst=61/12-(119/48)eta,
 Erow=Jfirst-C^2 eta^2>4.                             (12)

The last inequality follows already from119/624<1/5 and64/169<2/5,
which give61/12-119/624-64/169>269/60>4.
Thus the mixed second cut passes. The first adjacent triangle has
left side <(1/4)[1+(3/4)*9]=31/16<Htail.

The lifted middle triangle has left side
<(1/2)(61/12+11/3)=35/8, while its right side is

 lambda*N^2-c[N^2+c(N+C)]
       =S[lambda*S-c(S+2lambda)]
       =S(C+lambda)*epsilon>0.                       (13)

Thus it is strictly satisfied. At this d one has rtail=t^2:
substitution into Ktail, or expansion of
d(d+2)^2-Ktail=(c+d+2)[d(d+2)-1], proves the equality.

Because qpre>=qlate,

 Jpre>=N^2-kappa_pre(1+t),
 kappa_pre=(N-1)[b(N+1)-(N-1)]/b<0.                 (14)

The sign follows from N>4,b<1/3 and (N+1)/3<N-1.

 Jquad-N^2=(1+t)[1/(a^2b)-bN^2]/(1+b)>0             (15)

by abN<1. Both independent terminal lifts, with their full max and

The lifted return also has a large strict margin. Its left side is
<64*(1/3)^2*(1/2)^2=16/9<2. Its right side is
Rfirst(2+c)^2-kfirst*b(2+c)>5*3^2-3*(1/3)*4=41,
using Rfirst>5,kfirst<3,3<2+c<4.

 ell>[(5/4)(7/5)(19/10)]/6=133/240>1/2,
 K<(bN)^2<16/9.                                     (16)

Here Dtail-bc>12/5-1/2=19/10 and lambda^2<6.

E82(u,z)>=16/9-K>0 already proves the unpriced family.
For the STRONGER all-value family use its price at most H82(u)-z^2.
Its energy is therefore at least

 (1-a^2)u^2-2(1+a)a*u+1+2(1+a)
                         +[2(1+a)+1-b^2]z^2-K.

The quadratic in u has unrestricted minimum9/16 when a=3/4.
The coefficient of z^2 is9/2-b^2>4. Thus EVERY legal parameter,
including the value-price switching branch, has energy

 >=9/16+4ell-K>9/16+2-16/9=113/144>0.                (17)

No optimized triangle formula or numerical root is required for this
check. It includes C82, C84 and the later optimized-value improvement.

For FULL2948, u0=7/4,delta0=17/16 and
nu<(1+1/13)*4/3=56/39<3/2. Products(9) give Krow=u0.
By(12), Erow>4>nu*delta0. Therefore
u0 Erow/nu>u0 delta0, proving(3) even before its nonnegative square-root
term is added. Since0<delta0<u0^2, this also proves the strict corollary
on its actual domain. No weak replacement of FULL2948 was used.

## 4. EVERY curvature, including the true last-step quadratic

The proof in Appendix B establishes the following exact sign statement:
for a word with1<h0,h3<9/5,1<h1,h2<49/20,C<87/10,bc<2+d,
the auxiliary energy for either middle cut is nonnegative at EVERY
k in(0,1] if its value at k=1 is nonnegative. It explicitly covers all
mixed signs and both middle-step orders; it is not a monotonicity claim
across a reciprocal root. Its mixed-interval estimates and high-interval
decreasing normalized energy are included in full.

Our word is in that domain. At j=1 its k=1 auxiliary energy is
1-C^2 a^2 eta^2>1-(6/13)^2>0. At j=2 it is
1-C^2 a^2 b^2 t^2>1-(abN)^2>0. Thus every middle auxiliary energy
is positive, and every expression(2) is nonnegative because V0j>=0.
This proves the WHOLE C83/C86 middle families, not just k=1.
For j=0 the affine Qcut polynomial and the first cut imply positivity
on the entire interval, as proved in Appendix B.

It remains to check the Rj=0 last cut, equivalently the actual
unrepriced quadratic energy B_full(k). Here is a direct relation that
also prevents confusing a clipped auxiliary with a true quadratic.
Let v=h3=sqrt2, H=h0+h1+h2,s=H+v, and p=p_3(k). Let Vaux_2(k)
be the third-cut auxiliary energy from Appendix B. Direct expansion gives

 B_full(k)-Vaux_2(k)
 =k^3 v^2 p^2 (1-k)
      [2-v+2s(1+Ck)(1+k)]/(1+kv)^2 >=0.              (18)

For a check before using v^2=2, the difference divided by k*p^2 is

 v[1+k(1-v)]-s(1+Ck)(1-kv)^2
                  +H[1+k(H+2v+1)]/(1+kv)^2.

Use H[1+k(H+2v+1)]=s(1+Ck)-v[1+k(v+1)]. Multiplying by(1+kv)^2
then gives k^2 v^2 times
2-v+kv-kv^2+s(1+Ck)(2-k^2v^2). At v^2=2 this is exactly
(1-k)[2-v+2s(1+Ck)(1+k)], proving(18), including p=0 without division.
Since Vaux_2>=0, all actual quadratics pass. At k=0 the zero function
has energy0. This finishes every curvature in N1 and N5.

## 5. The OLD cut-slack conditions really pass, including EVERY prefix

For this word we have the useful strict lower bounds

 beta1>4, beta2>1/100, beta3>10.                       (19)

For beta1, with B=Htail>9/2 and b<1/3,
B/(B+b)>9/10 and Cb/(B+b)<1. Thus
beta1>(121/16)(81/100)-1=8201/1600>4.
For beta2 use(10): its numerator is
epsilon(C+lambda)(Nlambda+Cc)>epsilon*10*18.
Its denominator(lambda+c)^2<16, so beta2>9/800>1/100.
For beta3 the prefix capacity is A=C-sqrt2>6, d<1/2,C<8,
and (1+d)^2=2. Hence beta3>(36-16)/2=10.

The terminal summand alone proves the jump/quadratic upper bound(4).
Indeed p3(1)=-abc and h3^2=2, so its squared contribution is

 (h3*p3(1))^2 beta3=a^2b^2c^2[(C-sqrt2)^2-C^2d^2].

Subtracting Qunit gives a^2b^2c^2(C-sqrt2)^2-1>0, because
abc(C-sqrt2)>(3/4)(1/4)(5/4)*6=45/32>1.
Every other summand is nonnegative.

For any nonnegative prefix of sum Pj and any ACTUAL convex trajectory,
convexity and GD give |f_i-f_j|<=Pj*Gmax^2. Consequently

 V_prefix>=-(4Pj^2+Pj)Gmax^2.                         (20)

To verify the coefficient, the value sum costs at most2Pj^2, the
gradient-diagonal sum costs at most sum h_i^2<=Pj^2, and the terminal
term costs Pj(Pj+1). These bounds also hold at Gmax=0 without division.
We show that each retained Rbd+Kbd Lbd exceeds this universal budget.

At j=0 the prefix is empty. At j=1, sqrt(beta1)>2 and
Drow4=sqrt(beta3)>3, so Ebd>12. Because dcut=b<1/3 and
2B/(B-dcut)>2, Lbd>72. Thus Rbd+Kbd Lbd>72>14=4(7/4)^2+7/4.

At j=2, sum_(i<2)Drow_i>Drow1>2, sqrt(beta2)>1/10,
and B=lambda<5/2. Therefore Ubd>4/25, eta_bd>4/25,
Ebd>4C*eta_bd>112/25 and, since dcut=c<4/3,
Lbd>168/25. Also Pj=N-1>3, h_j>2, so Kbd>17/2.
Thus

 Rbd+Kbd Lbd>1428/25>91/2>4Pj^2+Pj,

where Pj<13/4 was used for the last comparison.

At j=3, Ebd>=2sqrt(beta3)*Drow4=2beta3>20.
Since dcut=d<1/2, Lbd>80. Here5<Pj=C-1-sqrt2<6, so Kbd>10,
and the resulting budget is>800>150>4Pj^2+Pj.

The cut inequalities ensure Rbd>=0 and B-dcut>0 in every use above.
Equations(19)--(20) therefore prove ALL of(5), not only the earlier
listed prefix example. All saturated-cut implications are inapplicable,

## 6. Remaining original-row families and the exact scope of survival

The word has a=3/4>sqrt2-1,ab<1,cd<1,bc<1,a eta<1 and
v=sqrt2. The formulas in Appendix E define
beta=1+b,mu=1+t,ell=a^2b(1-bt),
Rcut=ell*N^2+mu-C^2a^2b*beta*t^2 and Dprefix=b^2N^2-P^2.
By(15), Rcut=a^2b*beta*(Jquad-C^2t^2)>0. Also

 Dprefix<16/9-121/16=-833/144,
 u0^2*delta0/a^2=833/144.                              (28)

For EVERY nonnegative price parameter k, therefore,
eps_k=k*Rcut-Dprefix>u0^2*delta0/a^2. The old two-energy
ratio tests and every capped or uncapped sharp-root ratio test pass
already because their right side is nonnegative whereas Dprefix<0.
Their zero-remainder equality restrictions do not apply.

In the old three-vector calibration, Appendix E gives
0<=sigma<=u0^2*Lk, where Lk=k*mu/(1+a^2k*mu)<=1/a^2.
In the later original02-row calibration, its displayed completion of
the square gives the same upper bound sigma<=u0^2/a^2. Thus every
retained coefficient obeys0<=sigma<=u0^2/a^2, regardless of its cap
branch. Write Q=Qunit and Qplus=Q+u0^2*delta0; both are positive.

For any A0>0 the EXACT unrestricted price supremum is

 sup_(r<A0/u0)[r*u0*delta0-r^2*Q/(A0-r*u0)]
       =(A0/u0^2)(sqrt(Qplus)-sqrt(Q))^2.              (29)

To prove it put y=A0-r*u0>0. The expression is
A0*delta0+2A0*Q/u0^2-Qplus*y/u0^2-Q*A0^2/(u0^2*y).
The square of sqrt(Qplus*y)-A0*sqrt(Q/y) proves(29), with equality
at y=A0*sqrt(Q/Qplus). Restricting r by any original coefficient cap
cannot increase the supremum. Also
(sqrt(Qplus)-sqrt(Q))^2<=Qplus-Q=u0^2*delta0.

Consequently(28) pays EVERY unmixed calibration, and Erow>nu*delta0
from Section3 pays every lambda_mix>=0 mixture:

 eps_k+lambda_mix*Erow
   >(u0^2/a^2+lambda_mix*nu)*delta0
   >=((sigma+lambda_mix*nu)/u0^2)
                         *(sqrt(Qplus)-sqrt(Q))^2.

This directly proves the complete price/cap families for this word.
For the full terminal Psi test, the constants in Appendix E satisfy
k>k0>0 and Gcost>=1/(k-k0), with all other terms nonnegative.
Since Dprefix<0, eps=k*Rcut-Dprefix>k*Rcut, whence

 Rcut<eps/k<eps/(k-k0)<=Psi(sqrt(eps)).                (30)

The old terminal cut ensures that every radical in Psi is defined.
This uses the actual published Psi and its actual same-array budgets;
no covariance or original multiplier is independently selected.

Hence (6) satisfies N_pub AS DEFINED IN SECTION1, including every
retained domain, every curvature and every old quantitative prefix test.
This is a nonemptiness statement at the requested ACTUAL threshold.
It is not a theorem that the full primitive certificate cone is nonempty.

## 7. An explicit static function excludes the SAME threshold word

We now prove that the survivor has no full array. This new test has a
concrete independence witness against N_pub: the SAME exact word(6).
It is not another condition whose intersection with the old tests is
unknown. No comparison with S_scalar is inferred.

In fact the following construction works for any a,b,c,d>0. Put
v=1+d,t=c/(1+v),q=a/(2+b),N=3+a+b and C=5+a+b+c+d.
Assume tq<1 and define

 rho=(1+t)/(q(1-tq))>0.

Use the two scalar arrays, whose entries run from0 through4,

 Xbase=(N,2+b,1,-c,-t), Gbase=(1,1,1,-t,-t),
 Xaux=(1,-a,-q,cq,tq), Gaux=(1,-q,-q,tq,tq).           (21)

Both follow all four ORIGINAL GD steps. For Xaux the first two updates
use a=(2+b)q; the last two use c=(1+v)t. Assign values in each array
by f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2. Let

 X_i=(Xbase_i,sqrt(rho)*Xaux_i),
 G_i=(Gbase_i,sqrt(rho)*Gaux_i),
 F_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2.                           (22)

For saturated values Q_ij=(g_i-g_j)(x_j-g_j).
The base array is the legal scalar third-cut function, so all its Q<=0.
For the auxiliary array the only nonzero target normals are

 y1=-(1+b)q, y3=v*t*q.

At target1 all source comparisons are nonpositive:
sources0,3,4 have gradients at least -q, and source2 equals -q.
At target3 the comparisons from sources1,2 are negative, from4 zero,
and the ONLY positive ordered residual is

 Qaux_03=(1-tq)*v*t*q.

Its base counterpart is Qbase_03=-(1+t)*v*t. Our rho makes
Qbase_03+rho*Qaux_03=0. All other combined finite rows remain
nonpositive. For the optimum sample0, every incoming residual
Q_(i,star)=0; the outgoing auxiliary residuals are
-(1+b)q^2 at1 and -v*t^2*q^2 at3, and zero elsewhere. The base
optimum rows are also nonpositive. This checks every original directed
finite and optimum interpolation pair.

For an explicit ONE STATIC function put

 Kphys=conv{G0,G1,G3},
 F(X)=max_(G in Kphys)(<X,G>-||G||^2/2).               (23)

Here G2=G1 and G4=G3=-t*G1, so0 is in Kphys. The preceding comparisons
are precisely its projection normal inequalities at every X_i.
Thus its maximizing gradient at X_i is G_i and its value is F_i.
Completing the square identifies the optimizer with projection onto
Kphys. Adding two projection normal inequalities proves that this
projection is1-Lipschitz; the maximizing inequalities give
0<=F(X+Z)-F(X)-<grad F(X),Z><=||Z||^2. Consequently F is convex,
everywhere differentiable and1-smooth. Also F>=0=F(0), so it has
an actual minimizer. No claim of a unique minimizer or a normalized
W payoff is needed for the primitive-energy obstruction.

Saturated values telescope to V_h=||X0||^2-||X4+sG4||^2.
Here X4=G4, hence the exact value is

 V_h=N^2-C^2t^2+rho*(1-C^2t^2q^2).                  (24)

For the word(6), tq<1 since t<15/28 and q<3/8.
Moreover Ct=N0-(C/lambda)epsilon>4-1/300>39/10,
while q>9/28. Thus Ctq>351/280>5/4 and

 1-C^2t^2q^2<-9/16, rho>1/q>8/3.

By(10),

 0<N^2-C^2t^2=epsilon(1+C/lambda)(N+Ct)
                         <13/300<1/20.

Combining these exact rational bounds yields

                         V_h < -29/20 <0.            (25)

Accepted04219554e41d9750 now rules out all20-sign/fullR5 arrays for
this word. This is an actual function, not an infeasible formal Gram,
an independently selected covariance, or a numerical dual certificate.

The new necessary inequality supplied by(21)--(24), when tq<1, is

 q(1-tq)(N^2-C^2t^2)+(1+t)(1-C^2t^2q^2)>=0,          (26)

equivalently

 C^2t^2 <= [N^2(1-tq)+(1+t)/q]/(1+q).

If tq>=1 the auxiliary array is itself legal, and its energy
1-C^2t^2q^2 is negative; hence that domain is excluded directly.
No singular division is used at tq=1. The full old-system survivor
(6) violates(26), proving an ACTUAL threshold separation.

At epsilon=0 the old exact prefix-heredity condition already excludes
the analogous word: its two-step prefix violates N0*(3/4)<=2+b.
We deliberately do not use that rejected boundary as the survivor.
Section5 checks the strictly perturbed word against the complete OLD
quantitative version before(26) is introduced.

## 8. The distinct complete scalar channel and static attainment

For ANY h>=0 and theta in[0,1] define q_i=product_(r<i)(1-theta h_r),
scalar positions x_i=q_i, gradients g_i=theta q_i, and base values
f_i^0=theta q_i^2/2. Put Utheta=theta(1-theta)/2. Their base slacks are

 -Q_ij(f^0)=Utheta(q_i-q_j)^2.

Let d_i be the minimum SUM OF SQUARED GAP LENGTHS on a path from
q_4 to q_i through the five sampled coordinates. Equivalently, sort
the distinct coordinates and sum the squared adjacent gaps between
the two endpoints. For any edge spanning several gaps its squared
length is at least the sum of their squares; every path crosses each
intervening gap. The sorted monotone path attains this lower bound.
Ties add zero gaps and cause no exception.

Writing f_i=f_i^0+z_i, every original finite Q row is exactly
z_i-z_j<=Utheta(q_i-q_j)^2. Following paths from4 gives
z_i-z_4>=-Utheta*d_i. These simultaneous lower bounds are attained
by z_i=-Utheta*d_i, since d_j<=d_i+(q_i-q_j)^2. As every h_i>=0,
the exact scalar value minimum is therefore

 J_theta=B_full(theta)-theta(1-theta)sum_(i<4)h_i*d_i.  (27)

This proves the channel/witness identity, including theta=0,1 and
coincident samples. The COMPLETE S_scalar predicate is J_theta>=0
for EVERY theta in[0,1]. Merely passing B_full and C83 is a different
statement. The finite scalar path calculation also gives an attaining
terminal-source flow, but makes no PSD assertion about that flow.

For completeness, these optimized finite values are always attained
by one real static smooth convex function with a nonempty minimizer
set, without retaining the base origin as a prescribed minimizer.
Set f_i=f_i^0-Utheta*d_i, cjet_i=g_i x_i-f_i and

 H_i(w)=cjet_i+x_i(w-g_i)+(w-g_i)^2/2,
 H(w)=max_i H_i(w), F(x)=sup_w[xw-H(w)].

The function H is finite and1-strongly convex with quadratic growth;
the supremum has a unique maximizing w(x). Strong subgradient
monotonicity proves |w(x)-w(y)|<=|x-y|, and the two maximizing
inequalities prove differentiability with F'=w. Also
H_j(g_i)-cjet_i=Q_ij<=0, with equality at j=i. Hence
H(g_i)=cjet_i and x_i is its subgradient there, proving interpolation
of all five prescribed positions, gradients and values.

Writing A_i=H_i(0), the minimizer set is

 argmin F=conv{(1-theta)q_i:A_i=max_j A_j}.

Indeed these are precisely the subgradients of the finite maximum H
at0; the conjugate gradient equals0 exactly at such subgradients.
Thus a minimizer exists. No original optimum at0 is silently assumed,
and V_h is invariant under common function-value shifts or translations.
This establishes the exact minimizer scope of(27). For a normalized
objective payoff the true distance to this hull would still have to be
paid; no such payoff is asserted here.

The word(6) has been proved to satisfy N_pub and to fail the NEW
two-dimensional witness(26). Whether it satisfies the additional
S_scalar channel(27) remains a sharply delimited question for the
remaining authorized analytical round. A scalar violation would be
an additional legal obstruction, not a defect in its proved N_pub
survival. If the scalar channel also passes, then(26) separates even
N_pub AND S_scalar. Neither stronger conclusion is claimed now.

## 9. Accounting and remaining task

The full primitive seed and the original exact-profile Goal remain OPEN.
No h,Lambda,M certificate was constructed or submitted. The exact
threshold word(6) cannot be completed, by(25). The NEW expanded
necessary conjunction N_pub plus(26) has not been proved empty or
nonempty globally. Its original-array completion problem remains
